- Rights: public-domain (1897 words); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « une oeuvre bien importante ! »
- Translation: "a very important work!"
- Other sources: Langlois warns that sayings giving Mère Agnès authority over the writings are among those most open to later reworking (see card on reliability).
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium (a "strategic" saying in Langlois's sense).

### F-OW-267 — "You dither far too much, my little Mother"
- Claim: Mère Agnès said she wanted to have Thérèse's heart preserved, like Mère Geneviève's; Thérèse said "Do as you like!" When Agnès changed her mind because the idea repelled her, Thérèse looked rather sad and said: "You dither far too much, my little Mother; I've noticed it many times in my life..."
- When / where: 2 Aug 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 2.8.1, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Vous tergiversez bien trop, ma petite mère, je l'ai remarqué bien des fois dans ma vie... »
- Translation: "You dither far too much, my little mother; I've noticed it many times in my life."
- New to English readers: yes (not found in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Frank criticism of Pauline, recorded by Pauline herself.

### F-OW-268 — "I have no great desire for Heaven"; kissing the crucifix
- Claim: "I have no great desires for Heaven; I'll be glad to go, that's all!" "No one will be able to say of me: 'She is dying of not dying.'" To Céline, who kissed the feet of the crucifix: "You're not following 'baby's' doctrine! Kiss him quickly on both cheeks and make him kiss you." She said she felt "very lively joy" not only when found imperfect but at feeling it herself: "that is better than all the compliments, which bore me".
- When / where: 2 Aug 1897
- People: Sr Geneviève; Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 2.8.3–6, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Baisez-le bien vite sur les deux joues et faites-vous embrasser. »
- Translation: "Kiss him quick on both cheeks, and let him kiss you."
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-269 — "I will die of death"; the bruised shoulder
- Claim: No one understood the course of her illness; a sister asked, "What will you die of, then?" "Why, I'll die of death! Didn't God tell Adam what he would die of: 'You shall die the death'? That's all." Her shoulder was bruised; offered cotton wool: "No, you mustn't take my little cross away." "I have suffered a long time, but little sufferings. Since 28 July they have been great sufferings." To her three sisters: after a parlour visit, do not stop and chat among yourselves, "for then it's like being at home".
- When / where: 3 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Sr Geneviève
- Source: CJ 3.8.6–9, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Mais, je mourrai de mort ! »
- Translation: "Why, I'll die of death!"
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-270 — "If I had been a priest I would have learned Hebrew and Greek"
- Claim: "It's only in Heaven that we'll see the truth about everything... Even for Holy Scripture, isn't it sad to see all the differences of translation! If I had been a priest I would have learned Hebrew and Greek; I would not have been satisfied with Latin; that way I would have known the real text dictated by the Holy Spirit." She dreamed she was sent to a war and said she would have loved to go on crusade: "I wouldn't have been afraid of catching a bullet!" "And I who wanted martyrdom, is it possible I will die in a bed!"
- When / where: 4 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 4.8.5–7, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Si j'avais été prêtre, j'aurais appris l'hébreu et le grec »
- Translation: "If I had been a priest, I would have learned Hebrew and Greek."
- Other sources: Varia (Céline's list, n. 11): "When I think that I'm dying in a bed! I would have liked to die in an arena."
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-271 — "My little life is to suffer, and that's it"
- Claim: Asked how she arranged her "little life" now: "My little life is to suffer, and that's it! I couldn't say: My God, this is for the Church, my God, this is for France... It would tire me too much to tell him: Give this to Peter, give this to Paul." For her missionary brothers she says simply: "My God, give them all that I want for myself." "No, I don't think I'm a great saint! I think I'm a very little saint."
- When / where: 4 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 4.8.2, 4.8.8, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Ma petite vie, c'est de souffrir et puis ça y est ! »
- Translation: "My little life is to suffer, and that's that!"
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-272 — The sparrow's egg: a "conjuring trick" for the saints
- Claim: Céline said it was impossible for a sparrow's egg (herself) to hatch a bird as lovely as Thérèse. Thérèse: "Yes, but! I'll do a conjuring trick to amuse all the saints." She would whisper to God, "Change the nature of the little bird by blowing on it", have the Virgin kiss it and Joseph caress it, make all the saints say they love it as much as her, then "with a triumphant air I'll break the little egg and a pretty little bird will come and sit beside me on God's knees". Céline notes her "oui, mais !" ("yes, but!") was a phrase "peculiar to her".
- When / where: 4 Aug 1897
- People: Sr Geneviève (recorder)
- Source: Dernières paroles à Céline, 4 Août n. 4; 3 Septembre n. 2, same URL
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Sr Geneviève)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « je ferai un tour de physique pour amuser tous les saints »
- Translation: "I'll do a magic trick to entertain all the saints."
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium.

### F-OW-273 — "A thread on my leg" and "the little grey mouse"
- Claim: Unable to move: "David said in the psalms: 'I am like the grasshopper that keeps changing place.' Well, I can't say as much! I'd like to go for a walk, but I have a thread on my leg!" "When the saints have shut the door of Heaven on me, they will sing: 'At last we've got you, little grey mouse, at last we've got you and we'll keep you!'" (a little song that came back to her). When Marie told her she would see resplendent angels at her death: "All these images do me no good; I can feed only on truth. That's why I never wanted visions."
- When / where: 5 Aug 1897
- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 5.8.2–4, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Enfin nous te tenons, / Petite souris grise »
- Translation: "At last we've got you, little grey mouse"
- New to English readers: yes (grey mouse and "fil à la patte" not found in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-274 — The Holy Face and how she wrote "Vivre d'amour"
- Claim: For the Transfiguration (6 Aug) the sisters hung the choir's picture of the Holy Face by her bed. She said: "How well Our Lord did to lower his eyes to give us his portrait! Since the eyes are the mirror of the soul, if we had seen his soul we would have died of joy." "While I was composing my hymn 'Living by Love' it helped me write it very easily. I wrote from memory, during my evening silence, the 15 stanzas I had composed without a draft during the day." After composing the stanza "Living by love is to wipe your Face", she said it to the picture in passing "and wept with love". Isaiah 53 ("without beauty") "was the whole basis of my devotion to the Holy Face, or rather of all my piety".
- When / where: 5 Aug 1897 (recalling 26 Feb 1895)
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 5.8.7 and 5.8.9, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « J'ai écrit de mémoire, pendant mon silence du soir les 15 couplets que j'avais composés, sans brouillon, dans la journée. »
- Translation: "I wrote down from memory, during my evening silence, the 15 verses I had composed in my head during the day, without a draft."
- New to English readers: yes (how PN 17 was written)
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Detail on her working method as a poet.

### F-OW-275 — Waiting for death all night; "like a little Robinson on his island"
- Claim: She hoped to die in the night of 5–6 Aug: "I kept watch all night, like the little girl in the song of the little Christmas shoe... I didn't stop looking at the Holy Face... I pushed away many temptations... Ah, I made many acts of faith!" After Dr de Cornière found her as ill but doubted death was near: "I'm like a poor little Robinson on his island... they announce a ship... I stay on the shore, I look far off... and, seeing nothing on the horizon, I tell myself: They've tricked me! I'm not going to leave!"
- When / where: 6 Aug 1897
- People: Dr Alexandre-Damase de Cornière (Carmel's doctor); Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 6.8.1–2, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Ils m'ont trompée ! Je ne vais pas m'en aller ! »
- Translation: "They've tricked me! I'm not going to leave!"
- New to English readers: yes (Robinson image not found in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-276 — Pride and "martyrdom" in singing the Office
- Claim: "How proud I was when I was hebdomadary at the Office and said the prayers aloud in the middle of the choir! Because I thought the priest said the same prayers at Mass and that, like him, I had the right to pray aloud before the Blessed Sacrament, to give blessings and absolutions, to read the Gospel when I was first chantress." "The Office was both my happiness and my martyrdom", because she wanted to say it without mistakes and sometimes, from distraction, let her part pass "without opening my mouth". "I excuse sisters who forget or make mistakes very much."
- When / where: 6 Aug 1897 (recalling 1890s)
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 6.8.6, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Que j'étais fière quand j'étais semainière à l'Office »
- Translation: "How proud I was when it was my week to lead the Office!"
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Links to her wish to be a priest (Ms B 2v).

### F-OW-277 — "Staying a little child": what it means (her own definition)
- Claim: Asked at Matins what she meant by "remaining a little child before God": "It is to recognise one's nothingness, to expect everything from God as a little child expects everything from its father; not to worry about anything, not to earn a fortune... As soon as the child grows up his father... says: Work now, you can support yourself. It's so as not to hear that that I did not want to grow up, feeling incapable of earning my living, the eternal life of Heaven." It also means "not to be discouraged by one's faults, for children fall often, but they are too small to hurt themselves much."
- When / where: 6 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 6.8.8, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « C'est pour ne pas entendre cela que je n'ai pas voulu grandir »
- Translation: "It's so as not to hear that, that I didn't want to grow up."
- New to English readers: partial (a version is in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-278 — An open window and a sharp question from the prioress
- Claim: Sr Saint-Stanislas, her first infirmarian, left her during Vespers with the door and window open; the draught was strong. The prioress was displeased and asked for explanations. Thérèse told Mère Agnès she told the truth, but chose "a more charitable expression" than the one she was about to use.
- When / where: 6 Aug 1897, infirmary
- People: Sr Saint-Stanislas (first infirmarian); Mère Marie de Gonzague
- Source: CJ 6.8.7, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « il m'est venu à la pensée une expression plus charitable que celle dont j'allais me servir »
- Translation: "a kinder way of putting it came to mind than the one I was going to use"
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Nursing care in the infirmary was sometimes careless; compare other parts (testimony).

### F-OW-279 — The flower from her father, glued to a picture
- Claim: She showed Mère Agnès the photograph of Notre-Dame des Victoires on which she had glued the little white flower her father gave her the day she told him her vocation (29 May 1887); the root had come off, "and the Child Jesus seems to hold it in his hand and smile at it". "That the little flower has lost its root will tell you I'm in Heaven."
- When / where: 7 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Louis Martin (recalled)
- Source: CJ 7.8.3, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Que la petite fleur ait perdu sa racine, ça vous dira que je suis au Ciel... »
- Translation: "The little flower losing its root will tell you I'm in Heaven."
- Other sources: Ms A 50v says she put the flower in her Imitation; by 1897 it was on the picture.
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-280 — Looking at the sky: "the other is more and more closed to me"
- Claim: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur said, "How lovingly you look at the sky!" Thérèse only smiled; later she told Mère Agnès: "Ah, she thinks I'm looking at the firmament thinking of the real Heaven! But no, it's simply because I admire the material sky; the other is more and more closed to me." Then she told herself it was by love that she looked, "since everything I do... since my offering, is by love".
- When / where: 8 Aug 1897
- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 8.8.2, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « l'autre m'est de plus en plus fermé »
- Translation: "the other one is more and more closed to me"
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Trial of faith in her last weeks.

### F-OW-281 — Pity for servants at grand dinners
- Claim: "If Our Lord and the Blessed Virgin had not gone to feasts themselves, I would never have understood the custom of inviting friends to meals." "I felt great pity for the people who served at big dinners. If by bad luck they let a few drops fall on the tablecloth or on a guest, I saw the lady of the house look at them severely; then those poor people blushed with shame, and I said to myself, inwardly quite revolted: Oh! how this difference between masters and servants here below proves there is a heaven where each will be placed according to inner merit."
- When / where: 8 Aug 1897 (recalling her youth)
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 8.8.4, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « je me disais, toute révoltée intérieurement »
- Translation: "and I said to myself, inwardly up in arms"
- New to English readers: yes (not found in Novissima Verba; check)
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: A social observation from a bourgeois girl; useful "world around her" detail.

### F-OW-282 — "I will die with weapons in hand"; "something for every taste"
- Claim: Mère Agnès said, "Our warrior is struck down!" Thérèse: "I'm not a warrior who fought with earthly weapons but with 'the sword of the spirit which is the word of God'. So illness could not strike me down, and only last night I used my sword with a novice. I've said it: I will die with weapons in hand." About her manuscript: "There will be something for every taste, except for extraordinary ways." Told she was a saint: "No, I'm not a saint; I've never done the actions of saints. I'm a very little soul whom God has filled with graces."
- When / where: 9 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); an unnamed novice
- Source: CJ 9.8.1–3, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Il y en aura pour tous les goûts, excepté pour les voies extraordinaires. »
- Translation: "There will be something for every taste, except for extraordinary ways."
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-283 — Théophane Vénard's picture pinned to her bed curtain
- Claim: A picture of the martyr Théophane Vénard (MEP, beheaded in Tonkin 1861), showing him pointing to Heaven, was pinned to her bed curtains. "Do you think he knows you?" "Look what he's showing me... He might well not have taken that pose..." "Each time I look at him, he looks at me too, and seems to watch me out of the corner of his eye with a half-mischievous air." She caressed the picture on both cheeks because she could not reach to kiss it; on 17 Aug she put a periwinkle on it (Mère Agnès kept it); she said she could no longer look at it without crying; "Watch out for little Théophane!" when Céline fixed the pillows. On 6 Sep she wept with joy at receiving a relic of Vénard.
- When / where: Aug–Sep 1897, infirmary
- People: Théophane Vénard (d. 1861); Sr Geneviève; Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 10.8.1, 10.8.3, 17.8.3, 17.8.5, 19.8.5, 19.8.7, 6.9.2, cj-aout-1897 and cj-septembre-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « il semble me guetter du coin de l'oeil d'un air à moitié malin »
- Translation: "he seems to be watching me out of the corner of his eye, half teasing"
- New to English readers: partial (Vénard is named in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-284 — "The Blessed Virgin is less happy than we are"
- Claim: "The Blessed Virgin has no Blessed Virgin to love; she is less happy than we are." (She had said this at recreation earlier.) "I often pray to the saints without being heard, but the deafer they seem to my prayers, the more I love them... Because I wanted more not to see God and the saints, and to stay in the night of faith, than others want to see and understand." "I never 'pretend'; I'm not like the wife of Jeroboam."
- When / where: 11 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 11.8.4–6, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « La Sainte Vierge, elle n'a pas de Sainte Vierge à aimer, elle est moins heureuse que nous. »

- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-285 — Cravings: chicken, cutlets, Sunday sorrel rice, tuna
- Claim: "It's incredible: now that I can't eat any more, I get cravings for all sorts of good things, like chicken, cutlets, the Sunday rice with sorrel, tuna!" On 31 Aug: "I have an appetite for my whole life. I always ate like a martyr, and now I'd devour everything. I feel I'm dying of hunger." She told Mère Agnès that once, to mortify herself, she thought of dirty things while eating, "but afterwards I found it simpler to offer God what I found to my taste". Céline recorded: "Food was something I couldn't think of, it turned my stomach, and now I have an animal's cravings, I'd devour anything given me, and that humiliates me a lot." In her youth, joy took away her appetite, while on days of great sorrow "I ate like four".
- When / where: 12 and 31 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Sr Geneviève (recorder of Varia)
- Source: CJ 12.8.5; 31.8.5; 31.8.12, cj-aout-1897; Dernières paroles à Céline, Varia 3 nn. 1, 18, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/oeuvres-de-therese/dernieres-paroles-de-therese/dernieres-paroles-a-celine/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès; Sr Geneviève)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « comme du poulet, des côtelettes, du riz à l'oseille du Dimanche, du thon !... »
- Translation: "like chicken, cutlets, Sunday sorrel rice, tuna!"
- New to English readers: yes (not found in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Food and body detail; the TB hunger of her last weeks.

### F-OW-286 — Why she resisted being spared community duties
- Claim: Her sisters recalled how she had resisted their pleas to rest, not rise with the community, skip Matins. "You didn't understand me when I insisted, but it was because I felt people were trying to influence Our Mother. I wanted to tell Our Mother the whole truth, so that she would decide by herself."
- When / where: 12 Aug 1897 (recalling 1896–1897)
- People: her three sisters; Mère Marie de Gonzague
- Source: CJ 12.8.4, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « je sentais bien qu'on essayait d'influencer Notre Mère »
- Translation: "I could tell people were trying to sway Our Mother."
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Relevant to the later charge that the prioress neglected her illness: Thérèse says she chose to leave the decision to the prioress.

### F-OW-287 — "He wants to make me believe there is no Heaven"
- Claim: "I wonder how God can hold back so long from taking me... And then, you'd think he wants to make me 'believe' there's no Heaven!... And all the saints I love so much, where are they 'nesting'?... Ah! I'm not pretending, it's really true that I can't see a thing. But anyway, I must sing very loud in my heart: 'After death, life is immortal', or else it would go badly..." Asked if she was anxious about what would become of her: "Oh no..." On the new bells ringing for Vespers: "Not very beautiful!"
- When / where: 15 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 15.8.2, 15.8.5, 15.8.7, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- Translation: "it's as if he wants to make me 'believe' there's no Heaven!"
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-288 — Sr Marie de la Trinité: the red balloon and the spinning top
- Claim: Sr Marie de la Trinité (novice) recorded: she was forbidden to speak to Thérèse "on the pretext that, being young, I might catch her illness" (Thérèse had told her no one would catch it, she had asked God). Once, crying in the Holy Face hermitage, she found Thérèse, feverish, had left her invalid carriage to sit by her on a tree trunk: "I'm not forbidden to come to you; even if I die of it, I want to comfort you" and "not before you've laughed for me!" About a month before her death, seeing a big red balloon at the foot of the bed, the novice wanted to play; Thérèse told her to play behind her while she shut her eyes, and peeked and laughed; then "ordered" her to take her Christmas top and play for an hour in the novitiate attic: "if anyone notices, say I told you to." She told her "Life is not sad... it is very cheerful... if you said 'exile is sad' I'd understand." She made her go and claim the forgotten dessert as a penance. A week before death she made her promise to cry "in the shell" (a novice rule).
- When / where: Jun–Sep 1897
- People: Sr Marie de la Trinité (Marie-Louise Castel, novice; recorder)
- Source: Dernières paroles à d'autres soeurs, "Marie de la Trinité" nn. 1–5, same URL
- Source type: eyewitness record (Sr Marie de la Trinité)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Mettez-vous derrière moi pendant qu'il n'y a personne, et puis jouez avec, je vais fermer les yeux »
- Translation: "Get behind me while nobody's here and play with it; I'll shut my eyes."
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium.
- Notes: The "coquille" (shell) was a small shell used by novices to catch tears, per other sources (check).

### F-OW-289 — "The devil is around me" (16 Aug); the right side and the left side
- Claim: Céline found her in the morning pale and distorted with pain: "The devil is around me, I don't see him but I feel him... he torments me, he holds me as with a hand of iron to stop me taking the least relief, he increases my pains so that I despair... And I can't pray! I can only look at the Blessed Virgin and say: Jesus!" Until then she suffered mostly on the right side; God asked if she would suffer for Céline; she said yes, and "at that instant the left side was seized with an unbelievable intensity. I'm suffering for you and the devil doesn't want it!" Céline lit a blessed candle and calm returned, though not the new pain. Afterwards she called her right side "Thérèse's side" and her left "Céline's side".
- When / where: 16 Aug 1897, infirmary
- People: Sr Geneviève (recorder)
- Source: Dernières paroles à Céline, 16 août, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/oeuvres-de-therese/dernieres-paroles-de-therese/dernieres-paroles-a-celine/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Sr Geneviève)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Je souffre pour vous et le démon ne veut pas ! »
- Translation: "I'm suffering for you, and the devil doesn't want it!"
- Other sources: The 1911 edited text (HA ch. XII) prints a version as told to "l'infirmière" (chk: "le démon est autour de moi"). See M-OW list.
- New to English readers: partial (in Taylor via HA)
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-290 — "The remedies hurt me"; a little linen
- Claim: "I feel God wants me to suffer. The remedies that should do me good and that relieve other patients hurt me." When they hesitated to give her a small cloth she asked for: "People ought to believe me when I ask for something, because I'm a very good 'little girl'" (meaning she asks only for what is essential). "I'm a very sick 'little girl', yes, very sick!"
- When / where: 17 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 17.8.1–2, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)

- Original words: « Les remèdes qui devraient me faire du bien et qui soulagent les autres malades, me font du mal à moi. »

- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-291 — She knew she was being "interviewed"
- Claim: When Mère Agnès took up her pencil to note a remark, Thérèse asked: "My merit is perhaps going to be lost, since I told you and you're writing it down?" — "So you want to gain merits?" — "Yes, but not for me; for poor sinners, for the needs of the whole Church, to throw flowers to everyone, just and sinners." Told she was very patient: "I haven't yet had one minute of patience. It's not my patience!... People always get it wrong!" "If I saw a big spider on our bed" she wonders what she would do.
- When / where: 18 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 18.8.3–4, 18.8.7, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Mon mérite va être perdu peut-être, puisque je vous l'ai dit et que vous l'écrivez ? »
- Translation: "Maybe my merit will be lost, since I've told you and you're writing it down?"

### F-OW-292 — "I'm afraid I'll lose my mind"; the dead Christ
- Claim: She nearly fainted before Communion when the Miserere was recited, even softly. "I may lose my ideas. Oh, if people knew what this weakness is!... Last night I couldn't go on; I asked the Blessed Virgin to take my head in her hands so I could bear it." Looking at a crucifix with bowed head: "He is dead! I prefer that he be shown dead, because I think he doesn't suffer any more." To Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie: "You mustn't sit crooked on chairs like that; it's written." "Everything has been said, hasn't it?" "Yes!" "I suffer only one instant. It's because we think of the past and the future that we lose heart and despair."
- When / where: 19 Aug 1897
- People: Sr Geneviève; Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie; Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 19.8.1, 19.8.3, 19.8.6, 19.8.8, 19.8.10, cj-aout-1897

- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- Translation: "He's dead! I prefer him shown dead, because then I think he's not suffering any more."
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Last Communion was 19 Aug 1897 (per CJ 20.8.10 she felt she could not receive again).

### F-OW-293 — Last Communion; a day of anguish behind the fly screen
- Claim: On 20 Aug, feeling she could not receive Communion any more, and hearing many remarks about it, she had "a day of anguish and temptations" that Mère Agnès "guessed were terrible". She asked for silence and not to be looked at: "I'd cry too much if I told you my troubles now, and I'm so short of breath I'd certainly suffocate." After at least an hour she spoke, holding in front of her eyes the screen she had been given against flies.
- When / where: 20 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 20.8.10, cj-aout-1897

- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- New to English readers: partial

### F-OW-294 — Taste of rot, milk refused, "Agne! Agne!", "a cooking recipe"
- Claim: To Céline: "You know you're nursing a 'baby' who's dying... Put something good in the big glass, because 'baby' has a taste of rot in her mouth." She asked to be kissed little because breath tired her: "hands don't breathe". She loathed milk; Mère Agnès asked, "Would you drink this cup to save my life?" "Oh yes!... Well then, and I wouldn't take it for the love of God?" and drank it in one go. To relieve breathlessness she gave a small regular cry: "Oh là là!" or "Agne! Agne!"; "that's not nice, it displeases me; now I'll say: Anne! Anne!" — "We'll put that in your obituary circular." — "It would look like a cooking recipe!" She coughed at her glass of Botot water: "It's not for drinking, I tell you!"
- When / where: 20 Aug 1897
- People: Sr Geneviève; Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 20.8.1–2, 20.8.5–6, 20.8.12, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- Translation: "It would read like a cooking recipe!"
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Humour at the edge of death; physical symptoms.

### F-OW-295 — She would have liked to be infirmarian to the difficult Mère Cœur de Jésus
- Claim: Hearing how the elderly Mère Cœur de Jésus troubled the infirmarians: "Oh, how I would have liked to be infirmarian, not by nature but 'by an attraction of grace'. And it seems to me I would have made Mère Cœur de Jésus happy!" (again 3 Sep: "Ah, how drawn I would have been to all that!")

### F-OW-296 — The Holy Family "very ordinary": Joseph planing, unpaid
- Claim: "It will be nice to know in Heaven all that happened in the Holy Family!" She imagined little Jesus wanting to fast like his mother; Joseph could not fast because of his work: "I see him planing, then wiping his forehead from time to time. Oh, how sorry I feel for him!" Village women asked Mary to lend them little Jesus to play with their children. "What does me good when I think of the Holy Family is to imagine a quite ordinary life. Not all they tell us... that the Child Jesus, after making clay birds, blew on them and gave them life. Ah no, little Jesus did not do useless miracles like that." "How many times did people refuse to pay him for his work!"
- When / where: 20 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 20.8.14, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Je le vois raboter, puis s'essuyer le front de temps en temps. »
- Translation: "I see him planing wood, then wiping his forehead now and then."
- New to English readers: partial (Novissima Verba has "good Saint Joseph")
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: Her rejection of apocryphal miracles (Infancy Gospel of Thomas) is a sign of her taste for realism.

### F-OW-297 — "Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur made me do many mortifications"
- Claim: "No one suspects that Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur, as provisor [in charge of food], made me do many mortifications. She loves me so much that I seemed very spoiled; but the mortification is greater that way... She looked after me according to her tastes, absolutely opposite to mine." When she received absolution: "He's put a nice white little dress on me and changed my smock."

- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (Marie); Mère Agnès (recorder)

- New to English readers: yes

### F-OW-298 — If she had been a priest she would have preached on the Virgin
- Claim: "How I would have liked to be a priest to preach on the Blessed Virgin! Once would have been enough." Preachers should not say improbable things, e.g. that Mary at three went to the Temple "with burning, quite extraordinary feelings... whereas she perhaps went simply to obey her parents". "They show her unapproachable; they should show her imitable... say she lived by faith like us." "She is more Mother than Queen"; she rejects the idea that Mary "eclipses the glory of all the saints as the rising sun makes the stars disappear. My God, how strange! A Mother who makes her children's glory disappear!" If a sermon is all "Ah! Ah!" "one has had enough!" "Everything I would preach about her, I said in my hymn 'Why I love you, O Mary!'" (PN 54).
- When / where: 21 Aug 1897 (also 23 Aug)
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 21.8.3 (see also Paroles retrouvées); 23.8.9, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « On la montre inabordable, il faudrait la montrer imitable »
- Translation: "They show her as out of reach; they should show her as someone you can imitate."
- New to English readers: partial (a softened form in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Notes: A critique of 19th-century Marian preaching: "what set her apart".

### F-OW-299 — Fear of gangrene; "several illnesses at once"
- Claim: She suffered violently in the intestines "and... elsewhere"; gangrene was feared. "Well, it's better, if one has to suffer much and everywhere, to have several illnesses together. It's like a journey, where one puts up with all sorts of discomforts, knowing it will end soon." "There are only the hands left!... People don't know what it is to suffer like this. No, you have to feel it." "Pray for me, my little sisters, but not on your knees, sitting down." (they were kneeling). Milk diet stopped 23 Aug.
- When / where: 22–23 Aug 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 22.8.2, 22.8.4, 22.8.10; 23.8.10, cj-aout-1897; Carmel editors' health note for August
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune); scholarship (Carmel editors) for the medical summary
- Rights: public-domain; transcription and notes: unknown
- Original words: « Il n'y a plus que les mains !... »
- Translation: "Only my hands are left!"
- Other sources: The Carmel editors' August note: haemoptysis stopped 5 Aug; pain in side, shoulder and right arm; breathlessness daily from 11 a.m. to midnight; from 15 Aug oppression, left-side pain, swollen legs, fever, could not rise alone; 17 Aug "tuberculosis at the last degree": left lung one-third affected, right lung wholly; hours of coughing, headaches, bedsores from emaciation, intestinal pain "to scream", fear of gangrene, burning thirst, hard swollen abdomen, very red urine; 30 Aug left lung half affected.
- New to English readers: yes (clinical detail)
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-300 — A private baby-talk that the infirmarian could not follow
- Claim: Céline: "We spoke together a sort of childish language the others could not grasp." Sr Saint-Stanislas, the first infirmarian, said admiringly: "How sweet those two little girls are with their unintelligible jargon!" Thérèse called Céline "bobonne" (nursie) with the prioress's permission, because it was easier to say than her name; later she stopped the nicknames as "not religious" (CJ 22.9.4). She told Céline stories and allegories (the rich "demoiselle" and her poor little brother; a dream of a lamb). Céline made a sling from folded linen hung from the bed canopy to hold her painful right arm: "God will make 'hangings' for bobonne too!"
- When / where: Aug 1897
- People: Sr Geneviève (recorder); Sr Saint-Stanislas
- Source: Dernières paroles à Céline, 24 Août; Juillet n. 2 note; 4 Août n. 2; Août nn. 2, 6, same URL

- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- Translation: "How sweet those two little girls are, with their gibberish nobody can understand!"
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: medium.

### F-OW-301 — Sr Saint-Jean de la Croix laughing at the foot of the bed
- Claim: During the evening silence the infirmary door was open, and Sr Saint-Jean de la Croix came every evening, stood at the foot of the bed and looked at her laughing for quite a long time. Mère Agnès called the visit "indiscreet". Thérèse: "Yes, it's very painful to be looked at laughing when one suffers. But I think Our Lord on the cross was looked at like that in his sufferings... it was even worse, for they really mocked him."
- When / where: 25 Aug 1897
- People: Sr Saint-Jean de la Croix; Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 25.8.1, cj-aout-1897

- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- Translation: "it's very hard to be stared at, laughing, when you're suffering"

- Claim: "Oh, how we must pray for the dying! If people knew!" "I believe the devil asked God's permission to tempt me by extreme suffering, to make me fail in patience and faith." On 25 Aug, feast of St Louis, she prayed fervently to her father "and was not heard"; "despite what I felt at first, I told God again that I loved him more, and all the saints too." "I'll suffocate!"

- Claim: "How I'd like the bishop not to come and see me... Still, a bishop's blessing is a grace." Laughing: "If it were only St Nicholas, who raised three little children!" (Mgr Hugonin was in Lisieux.) "How odd it is to be afraid of dying! ...When you're married, with a husband and children, one understands; but I who have nothing!..." ### F-OW-305 — The black hole under the chestnut trees
- Claim: The bed was turned towards the window. At midday silence: "Look, do you see down there the black hole (under the chestnut trees near the cemetery) where you can't make out anything any more? "I love flowers very much, roses, red flowers and beautiful pink daisies." "The little flowers tremble with me, I like that" (forget-me-nots around her pictures). Of the statue through a slit in the curtain: "Look, she's watching me!"

### F-OW-307 — 30 Aug 1897: wheeled to the choir door; the last photograph; the doctor's verdict
- Claim: She was put on a folding bed and rolled to the choir door under the cloister, left alone a long while, prayed looking at the grille, and threw rose petals on it. "She was photographed before going back in." Dr La Néele said: "It's for soon, my little sister, I'm sure." She smiled with happiness. The chaplain Youf told her: "You have suffered more than you will suffer now... We are finishing our ministry together, you as a Carmelite, I as a priest."
- When / where: 30 Aug 1897, cloister of the Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Dr Francis La Néele (her cousin Jeanne's husband); Abbé Youf; Mère Agnès (recorder); photographer (Sr Geneviève, not named here)
- Source: CJ 30.8.3, cj-aout-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown. The photograph itself: not free to use (plan 01 §9)

- Translation: "She was photographed before being taken back in."
- New to English readers: yes (the circumstance of the last photograph)
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-308 — "Who could have invented the Blessed Virgin?"
- Claim: After looking long at the statue: "Who could have invented the Blessed Virgin?" "How much courage I need to make a sign of the cross!" "Ah! If you love me, how I love you too!" At lunch: "I wanted to have a real dinner, so I took a grape and a little sip of wine, which I offered to the Blessed Virgin. Then I did the same for the Child Jesus, and my little dinner was over." Told the bed would soon be empty: "Ah, in your place how happy I'd be!" "It's incredible how all my hopes have been fulfilled... I begged God to... consume me quickly in love, and I've been heard!"

- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)

- Claim: Céline: "You'll look down on us from Heaven, won't you?" Thérèse answered spontaneously: "No, I'll come down!" At night Céline found her with joined hands and eyes raised: "I can't [sleep], I'm suffering too much, so I pray..." "And what do you say to Jesus?" "I don't say anything to him, I love him!"

### F-OW-310 — Two years before she could open her soul to her novice mistress; mortifications in the refectory
- Claim: "Perhaps two years after I came here God ended my trial with regard to Sr Marie des Anges, and I could open my soul to her... in the end she really comforted me." "It cost me a lot to ask to do mortifications in the refectory, because I was shy, I blushed, but I was faithful to my two times a week. When that trial of shyness passed, I paid less attention and must have forgotten more than once." To the sisters calling her "the leader of the band": she held her hands a little apart — "This high in the family!" — and mimed sowing: "Tom Thumb!" To "They're still expecting you at Saigon!": "I'll go, I'll go soon; if you knew how fast I'll do my round!" She offered her trial of faith "above all for a member of our family by marriage who has no faith" (M. Tostain).
- When / where: 2 Sep 1897
- People: Sr Marie des Anges (novice mistress); Sr Geneviève; René Tostain (husband of a Guérin relative); Mère Agnès (recorder)

- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- Translation: "I offered my inner trial against faith above all for a relative of ours by marriage who has no faith."

- Claim: Told of the honours paid to the Tsar of Russia in France: "Ah! all that doesn't dazzle me! Talk to me about God, the example of the saints, everything that is truth..." "When you think we're nursing a little saint!" "Well, so much the better! but I'd like God to say so."

### F-OW-312 — Her list of loves at the Angelus
- Claim: At the Angelus, too weak to say it, told just to say "Virgin Mary!", she said: "Virgin Mary, I love you with all my heart", and, when Céline asked her to add "for me too", she whispered: "for 'Mlle Lili', for Mama, for Godmother, for Léonie, little Marie, my Uncle, my Aunt, Jeanne, Francis, 'Maurice', 'little Roulland' and all those I love." She was annoyed that her craving for a simple dish had been reported to her uncle: "It's very odd that they let that be known in the world!" then: "It's offered to God. Let them think what they like!"

### F-OW-313 — "There is one, perhaps... and even it's certain!"
- Claim: "So you're not sad to leave 'Mama'?" (childlike) "No!... If there were no eternal life, oh yes!... but there is one, perhaps... and even it's certain!" If she recovered: "Gentlemen, I'm very glad to be cured to serve God on earth still... I've suffered as if I were going to die; well, I'll start again another time!" After a drink with ice: "I drank without being thirsty! I'm a little 'drink-without-thirst'." Dr La Néele, who had said she might die turning over in bed, now said: "You're like a ship that neither advances nor goes back."

### F-OW-314 — Seventh anniversary of profession: a robin, Léonie's music box, wild flowers
- Claim: On 8 Sep a robin hopped onto her bed. Léonie sent a music box (kept by the Carmel) whose tunes, though secular, she enjoyed. A sheaf of wild flowers came for her profession anniversary; she cried with gratitude: "outwardly I'm loaded with them, and yet inwardly I'm still in the trial... but also in peace." On 9 Sep the box was overwound; the gardener Auguste repaired it, but it lost its prettiest note.
- When / where: 8–9 Sep 1897
- People: Léonie Martin; Auguste (the Carmel's gardener/handyman); Mère Agnès (recorder)

### F-OW-315 — "A kiss that makes a noise"; fear of the separation of soul and body
- Claim: "Give me a kiss, a kiss that makes a noise; the lips must go 'pit!'" "You are a lyre, a song to me... much more than a music box." "I'm afraid I was afraid of death... But I'm not afraid of what comes after, of course! And I don't regret life, oh no. It's only telling myself: what is this mysterious separation of soul and body? It's the first time I've felt that." She kissed her crucifix for the plenary indulgence for the souls in purgatory ("I give them nothing but that now") and the relics of Anne of Jesus and Vénard. Unable to sleep: "It's the devil's malice; he's furious because I didn't forget my little devotions."

- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)

### F-OW-316 — Too weak to be touched; "like a little grasshopper"
- Claim: Her feet had swollen the day before; any movement of the bed or touch hurt her. Marie and Agnès took her pulse at length; she said nothing so as not to hurt them, then cried. "I'd like... not to hurt my little sisters any more, and for that to go away quickly." Trying to shift her cushion herself: "Wait, I'll push myself to the end of the bed, making the movements of a little grasshopper." A sister brought a violet; she signed to ask if she might smell it "without immortification".

### F-OW-317 — Rose petals on the crucifix: "don't lose any"
- Claim: Given a rose, she pulled off the petals over her crucifix, touching Christ's wounds with each petal: "In September little Thérèse still strips 'the spring rose'." As the petals slid to the floor: "Gather these petals carefully, my little sisters; they will help you give pleasure later... don't lose any." Dr La Néele had told her she would have no agony; "They told me I'd have no agony!... But after all, I'm willing to have one." "Would you choose?" "I'd choose nothing!"

### F-OW-318 — Seeing her face in the chalice
- Claim: After Abbé Denis's first Mass she asked to see his chalice and looked long at the bottom of the cup: "Because I'm reflected in it. In the sacristy I liked doing that. I was glad to say: My features have been reflected where the blood of Jesus has rested and will come down again. How often too I've thought that in Rome my face was reproduced in the Holy Father's eyes." On 18 Sep she admired Céline's altar cloth for the oratory and Abbé Denis's vestment.

### F-OW-319 — "Patient? That's a lie!" "A skeleton!"
- Claim: Dr de Cornière said she must be suffering "a real martyrdom" and praised her "heroic patience". Thérèse: "How can he say I'm patient! It's a lie! I never stop moaning, I sigh, I cry out all the time: Oh là là! and then: My God, I can't go on! Have pity, have pity on me!" When her tunic was changed they were struck by her thinness, though her face was unchanged; the prioress came late and said, "What's this, such a thin little girl?" — "A skeleton!" ("Un quelette!", mimicking a child).

- People: Dr de Cornière; Mère Marie de Gonzague; Mère Agnès (recorder)

### F-OW-320 — The spittoon; eyelashes "for the earth"
- Claim: Mère Agnès silently emptied her spittoon, thinking how happy she would be if Thérèse said she would repay it in Heaven; at once Thérèse said: "In Heaven I'll repay you for that." When some eyelashes came out as she wiped her eyes: "Take these eyelashes, my little Sr Geneviève; we must give as little as possible to the earth," and punned on the name of a workman, Père Alaterre ("to the earth"): "Poor old fellow, though, if it gives him pleasure!" Of Bellière: "He calls me his little Thérèse!... I'm 'fed up' with little Thérèse! ...Not fed up with little Pauline, oh no!" "What is agony? It seems to me I'm in it all the time!"

### F-OW-321 — "If I had not had faith, I would have killed myself"
- Claim: "What a terrible illness, and how much you've suffered!" — "Yes!!! What a grace to have faith! If I had not had faith, I would have killed myself without hesitating a single instant..." Earlier that day: "It would not have been necessary to pity me so much, I assure you... I came out strengthened by humiliations; there was no one braver than me under fire." About the saints: "They want to see... how far I'll push my trust." She stopped the use of nicknames ("bobonne", "poupée"): "We mustn't call each other all sorts of names. All the same, it's not religious!" "It seems to me it was yesterday that I followed the Community, that I wrote the notebook."
- When / where: 22 Sep 1897
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Sr Geneviève ("bobonne"); Sr Marie de la Trinité ("poupée")

- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- Translation: "If I had not had faith, I would have taken my own life without hesitating for a moment."

### F-OW-322 — "You raised me"; the bird of prey
- Claim: After a long silence, to Marie and Agnès alone: "My little sisters, it's you who brought me up!..." and her eyes filled with tears. At night a cooing was heard at the window; one sister said a turtle-dove, another a bird of prey: "Well, if it's a bird of prey, too bad! Birds of prey came to eat the martyrs." "Many times I've given beautiful smiles, lost, to 'Bobonne' and others."
- When / where: 23 Sep 1897
- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Mère Agnès (recorder)

- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown

- Translation: "My little sisters, it's you who raised me!"
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-323 — The chestnut trees pruned; Dr de Cornière "had never seen that"
- Claim: She told of an old sorrow: one year the chestnut trees were pruned far too late; "I loved the shade so much! ...the branches already green lay in bundles on the ground"; then she thought: "If I were in another Carmel, what would it matter to me if they cut down even all the chestnut trees of the Carmel of Lisieux!" and felt peace. Dr de Cornière told the prioress: "She's an angel! Her face isn't altered despite her great sufferings. I've never seen that. With her general emaciation, it's supernatural." Thérèse: "I wanted to tell M. de Cornière: I'm laughing because you couldn't stop me going to Heaven after all; but for your trouble, when I'm there I'll stop you going there too soon." "If I go among the Seraphim I won't do as they do... all cover themselves with their wings before God; I'll take good care not to cover myself with my wings."
- When / where: 24 Sep 1897
- People: Dr de Cornière; Mère Marie de Gonzague; Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 24.9.2–3, 24.9.5, 24.9.7, cj-septembre-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « quand j'y serai je vous empêcherai d'y aller si tôt »
- Translation: "when I'm there I'll keep you from getting there too soon"
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: medium-high.

### F-OW-324 — "I have no intuitions... I'm in such poverty"
- Claim: "So you have no intuition of the day of your death?" — "Ah, Mother, intuitions! If you knew what poverty I'm in! I know nothing but what you know; I guess nothing except by what I see and feel. But my soul, despite its darkness, is in astonishing peace."

- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 24.9.10, cj-septembre-1897

### F-OW-325 — "It's easy to write beautiful things about suffering"
- Claim: In a day of terrible suffering she had said: "Mother, it's very easy to write beautiful things about suffering, but writing is nothing, nothing! You have to be in it to know!" On 25 Sep, as if remembering, she looked at Mère Agnès "solemnly": "I feel now that what I said and wrote is true on every point... It's true I wanted to suffer much for God, and it's true that I still want it." Also: "If I had died at 80, if I had been in China, everywhere, I would have died, I feel sure, as little as today." "For the little ones, they will be judged with extreme gentleness."
- When / where: 25 Sep 1897 (the first saying some days earlier)
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Abbé Youf (mentioned: he feared death)
- Source: CJ 25.9.1–3, cj-septembre-1897
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès, Carnet jaune)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « c'est bien facile d'écrire de belles choses sur la souffrance, mais d'écrire ce n'est rien, rien ! Il faut y être pour savoir !... »
- Translation: "it's very easy to write fine things about suffering, but writing is nothing, nothing! You have to be in it to know!"
- New to English readers: yes (the first saying)

### F-OW-326 — Mère Cœur de Jésus and "Father Corbinelli"
- Claim: The refectory reading was the Life of St Aloysius Gonzaga; the old Mère Cœur de Jésus told Thérèse: "You are little Louis and I am old Father Corbinelli; when you're in Heaven, remember me!" Thérèse offered to come and fetch her soon; Mère Cœur de Jésus said she needed permission from authority; the prioress said yes without attaching importance. In her last days Thérèse sent her a message: "Tell Mère Cœur de Jésus that this morning, during Mass, I saw the tomb of Father Corbinelli very close to that of little Louis." (The account, by Sr Hermance du Cœur de Jésus, calls the word "truly prophetic"; it does not state when Mère Cœur de Jésus died.)

### F-OW-327 — The dead leaf on a spider's thread
- Claim: Looking out of the window at a dead leaf hanging in the air on a thin thread: "Look, it's my image; my life hangs by no more than a light thread." Mère Agnès adds that on the evening of 30 Sep, after her death, the leaf fell and she picked it up "with its spider's thread still attached". On 27 Sep, between two and three o'clock, offered a drink, she asked for Lourdes water: "Until 3 o'clock I prefer Lourdes water, it's more pious."

### F-OW-328 — She would not throw flowers to creatures
- Claim: Mère Agnès said, with the community around the bed, "If you threw flowers to the Community!" Thérèse: "Oh no, my little mother, don't ask me that, please; I don't want to throw flowers to creatures. I'm still willing for the Blessed Virgin and St Joseph, but not for other creatures." Sr Marthe de Jésus recorded: weeks before death, Thérèse made her promise to say a Memorare every day ("I won't be at peace about you").
