- Confidence: high.
- Notes: St Agnes was Pauline's patron (Sr Agnès). In the same letter she tells Marie she read Marie's letter before Pauline's, correcting Marie's "rash judgement".

### F-OW-361 — Eve of the audience: "there is nothing for me in Rome, everything is for artists"
- Claim: The evening before the audience, Thérèse writes that tomorrow, Sunday, she will speak to the Pope and her heart beats hard. She cannot tell of her journey; she leaves that to Céline, whose pen "runs" while hers stops. Italy is "the land of artists"; "I am not an artist"; "there is nothing for me in Rome, everything is for artists! If only I could have one word from the Pope..."
- When / where: Saturday 19 November 1887, Rome.
- People: Marie Guérin (recipient, musician); Jeanne Guérin (painter); Céline; Mme Guérin (feast that day).
- Source: LT 35 (to Marie Guérin, 19 Nov 1887), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-35-a-marie-guerin-19-novembre-1887/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « tu vois, ma petite sœur, qu'il n'y a rien pour moi à Rome, tout est pour les artistes ! Si seulement je pouvais avoir un mot du Pape, je n'en demanderais pas davantage... »
- Translation: "You see, little sister, there is nothing for me in Rome; everything is for the artists! If only I could have one word from the Pope, I would ask for nothing more..."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: She contrasts herself with her artistic cousins (Marie: music; Jeanne: painting).

### F-OW-362 — The audience with Leo XIII, told the same evening: "so old you would think he was dead"
- Claim: Thérèse tells Pauline the audience the same day. The bishop was absent; Abbé Révérony replaced him and stood beside the Pope, who sat on a very high chair. Pilgrims kissed the Pope's foot and passed. She said part of what Pauline had written, but Révérony did not give her time: he said, "Most Holy Father, this is a child who wants to enter Carmel at fifteen, but her superiors are dealing with it at the moment." She writes: "The good Pope is so old that you would think he was dead... he can say almost nothing; it is M. Révérony who speaks." The Pope said simply: "If the good God wills it, you will enter." She was taken into another room. She felt "as if crushed", abandoned, far away. She is afraid Papa will come and ask to read her letter.
- When / where: Sunday 20 November 1887, Vatican; letter written that evening.
- People: Pope Leo XIII; Abbé Maxime Révérony (vicar general of Bayeux, leader of the pilgrimage); Mgr Hugonin (absent); Pauline (recipient); Louis Martin; Mère Marie de Gonzague; Marie.
- Source: LT 36 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 20 Nov 1887), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-36-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-20-novembre-1887/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Très Saint Père, c'est une enfant qui veut entrer au Carmel à quinze ans, mais ses supérieurs s'en occupent en ce moment. (Le bon Pape est si vieux qu'on dirait qu'il est mort, je ne me le serais jamais figuré comme cela, il ne peut dire presque rien, c'est M. Révérony qui parle). [...] Le Saint-Père m'a dit simplement : Si le bon Dieu veut vous entrerez. [...] j'étais comme anéantie, je me sentais abandonnée [...] j'ai peur que Papa ne vienne et me demande de lire ma lettre, et c'est impossible. »
- Translation: "'Most Holy Father, this is a child who wants to enter Carmel at fifteen, but her superiors are looking into it at present.' (The good Pope is so old you would think he was dead; I would never have imagined him like that; he can say almost nothing; it is M. Révérony who does the talking.) [...] The Holy Father simply said to me: 'If the good God wills, you will enter.' [...] I was as if crushed; I felt abandoned. [...] I am afraid Papa will come and ask to read my letter, and that is impossible."
- Other sources: Ms A (1911) — see conflict card below. Baseline: Rome trip and Leo XIII.
- New to English readers: yes — the letter's version ("si vieux qu'on dirait qu'il est mort") is ABSENT from 1911 and not in baseline.
- Confidence: high — eyewitness letter written hours later.
- Notes: She hides the letter from her father. She writes that she said only part of Pauline's text.

### F-OW-363 — Conflict: the audience in the letter (1887) vs Story of a Soul (1895, printed 1911)
- Claim: The same-day letter and Ms A differ. LT 36: Révérony interrupts; the Pope "can say almost nothing"; he says only "Si le bon Dieu veut vous entrerez"; she is "made to pass" into another room. Ms A adds: Révérony loudly forbade anyone to speak to the Pope; Céline whispered "Parle!"; she asked "in honour of your Jubilee"; the Pope said "Do what the superiors decide", then, stressing each syllable, "Allons... Allons... vous entrerez si le bon Dieu le veut"; two noble guards and Révérony lifted her; the Pope put his hand on her lips and followed her with his eyes.
- When / where: 20 November 1887, Vatican.
- People: Leo XIII; Abbé Révérony; Céline; noble guards.
- Source: LT 36 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 20 Nov 1887), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-36-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-20-novembre-1887/ ; Histoire d'une âme 1911, chap. VI (Gutenberg #36708).
- Source type: own-writing (both)
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: LT 36: « Si le bon Dieu veut vous entrerez » ; 1911: « Allons... Allons... vous entrerez si le bon Dieu le veut. »
- Translation: LT 36: "If the good God wills, you will enter." / 1911: "Come, come... you will enter if the good God wills it."
- New to English readers: yes — the letter's plainer version is not in English baseline.
- Confidence: high that the texts differ; the letter is closer in time. Ms A may add true details omitted in a hurried letter, but the letter gives no guards and no lifting.
- Notes: The letter's "he can say almost nothing" contrasts with Ms A's "his black, deep eyes" and firm, syllable-by-syllable speech.

### F-OW-364 — Assisi: left behind, she rides in Révérony's carriage — "he did not speak to me about my affairs"
- Claim: At Assisi, coming back from a church, Thérèse found herself alone with no carriage except Abbé Révérony's. He made her get in with him. He was very kind and would not let her pay her fare. "He did not speak to me at all about my affairs; I don't know what he thinks of the audience." She also writes that she thinks of her family so often "that I dream of it at night" and feels "a magnet" drawing her back to Lisieux.
- When / where: 24 November 1887 ("Hier"), Assisi; letter from Florence, Friday 25 November 1887.
- People: Abbé Maxime Révérony; Marie Guérin (recipient); Mme Guérin.
- Source: LT 37 (to Marie Guérin, 25 Nov 1887), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-37-a-marie-guerin-25-novembre-1887/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Il m'a fait monté avec lui ; il était très aimable, il n'a pas voulu que je paie ma place. Il ne m'a pas du tout parlé de mes affaires, je ne sais ce qu'il pense de l'audience. »
- Translation: "He had me get in with him; he was very kind; he would not let me pay for my seat. He did not speak to me about my affairs at all; I don't know what he thinks about the audience."
- Other sources: Ms A (1911) tells the same scene (she lost her belt buckle; felt "like a squirrel caught in a trap") but says Révérony talked to her about Carmel and promised to do all he could. LT 38 (early December) says Révérony "promised me during the Rome journey" to speak to the bishop.
- New to English readers: partial — the Assisi carriage scene is in Ms A (Taylor); the letter's "did not speak of my affairs" and fare detail are new.
- Confidence: medium on what Révérony said — the three texts conflict (see Gaps). High that the letter says this.
- Notes: The promise mentioned in LT 38 may have come on another day of the trip; the sources do not settle it.

### F-OW-365 — Letter to the bishop: "trials are a sign of vocation"
- Claim: Back in Lisieux, Thérèse writes to Mgr Hugonin asking for "the answer I have desired so long". She says trials are a sign of vocation and God has not spared her them. The Child Jesus "made me feel so strongly that he wanted me at Christmas" that she cannot resist. "It is true I am very young; but, Monseigneur, since God calls me and Papa is willing..." She hopes Abbé Révérony has spoken for her, as he promised during the Rome journey. She signs "the littlest daughter" of his Grandeur, "Thérèse Martin".
- When / where: 3–8 December 1887 (date uncertain in source), Lisieux.
- People: Mgr Flavien-Abel-Antoine Hugonin, bishop of Bayeux and Lisieux; Abbé Révérony; Louis Martin.
- Source: LT 38 (to Mgr Hugonin, 3–8 Dec 1887), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-38-a-mgr-hugonin-3-8-decembre-1887/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « O Monseigneur ! on dit que les épreuves sont un signe de vocation [...] Le petit Jésus m'a si bien fait sentir qu'il me voulait à Noël que je ne puis résister à la grâce qu'il me fait. Il est vrai que je suis bien jeune ; mais, Monseigneur, puisque le bon Dieu m'appelle et que Papa veut bien. »
- Translation: "O Monseigneur! They say trials are a sign of vocation [...] The little Jesus made me feel so clearly that he wanted me at Christmas that I cannot resist the grace he gives me. It is true that I am very young; but, Monseigneur, since the good God calls me and Papa is willing."
- Other sources: Ms A (1911) mentions writing to the bishop after the return.
- New to English readers: yes — the letter text is not in 1911 or baseline.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Her sentence "puisque le bon Dieu m'appelle et que Papa veut bien" is left unfinished in the source.

### F-OW-366 — To Abbé Révérony: "Why call me so strongly if it is to make me languish far from him?"
- Claim: Thérèse writes to the vicar general that she has written to the bishop "with Papa's and my Uncle's permission". Eight days remain until Christmas; the closer it comes, the more she hopes — "perhaps it is rashness", but it seems Jesus speaks in her. The distractions of the Rome journey never drove out her desire. "Ah! why call me so strongly if it is to make me languish far from him?" If she enters at Christmas she will owe it to him.
- When / where: 16 December 1887, Lisieux.
- People: Abbé Maxime Révérony (vicar general of Bayeux); Mgr Hugonin; Louis Martin; Isidore Guérin.
- Source: LT 39 (to Abbé Révérony, 16 Dec 1887), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-39-a-labbe-reverony-16-decembre-1887/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « plus le temps s'avance, plus j'espère, c'est peut-être de la témérité mais pourtant il me semble bien que c'est Jésus qui parle en moi. [...] Ah ! pourquoi m'appeler si fort si c'est pour me faire languir loin de lui ? »
- Translation: "The more time goes on, the more I hope. Perhaps it is rashness, but it really seems to me that it is Jesus speaking in me. [...] Ah! why call me so strongly if it is to make me languish far from him?"
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Shows she wrote again to the bishop around 16 Dec with family consent (a second letter, not LT 38, or LT 38 itself; the source does not say).

### F-OW-367 — The bishop's New Year gift: "He said yes to me from his cradle"
- Claim: Thérèse began a thank-you letter to Mgr Hugonin for the "beautiful New Year's gifts" (étrennes) he sent her. "All the beauties of this world would not have given me so much pleasure. So the Child Jesus did not deceive me! He said yes to me from his cradle." The draft breaks off.
- When / where: early January 1888, Lisieux.
- People: Mgr Hugonin.
- Source: LT 40 (to Mgr Hugonin, early Jan 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-40-a-mgr-hugonin-debut-janvier-1888/
- Source type: own-writing; plus a Carmel editorial note (scholarship, Carmel editors)
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « L'Enfant Jésus ne m'a donc pas trompée ! Il m'a dit oui dans son berceau. »
- Translation: "So the Child Jesus did not deceive me! He said yes to me in his cradle."
- Other sources: Ms A (1911) says the bishop's positive answer (via the prioress) came on 28 December 1887 but entry was delayed to after Lent.
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for her words. The Carmel's note says the text is unfinished, perhaps a first copy she judged badly written (editor note, rights unknown).
- Notes: The letter does not say what the "étrennes" were, nor mention the delay of entry to April.

### F-OW-368 — A courteous letter to Canon Delatroëtte, the Carmel's superior
- Claim: Thérèse thanks "Monsieur le Curé" for "the beautiful picture" he sent her through Sr Agnès, which she will keep as a precious first souvenir. She asks him to bless "the littlest of your children", who is preparing her soul for Carmel. "I know it is a very great grace to be called so young." A postscript passes on a note from M. Révérony, which Céline was asked to give him.
- When / where: 13–30 January 1888, Lisieux.
- People: Canon Jean-Baptiste Delatroëtte (parish priest of Saint-Jacques, ecclesiastical superior of the Lisieux Carmel); Sr Agnès; Abbé Révérony; Céline.
- Source: LT 41 (to Canon Delatroëtte, 13–30 Jan 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-41-au-chanoine-delatroette-13-30-janvier-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Je sais que c'est une bien grande grâce d'être appelée si jeune, mais je ne serai pas ingrate »
- Translation: "I know it is a very great grace to be called so young, but I will not be ungrateful."
- Other sources: Ms A (1911): the superior would not allow entry before 21, received her "very coldly", and said he was only the bishop's delegate. This letter shows her polite tone toward the main opponent after the decision.
- New to English readers: yes — not in 1911 or baseline.
- Confidence: high for the text; the identification of "Monsieur le Curé" as Delatroëtte is from the file header.
- Notes: Opponent: Canon Delatroëtte; type: Church authority; reason (from Ms A): her age. The letter itself does not mention any conflict.

### F-OW-369 — The newborn lamb that died the same day
- Claim: On Ash Wednesday her father gave her a present: "at the bottom of Papa's big bag, a lovely little curly Lamb". He wanted her to have the pleasure of a little lamb before she entered Carmel. Céline was delighted. The lamb died that afternoon; it had been too cold in the cart where it was born. Céline painted its portrait on a small canvas; Papa dug a grave; Thérèse did not want earth to cover it, so they threw snow on it. She draws the lesson: on earth one must be attached to nothing, not even innocent things. She also wishes Marie a happy 28th birthday (22 Feb) and says she prayed much for "Monsieur de Virville".
- When / where: Ash Wednesday (15 Feb 1888) at Les Buissonnets; letter Tuesday 21 February 1888.
- People: Louis Martin; Céline (painter); Marie (Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur, recipient); Léonie (wanted part of the page); M. de Virville (not identified).
- Source: LT 42 (to Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur, 21 Feb 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-42-a-soeur-marie-du-sacre-coeur-21-fevrier-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Figure-toi, ma chère Marie, dans le fond du grand sac à Papa un petit Agneau ravissant et tout frisé. [...] elle avait eu trop froid dans la voiture où elle était née [...] je n'ai pas voulu que ce soit la terre qui le recouvre, nous avons jeté de la neige sur lui et puis tout a été fini... »
- Translation: "Just imagine, my dear Marie, at the bottom of Papa's big bag, a lovely little curly Lamb. [...] it had been too cold in the cart where it was born [...] I did not want the earth to cover it; we threw snow over it, and then it was all over..."
- Other sources: 1911 prints this letter in edited form (see next card).
- New to English readers: partial — the story is in 1911/Taylor; the bag, Ash Wednesday, the cold cart and the canvas are cut there.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: The date of entry was still not fixed at this point.

### F-OW-370 — Edit: the lamb letter in 1911 — "bought", "last week", cause of death cut
- Claim: The 1911 edition changes LT 42. Original: Papa's gift appears "at the bottom of Papa's big bag" on "Ash Wednesday". 1911: "the week before, this good little father bought me a little lamb, one day old, all white and all curly". 1911 cuts the cause of death (cold cart where it was born), the Lenten sacrifice, "sur une petite toile", the birthday, and M. de Virville. 1911 addresses "MA CHÈRE MARIE" instead of "Ma chère Marraine".
- When / where: 21 February 1888 letter; 1911 print.
- People: Louis Martin; Marie; Pauline (editor of 1911 text, per general knowledge; not stated in the file).
- Source: LT 42 (to Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur, 21 Feb 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-42-a-soeur-marie-du-sacre-coeur-21-fevrier-1888/ ; Histoire d'une âme 1911, "Lettres à Sœur Marie du Sacré-Cœur", Lettre Ire.
- Source type: own-writing vs edited text
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: LT 42: « le Mercredi des Cendres papa m'a fait un cadeau [...] dans le fond du grand sac à Papa » ; 1911: « Si tu savais le cadeau que papa m'a fait la semaine dernière! [...] ce bon petit père m'a acheté un petit agneau d'un jour, tout blanc et tout frisé. »
- Translation: LT 42: "On Ash Wednesday Papa gave me a present [...] at the bottom of Papa's big bag." / 1911: "If you knew the present Papa gave me last week! [...] this good little father bought me a one-day-old lamb, all white and curly."
- New to English readers: yes — the difference is not known from Taylor.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: A small smoothing edit: domestic details (bag, cart) removed; "bought" added.

### F-OW-371 — Waiting for 9 April: "I felt it in Rome, when I thought the earth might give way under my feet"
- Claim: Thérèse writes that trials help detach from earth. Her "little boat" struggles to reach port. Once in Carmel she wants only "always to suffer for Jesus". She felt God's gentle hand strike her "in Rome at the very moment when I would have thought the earth might give way under my feet". If at death she could have one soul to offer Jesus, "a soul snatched from the fire of hell", she would be happy. Céline has a sore foot and may miss Vespers; "almost everyone is ill at my uncle's". She ends "Until Easter Monday, but above all until 9 April".
- When / where: about 18 March 1888, Lisieux.
- People: Pauline (Sr Agnès, "ma Confidente"); Marie (profession "à l'intérieur" pending); Céline; the Guérins; Mère Marie de Gonzague.
- Source: LT 43 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 18(?) Mar 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-43-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-18-mars1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « je l'ai bien senti à Rome au moment même où j'aurais cru que la terre aurait pu manquer sous mes pas. / Je ne désire qu'une chose quand je serai au Carmel, c'est de toujours souffrir pour Jésus. [...] Au revoir ma Pauline chérie, ma Confidente. Au Lundi de Pâques mais surtout au 9 Avril... »
- Translation: "I really felt it in Rome, at the very moment when I would have thought the earth could give way under my feet. / I desire only one thing when I am in Carmel: always to suffer for Jesus. [...] Goodbye, my dear Pauline, my Confidante. Until Easter Monday, but above all until 9 April..."
- Other sources: 1911 prints an edited version (next card). Baseline: entry 9 April 1888.
- New to English readers: partial — edited version in Taylor; Rome sentence, family illness and the date line are new.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Shows the entry date (9 April) was fixed by mid-March 1888.

### F-OW-372 — Edit: LT 43 in 1911 — redated to 1887, "Pauline" becomes "Maman", Rome cut
- Claim: 1911 prints LT 43 as "Lettre Ire" to Mère Agnès, dated "1887, a few months before Thérèse's entry". The greeting "Ma chère petite Pauline" becomes "MA PETITE MAMAN CHÉRIE". The sentence about Rome ("I felt it in Rome...") is cut. "A soul snatched from the fire of hell, which would bless God for all eternity" becomes "one soul fewer in hell, one more to bless God". "Ma petite nacelle" becomes "Ma nacelle", and the port is made explicit: "the blessed shore of Carmel".
- When / where: letter 18 March 1888; 1911 print.
- People: Pauline (recipient; later editor).
- Source: LT 43 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 18(?) Mar 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-43-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-18-mars1888/ ; Histoire d'une âme 1911, "Lettres à la Rde Mère Agnès de Jésus", Lettre Ire.
- Source type: own-writing vs edited text
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: LT 43: « Il y aurait une âme qui serait arrachée au feu de l'enfer » ; 1911: « Il y aurait une âme de moins dans l'enfer »
- Translation: LT 43: "There would be one soul snatched from the fire of hell." / 1911: "There would be one soul fewer in hell."
- New to English readers: yes (the difference).
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: The wrong year (1887) in 1911 could mislead a biographer: the letter is from March 1888, after Rome.

### F-OW-373 — Pauline wrote her letter to the bishop; "I want to be a saint"
- Claim: On the eve of entry, Thérèse copied and sent to the bishop "the letter you made for me" — a letter composed by Pauline. Pauline also painted a picture for her. Thérèse asks whether she may tell her uncle's family that Pauline made the picture and that she wrote to the bishop: "If they learn it later they will not be pleased." She takes up Pauline's image: "I want always to be the LITTLE grain of sand." In brackets: "(I want to be a saint...)". She quotes a saying she read, author forgotten: "I am not perfect but I want to become so."
- When / where: Tuesday 27 March 1888, Lisieux.
- People: Pauline (Sr Agnès); Mgr Hugonin; the Guérins; Louis Martin (to take a note via the turn).
- Source: LT 45 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 27 Mar 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-45-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-27-mars-1888/ ; LT 44 (to Mgr Hugonin, 27 Mar 1888, fragment), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-44-a-mgr-hugonin-27-mars-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Je viens d'écrire à Monseigneur la lettre que tu m'as faite, je te remercie beaucoup. [...] Si plus tard ils le savent, ils ne seront pas contents [...] Oh ! oui Pauline, je veux toujours être le PETIT grain de sable... [...] (Je veux être une sainte...) [...] «Je ne suis pas parfait mais je veux le devenir.» »
- Translation: "I have just written to Monseigneur the letter you made for me; thank you very much. [...] If they find out later, they will not be pleased [...] Oh yes, Pauline, I always want to be the LITTLE grain of sand... [...] (I want to be a saint...) [...] 'I am not perfect, but I want to become so.'"
- New to English readers: yes — ABSENT from 1911 ("pas parfait mais je veux"); not in baseline.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Important for authorship: at least one of her letters to the bishop (LT 44, 27 Mar 1888) was drafted by Pauline. First appearance in these letters of the "grain of sand" image, which she says comes from Pauline's letter.

### F-OW-374 — Three weeks in Carmel: "the little Orphan of the Berezina" and the Roman guide's jokes
- Claim: Her father sends a present almost every day. Thérèse calls herself "the little Orphan of the Berezina" (a family nickname). She jokes in the words of their guide in Italy: in Heaven "we will see fine 'Estatues' on fine 'Cornichons'", "we will go into 'Extasaison'", "many saints will have a Byzantine cross in their halo", and "only sarcophagi we will not see, for in Heaven there are no more tombs". "The little fine pearl" (Pauline) and "the brilliant Diamond, the Bohemian" (Marie) send kisses. She signs "your little Queen who has at last been pulled out from under the cart", then "Thérèse de l'Enfant Jésus" — her first letter signed with her religious name.
- When / where: Sunday 29 April 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Louis Martin; Sr Agnès (perle fine); Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (Diamant); the pilgrimage guide in Italy (unnamed).
- Source: LT 46 (to Louis Martin, 29 Apr 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-46-a-m-martin-29-avril-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « C'est là que nous verrons de belles Estatues sur des beaux Cornichons, alors nous pourrons vraiment entrer en Extasaison, et puis quel guide pour nous faire visiter les merveilles du Ciel !... [...] ta petite Reine qui est enfin tirée de dessous la charrette »
- Translation: "That is where we will see fine 'Estatues' on fine 'Cornichons' [gherkins, for 'cornices'], and then we will truly go into 'Extasaison'; and what a guide we will have to show us the wonders of Heaven!... [...] your little Queen who has at last been pulled out from under the cart."
- New to English readers: yes — "Bérézina" and "Extasaison" ABSENT from 1911; not in baseline or Taylor.
- Confidence: high for the text; medium for the meaning of the jokes (the guide's mistakes are implied, not explained).
- Notes: Shows father and daughter shared private jokes from Rome. "Pulled out from under the cart" suggests she felt rescued after the long struggle; the letter does not explain the phrase.

### F-OW-375 — "A Carmelite's day spent without suffering is a day lost" — and tablecloths to stitch
- Claim: A month after entry, Thérèse sends Céline two small tablecloths to be quilted "à la mécanique" (on a sewing machine), with two lines of stitching; one hem is too small. She needs them by Thursday, Ascension. It is four years since her First Communion. She sometimes cannot believe she is in Carmel. "A Carmelite's day spent without suffering is a lost day; for you it is the same, since you are a Carmelite at heart."
- When / where: 8 May 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Léonie.
- Source: LT 47 (to Céline, 8 May 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-47-a-celine-8-mai-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Je t'envoie, ma petite Céline, deux petites nappes à piquer à la mécanique. [...] Une journée de Carmélite passée sans souffrance est une journée perdue ; pour toi c'est la même chose car tu es Carmélite par le cœur. »
- Translation: "I am sending you, little Céline, two small tablecloths to be stitched on the machine. [...] A Carmelite's day spent without suffering is a lost day; for you it is the same, since you are a Carmelite at heart."
- New to English readers: yes — "journée de Carmélite" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: The postulant uses her family for practical needs of the Carmel (sewing machine at home).

### F-OW-376 — Edit: the 1911 "first letter to Céline" (8 May 1888) is a patchwork of three letters
- Claim: 1911 prints "Lettre Ire, 8 mai 1888" to Céline. It keeps the start of LT 47 ("Il y a des moments où je me demande...") but cuts the tablecloths and "A Carmelite's day without suffering is a lost day". It then adds a passage from LT 65 (20 Oct 1888: "si je m'écoutais, je demanderais à Jésus de me donner tes tristesses") and a passage from LT 57 (23 July 1888: "la vie est souvent pesante... quand Jésus se cache"). The phrase "l'ennuyeuse compagnie quand Jésus n'est pas là" (LT 57) is cut. In LT 57 Thérèse speaks of her own experience ("le faible bouton l'a vu comme le beau lis"); 1911 makes it a reply: "Tu as raison, la vie est souvent pesante".
- When / where: letters of 8 May, 23 July and 20 Oct 1888; 1911 print.
- People: Céline.
- Source: LT 47 (to Céline, 8 May 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-47-a-celine-8-mai-1888/ ; LT 57 (to Céline, 23 Jul 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-57-a-celine-23-juillet-1888/ ; LT 65 (to Céline, 20 Oct 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-65-a-celine-20-octobre-1888/ ; Histoire d'une âme 1911, letters to Céline, Lettre Ire.
- Source type: own-writing vs edited composite
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: LT 57: « oh ! l'ennuyeuse compagnie quand Jésus n'est pas là » (cut in 1911)
- Translation: "Oh! what boring company [we are to ourselves] when Jesus is not there."
- New to English readers: yes (the composite structure).
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: A biographer should not use the 1911/Taylor date "8 May 1888" for the "life is heavy" passage: it was written in July 1888.

### F-OW-377 — Her father's gifts and his sayings about famine and ruin
- Claim: Louis Martin sent candles ("belles petites bougies") for "illuminations" and a fish. Thérèse calls him "Jesus's postman". She jokes that he will ruin himself but she is not worried, and recalls his sayings: "We will eat this or that in the famine" and "We will do that when we are ruined."
- When / where: 8 May 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Louis Martin; Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (Diamant); Sr Agnès (Perle).
- Source: LT 48 (to Louis Martin, 8 May 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-48-a-m-martin-8-mai-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Te rappelles-tu quand tu me disais : «On mangera ceci ou cela dans la famine» ; ou bien : «On fera cela quand nous serons ruinés.» Dans ces dispositions aucun malheur ne peut faire peur. »
- Translation: "Do you remember when you used to say to me: 'We'll eat this or that in the famine,' or: 'We'll do that when we are ruined'? With that attitude, no misfortune can frighten you."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: A rare record of Louis Martin's own sayings.

### F-OW-378 — Apology to Marie before her profession: "you called me too often"
- Claim: Before Marie's profession (22 May 1888), Thérèse asks forgiveness "for all the sorrows I caused you" and says she repents of having told Marie "that you called me too often". She calls Marie an eagle and herself a weak reed; asks to remain "a little grain of sand, very obscure, hidden from all eyes". Life is like a night in a bad inn; better a wholly bad inn than a half-bad one. She has dried Marie's little violet.
- When / where: 12–20 May 1888, Lisieux Carmel (Marie in retreat, "La Solitaire du Coeur de Jésus").
- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (Marie Martin).
- Source: LT 49 (to Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur, 12–20 May 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-49-a-soeur-marie-du-sacre-coeur-12-20-mai-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Pardonnez-moi tous les chagrins que je vous ai faits, si vous saviez combien je me repens de vous avoir dit que vous m'appeliez trop souvent... »
- Translation: "Forgive me for all the sorrows I have caused you; if you knew how much I regret having told you that you called me too often..."
- Other sources: 1911 uses the "mauvaise hôtellerie" line inside a composite letter (see edit card, Jan 1889).
- New to English readers: yes — "appeliez trop souvent" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: A real friction inside the Carmel: Marie, her elder sister and godmother, sought her company; Thérèse pushed back, then apologized. The letter does not say when this happened.

### F-OW-379 — Joking with Marie Guérin from the Carmel: "strong as a little Samson"
- Claim: Thérèse laughs at her cousin's letter. Marie Guérin has a sore foot, "very strange, because your feet are so small there is no room". The Holy Spirit forgot one gift at her Confirmation (strength); Thérèse will pray so that at Pentecost she will be "strong as a little Samson" — "your strength must be not in your hair but in your foot". "Since I have been in Carmel, it is unbelievable how often I dream of Jeanne." She is glad the word "lamp" fell on this page (Marie had given her a lamp).
- When / where: Sunday 13 May 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Marie Guérin; Jeanne Guérin; Isidore and Mme Guérin.
- Source: LT 50 (to Marie Guérin, 13 May 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-50-a-marie-guerin-13-mai-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Petite vilaine, voilà que tu as mal au pied, c'est bien extraordinaire car tes pieds sont si petits qu'il n'y a vraiment pas la place. [...] Cette nuit j'ai beaucoup rêvé DANS Jeanne ; depuis que je suis au Carmel c'est incroyable combien j'en rêve souvent. »
- Translation: "Little wretch, now you have a sore foot; that is very odd, because your feet are so small there's really no room for it. [...] Last night I dreamed a lot about Jeanne; since I have been in Carmel it is incredible how often I dream of her."
- New to English readers: yes — "Samson" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high; the opening pun "la Pott au bout de la langue" is not explained.
- Notes: Humour continues in the postulancy. Dream report (see Miracles list, as a report only).

### F-OW-380 — Genoa memory: trailing "M. Benoît and the others"
- Claim: Thérèse tells her father he is "as holy as St Louis himself". She thanks him for "the beautiful shovel" and other gifts. She recalls how at Genoa "we followed M. Benoît and the others from a distance — ah, what fun we had!" She signs "Ta Reine de France et de Navarre".
- When / where: about 17 May 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Louis Martin; M. Benoît (a fellow pilgrim, not further identified).
- Source: LT 51 (to Louis Martin, 17(?) May 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-51-a-m-martin-17-mai-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Te rappelles-tu, Papa, quand à Gênes nous filions de loin M. Benoît et les autres, ah ! que nous avions de plaisir ! »
- Translation: "Do you remember, Papa, when in Genoa we trailed M. Benoît and the others from a distance? Ah, what fun we had!"
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for words; low for what exactly happened (not explained).
- Notes: A playful memory of the pilgrimage between father and daughter.

### F-OW-381 — Wine "from the Child Jesus's cellar" and three birds
- Claim: Her father sent wine; she will drink it "thinking it comes from the Child Jesus's cellar". Then she was shown birds he sent: three birds, "one for the Diamond, one for the fine Pearl and one for Papa's little Queen". She will try to "become a great saint" to be his glory.
- When / where: May–June 1888 (date uncertain), Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Louis Martin; Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Sr Agnès.
- Source: LT 52 (to Louis Martin, May–June 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-52-a-m-martin-mai-juin-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Je prendrai le vin qu'il me donne avec bonheur, pensant qu'il vient de la cave de l'Enfant Jésus. [...] Il y a trois oiseaux, un pour le diamant, un pour la perle fine et un pour la petite reine à Papa »
- Translation: "I will take the wine he gives me gladly, thinking it comes from the Child Jesus's cellar. [...] There are three birds: one for the diamond, one for the fine pearl, and one for Papa's little queen."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Postulants could receive such gifts; the letter does not say whether the birds stayed in the enclosure.

### F-OW-382 — Costume for St Agnes: apron fabric, a tartan belt, her First Communion ribbon
- Claim: Thérèse asks Céline to send the fabric she bought to make her an apron, the Scottish (tartan) belt Céline used for dressing up, and all her clean white ribbons, including the one she wore round her head at First Communion — "all this is to represent St Agnes". She tells Céline that the grace she received on Friday is the "decisive grace" Céline had been waiting for: Jesus "has already put the mysterious ring of betrothal on your finger".
- When / where: Sunday 17 June 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Louis Martin ("mon incomparable Roi").
- Source: LT 53 (to Céline, 17 Jun 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-53-a-celine-17-juin-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Il me faudrait aussi la ceinture écossaise que tu avais pour te déguiser. Envoie-moi aussi tous les rubans blancs propres que tu as, il y en a un que j'avais autour de la tête le jour de ma 1re Communion [...] Tout cela est pour représenter Ste Agnès... »
- Translation: "I would also need the tartan belt you used for dressing up. Send me also all the clean white ribbons you have; there is one I had round my head on my First Communion day [...] All this is to represent St Agnes..."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for the request; the occasion (probably a recreation piece for Pauline's feast, St Agnes) is not stated.
- Notes: The first sign in these letters of costume performance in the Carmel, years before the Joan of Arc plays. The letter does not say what Céline's Friday grace was.

### F-OW-383 — The reed by the water: "It is its weakness that makes all its trust"
- Claim: In the "agneau/agnelet" notes with Pauline, Thérèse writes that she desires "these anguishes of heart, these pin-pricks". The little reed does not fear breaking because it is planted by the water: when it bends it meets only a kindly stream. "It is its weakness that makes all its confidence." Small gusts (pin-pricks) are sometimes harder to bear than great storms. She read Pauline's note after 9 p.m. with no light.
- When / where: 5–9 July 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Agnès de Jésus.
- Source: LT 55 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 5–9 Jul 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-55-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-5-9-juillet-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « C'est sa faiblesse qui fait toute sa confiance, il ne saurait se briser puisque, quelque chose qui lui arrive, il ne veut voir que la douce main de son Jésus... »
- Translation: "It is its weakness that makes all its trust; it cannot break, because whatever happens to it, it wants to see only the gentle hand of its Jesus..."
- Other sources: 1911 prints an edited version (next card).
- New to English readers: partial — edited version in Taylor.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Early (1888) seed of the "weakness/trust" theme of 1895–97.

### F-OW-384 — Edit: "weakness makes all its trust" became "weakness makes all my strength"
- Claim: 1911 prints LT 55 as "Lettre IIIe" to Mère Agnès, dated "1889". It changes "C'est sa faiblesse qui fait toute sa confiance" (its weakness makes all its trust) to "C'est ma faiblesse qui fait toute ma force" (my weakness makes all my strength). It adds a sentence not in LT 55: "A toutes les extases, je préfère le sacrifice. C'est là qu'est le bonheur pour moi" (compare LT 106 of May 1890: "je n'ai pas envie d'aller à Lourdes pour avoir des extases"). It adds "de l'amour" to "les eaux". It drops the remark that small gusts are worse than great storms.
- When / where: letter July 1888; 1911 print.
- People: Sr Agnès (recipient and later editor).
- Source: LT 55, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-55-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-5-9-juillet-1888/ ; Histoire d'une âme 1911, "Lettres à la Rde Mère Agnès", Lettre IIIe.
- Source type: own-writing vs edited text
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: LT 55: « C'est sa faiblesse qui fait toute sa confiance » ; 1911: « C'est ma faiblesse qui fait toute ma force. »
- Translation: LT 55: "Its weakness makes all its trust." / 1911: "My weakness makes all my strength."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: A meaningful change: "trust" (confiance) is Thérèse's key word; the edit replaced it with "strength".

### F-OW-385 — "I dreamed that the lamb would soon fly away": fear that Pauline would die
- Claim: Thérèse begs Pauline ("l'agneau") not to "leap to Heaven" yet but to wait until the "agnelet" can leap too. "I dreamed that the lamb would soon fly away to its homeland, but I hope it will stay a little longer in exile to guide the poor little lamb." In January 1889 she again writes that Pauline has a bad headache and she fears Jesus will "make wings grow" on her: "Don't die yet."
- When / where: 11 July 1888 and 8 January 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Agnès de Jésus.
- Source: LT 56 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 11 Jul 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-56-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-11-juillet-1888/ ; LT 78 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 8 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-78-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-8-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « J'ai rêvé que l'agneau s'envolerait bientôt dans sa patrie, mais j'espère qu'il restera encore encore un peu dans l'exil pour guider le pauvre agnelet. » ; « Ne mourez pas encore tout de suite, attendez que l'agnelet ait des ailes pour vous suivre... »
- Translation: "I dreamed that the lamb would soon fly off to its homeland, but I hope it will stay a little, a little longer in exile to guide the poor little lamb." / "Don't die yet, right away; wait until the little lamb has wings to follow you..."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for words; the letters do not say what illness Pauline had (only a headache in LT 78).
- Notes: Pauline lived until 1951. The dream is listed in Miracles as a report only.

### F-OW-386 — Céline and the "yellow lily": the one thing that could have separated them
- Claim: Thérèse tells Céline that "only the yellow lily could have separated us a little", but she is sure Céline's lot will always be "a white Lily", since Céline chose it and it chose her first. She wonders why Jesus took her first, and answers with the image of two lilies: one "immortelle" (Céline, strong), one weak (Thérèse), which Jesus took before it opened. She adds that the Prioress brought her Céline's letter at 6 a.m.: "Jesus is good to have let me meet a mother like the one we have."
- When / where: Monday 23 July 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Mère Marie de Gonzague (prioress).
- Source: LT 57 (to Céline, 23 Jul 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-57-a-celine-23-juillet-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Tu sais, il n'y a que le lis jaune qui aurait pu nous éloigner un peu [...] L'un était faible et l'autre était fort, Jésus a pris le faible [...] Jésus est bon de m'avoir fait rencontrer une mère comme celle que nous avons. Quel trésor, petite sœur, si tu l'avais vue ce matin à 6 heures m'apporter ta lettre »
- Translation: "You know, only the yellow lily could have separated us a little [...] One was weak and the other strong; Jesus took the weak one [...] Jesus is good to have let me meet a mother like the one we have. What a treasure, little sister — if you had seen her this morning at 6 o'clock bringing me your letter."
- New to English readers: yes — "lis jaune" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: medium on the meaning of "lis jaune" — the letter implies marriage (not virginity) but does not say so directly.
- Notes: Praise of Mère Marie de Gonzague in July 1888 contrasts with Ms A's later account of the prioress's severity and with LT 78 (Jan 1889). See Gaps.

### F-OW-387 — Papa's carp: "I have never eaten so much as since I came to Carmel"
- Claim: Her father sent a huge carp, "your monster". Dinner was delayed half an hour; Marie du Sacré-Cœur made the sauce; "it tasted of worldly cooking", better even than the lavish Italian meals of the pilgrimage. "I have never eaten so much as since I have been in Carmel." She feels "entirely in my element"; "if Mlle Pauline were here, she would say: 'I have found my way'." Marie ("your Diamond") cannot write because she is at "the big wash". She recalls her father's saying "Vanity of vanities". It will be four months in a week since she entered; it feels both like always and like yesterday.
- When / where: 31 July 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Louis Martin; Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (cook of the sauce; at the laundry); "Mlle Pauline" (a family acquaintance, not Sr Agnès).
- Source: LT 58 (to Louis Martin, 31 Jul 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-58-a-m-martin-31-juillet-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Si tu savais comme ta carpe, ton monstre nous a fait de plaisir ! le dîner en a été retardé d'une 1/2 heure, c'est Marie du Sacré Cœur qui a fait la sauce, c'était délicieux, cela sentait la cuisine du monde. [...] je n'ai jamais tant mangé que depuis que je suis au Carmel. »
- Translation: "If you knew how much pleasure your carp, your monster, gave us! Dinner was delayed half an hour because of it. Marie du Sacré-Cœur made the sauce; it was delicious; it tasted of cooking in the world. [...] I have never eaten so much as since I have been in Carmel."
- New to English readers: yes — "carpe" and "jamais tant mangé" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Food and appetite in the postulancy; contrast with LT 69 (she is made to eat and warm her feet). "Mlle Pauline" is probably Pauline Romet of Alençon (LT 83 also mentions "Mlle Pauline"); the letter does not give a surname.

### F-OW-388 — Death of M. David, the Guérins' relative
- Claim: Mme Guérin wrote to the Carmel about the family's sorrows: "ce bon Monsieur David" was dying. Thérèse wrote to her uncle that his whole life is "a perpetual cross" and God acts so only with great saints. The next morning at 6, having learned of M. David's death, she wrote to her aunt: the angels "are astonished that we can call death the beginning of life". The bell cut her letter short; she offered that small sacrifice.
- When / where: 22 and 23 August 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Isidore Guérin; Mme Céline Guérin; M. David (relative of Mme Guérin, not further identified here); Mme Fournet (Mme Guérin's mother).
- Source: LT 59 (to Isidore Guérin, 22 Aug 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-59-a-m-guerin-22-aout-1888/ ; LT 60 (to Mme Guérin, 23 Aug 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-60-a-mme-guerin-23-aout-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « ils s'étonnent que nous puissions appeler mort le commencement de la vie. [...] il faut que je m'arrête car la cloche vient de m'avertir qu'il est temps que je finisse. J'offre ce petit sacrifice à Jésus »
- Translation: "They are amazed that we can call death the beginning of life. [...] I must stop, for the bell has just told me it is time to finish. I offer this little sacrifice to Jesus."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for words; M. David's link to the family (inheritance of La Musse) is not in these letters.
- Notes: She presents M. David's good disposition as an answer to the aunt's prayers (see Miracles, as report).

### F-OW-389 — A painted picture for her father's feast; "in Rome they knew the Queen was there"
- Claim: For 25 August 1888 Thérèse painted a small picture for her father. "The fine pearl" (Pauline) gave her artist's advice and drew the design, but made Thérèse paint it alone. She says her title "Queen of France and Navarre" is recognized "even abroad: in Italy, in Rome, they knew the Queen was there".
- When / where: 25 August 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Louis Martin; Sr Agnès (drew the design); Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur.
- Source: LT 61 (to Louis Martin, 25 Aug 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-61-a-m-martin-25-aout-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « la perle fine a bien voulu m'aider de ses conseils d'artiste et m'a formé le charmant dessin, mais elle a voulu que je la peigne toute seule »
- Translation: "The fine pearl kindly helped me with her artist's advice and drew the charming design for me, but she wanted me to paint it all by myself."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Early evidence of Thérèse painting in the Carmel under Pauline's guidance.

### F-OW-390 — A worldly visitor at the grille, and moss for Christmas cribs
- Claim: Thérèse received a visit in the parlour from "a fine lady of the WORLD", her husband, a girl of 16 and a boy of 14 — "the godmother who planted verbenas", with her niece Th. Gilbert and nephew Pierre. "It's the world indeed! If you had seen her in the parlour, she was almost singing 'Que mon cœur, que mon cœur a de peine' when she saw us behind the grille." She asks Marie Guérin to pick up dry moss and tree bark in her park, "to make little works, like cribs". She writes by the light of Marie's lamp. She sends a kiss "so hard that if you had a gland, it would be pierced as before the Rome journey".
- When / where: a Thursday in September 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Marie Guérin; the visiting lady ("la marraine qui plantait des verveines", not named), Th. Gilbert and Pierre; Mme Guérin (ill); Jeanne Guérin; Céline and Hélène (the letter gives first names only).
- Source: LT 62 (to Marie Guérin, Sept 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-62-a-marie-guerin-septembre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « si tu l'avais vue au parloir, elle chantait presque «Que mon coeur, que mon coeur a de peine» en nous voyant derrière la grille. [...] quelques petites mousses sèches, des écorces d'arbre, etc. Ce serait pour faire des petits ouvrages, comme des crèches »
- Translation: "If you had seen her in the parlour, she was almost singing 'How my heart, how my heart is grieved' when she saw us behind the grille. [...] some little bits of dry moss, tree bark, etc. It would be for making little works, such as cribs."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for words; the visitor's identity is only a nickname.
- Notes: Shows her amused view of worldly pity for nuns. The "glande ... avant le voyage à Rome" suggests Marie Guérin had a gland problem (swelling) before Nov 1887; not explained.

### F-OW-391 — Alençon lace for the clothing, and an "avalanche" of fruit and onions through the turn
- Claim: Her father sent Alençon point lace ("truly Royal") for her clothing. She would not dare wear such riches herself, but they are not too fine for the divine Spouse. She is still only "the Orphan of the Beresina" and will deserve her title of Queen only on her clothing day. He also sent "an avalanche of pears, onions, plums, apples", which came out of the turn "as from a horn of plenty"; an old fellow said it was from "a gentleman living near the Étoile garden". "The funny thing: it had less trouble getting in than your Queen, who had to go to Rome to have the door opened." The huge onions made her think of those of Egypt, and of the big Lyon onions that cost 0.50.
- When / where: 30 September 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Louis Martin; Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Sr Agnès; an unnamed old man who delivered the goods.
- Source: LT 63 (to Louis Martin, 30 Sep 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-63-a-m-martin-30-septembre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « D'abord c'est du point d'Alençon que j'ai vu arriver. [...] une avalanche de poires, d'oignons, de prunes, de pommes, qui est sortie du tour, comme d'une corne d'abondance. [...] La drôle de chose, elle a eu moins de mal à entrer que ta Reine, qui a été obligée d'aller à Rome pour se faire ouvrir la porte... »
- Translation: "First, it was Alençon point lace that I saw arrive. [...] an avalanche of pears, onions, plums and apples that came out of the turn like a horn of plenty. [...] Funny thing: it had less trouble getting in than your Queen, who had to go to Rome to get the door opened..."
- Other sources: 1911 (biographical note) mentions that Zélie made point d'Alençon lace.
- New to English readers: yes — "oignons" ABSENT from 1911; the lace gift for the clothing not in baseline.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: The lace was probably her mother's craft, but the letter does not say who made it. The "Rome" joke shows humour about her own struggle to enter.

### F-OW-392 — Talks at the belvedere windows; Fr Pichon leaves for Canada
- Claim: For Céline's feast Thérèse recalls their talks "at the windows of the belvedere" (the top room of the Buissonnets): "we are still the same soul". She quotes a saying: "Resignation is still distinct from God's will; there is the same difference as between union and unity. In union there are still two; in unity there is only one." She calls herself "too silly" to explain a thought. Postscript: "Oh yes, it is very sad to think that the Father is leaving for Canada, but Jesus remains to us!"
- When / where: 20 October 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Fr Almire Pichon SJ ("le Père").
- Source: LT 65 (to Céline, 20 Oct 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-65-a-celine-20-octobre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « nous sommes toujours bien la même âme, et nos pensées sont toujours les mêmes qu'elles étaient aux fenêtres du belvédère... [...] Oh ! oui, cela est bien triste de penser que le Père s'en va au Canada, mais Jésus nous reste !... »
- Translation: "We are still the same soul, and our thoughts are still the same as they were at the windows of the belvedere... [...] Oh yes, it is very sad to think that the Father is going to Canada, but Jesus remains to us!..."
- Other sources: Ms A (1911) mentions Pichon's departure for Canada; the belvedere talks are in Ms A (Taylor). 1911 splits this letter across its "Lettre Ire" and "Lettre IIe" and moves the union/unity saying into "Lettre IIIe (Janvier 1889)".
- New to English readers: partial.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: The "cognée à la racine / au pied de l'arbre" and "Jesus does not look so much at the greatness of actions ... as at the love" passage is in this letter (Oct 1888), an early statement of her later teaching.

### F-OW-393 — Her father's first serious illness in the Carmel period (autumn 1888)
- Claim: Thérèse thanks God for having cured her father: "you were very ill". "The whole Carmel was in prayer." She begs him to take good care of himself, and says "l'Intrépide N° 2" knows about nursing, by a title he himself gave. On 25 November she writes that she wishes only to see him "entirely cured", and that God sends this trial so that they may win Heaven. To her former school mistress (early Dec) she writes she feared for some days that God would take him, but Jesus restored him "for my clothing".
- When / where: 15 and 25 November, early December 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Louis Martin; "l'Intrépide N° 2" (a family nickname, probably Céline, who nursed him; not stated); the Carmel community; Mère Saint-Placide.
- Source: LT 66 (to Louis Martin, 15 Nov 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-66-a-m-martin-15-novembre-1888/ ; LT 68 (to Louis Martin, 25 Nov 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-68-a-m-martin-25-novembre-1888/ ; LT 70 (to Mère Saint-Placide, early Dec 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-70-a-mere-saint-placide-debut-decembre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Que le bon Dieu est donc bon de t'avoir guéri ! Je t'assure que ta petite Reine a été bien tourmentée et vraiment il y avait bien de quoi car tu as été très malade. Tout le Carmel était en prière » ; LT 70: « j'ai craint pendant quelques jours que le bon Dieu ne le ravisse à ma tendresse »
- Translation: "How good God is to have cured you! I assure you your little Queen was very worried, and truly there was reason, for you were very ill. The whole Carmel was praying." / "For some days I feared that God would take him from my love."
- New to English readers: yes — the letters are ABSENT from 1911 ("Intrépide").
- Confidence: high that he was seriously ill; the nature of the illness is not stated in these letters.
- Notes: The letters do not mention his June 1888 disappearance to Le Havre. See Gaps.

### F-OW-394 — Fur-lined boots, and "they look after you so well in Carmel"
- Claim: Sr Agnès told Thérèse she needs a pair of fur-lined shoes, "a kind of little boots edged with astrakhan", like Marie Guérin's winter ones. She asks if her aunt will buy them; Jeanne has exactly the same foot and can try them on. She wants them "this afternoon" if possible. "You cannot imagine how well looked after one is in Carmel; I always have to eat and warm my feet." She is "at the height of my desires". She calls her cousin "Ma belle Poupée".
- When / where: November 1888 (date uncertain), Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Marie Guérin; Sr Agnès; Mme Guérin; Jeanne Guérin (same shoe size); Isidore Guérin; Marcelline (named in a P.S.).
- Source: LT 69 (to Marie Guérin, Nov 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-69-a-marie-guerin-novembre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Sr Agnès vient de me dire qu'il me faut une paire de chaussures fourrées [...] ce sont des espèces de petites bottines qui sont bordées d'astrakan. [...] Jeanne pourrait les essayer, elle a exactement le même pied que moi. [...] Tu ne peux pas te figurer combien on est bien soigné au Carmel, il faut toujours que je mange et que je me chauffe les pieds... »
- Translation: "Sr Agnès has just told me I need a pair of fur-lined shoes [...] a sort of little ankle boots edged with astrakhan. [...] Jeanne could try them on; she has exactly the same foot as me. [...] You can't imagine how well cared for one is in Carmel; I am always made to eat and warm my feet..."
- New to English readers: yes — "astrakan" and "chauffe les pieds" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high for words.
- Notes: This cheerful claim (for family readers) sits in tension with later accounts of cold and severity in the Carmel. The request suggests her health was watched in her first winter; the letter gives no symptoms.

### F-OW-395 — To her old teacher: the Children of Mary and the "germ" of her vocation
- Claim: Invited by Mère Saint-Placide to a feast of the Children of Mary (Enfants de Marie) at the Benedictine abbey school, Thérèse writes that in that chapel the Virgin "adopted me as her child" on her First Communion day and on her reception into the Children of Mary. She believes her religious vocation took its "germ" on that day, when she consecrated herself to Mary after receiving Jesus for the first time. She was waiting to tell of her acceptance "au chapitre" (the community's vote for her clothing) until the bishop fixed the date. She sends respects to "Madame la Prieure".
- When / where: early December 1888, Lisieux Carmel; school = Benedictine abbey of Notre-Dame-du-Pré, Lisieux (not named in letter).
- People: Mère Saint-Placide (her former mistress at the abbey school); the Benedictine prioress ("Madame la Prieure"); her former classmates.
- Source: LT 70 (to Mère Saint-Placide, early Dec 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-70-a-mere-saint-placide-debut-decembre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « je ne puis douter que la grâce insigne de ma vocation religieuse n'ait pris son germe dans cet heureux jour où entourée de mes bonnes Maîtresses, j'ai fait à Marie la consécration de moi-même au pied de son autel »
- Translation: "I cannot doubt that the great grace of my religious vocation took its seed on that happy day when, surrounded by my good Mistresses, I consecrated myself to Mary at the foot of her altar."
- Other sources: Ms A (1911) is cooler about her school years (bullying at school is in baseline). This letter is formal and grateful.
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for words; the formal tone suits a letter to a former teacher.
- Notes: Date of clothing still unknown in early December 1888; it depended on the bishop.

### F-OW-396 — "I slept like a lazybones"; writing in time taken from the Office
- Claim: Thérèse did not know her sisters were writing to her aunt the evening before, and "slept like a lazybones". She writes in the morning, taking the time "from the Office". Nearly nine months in Carmel; "already 16 years on earth". She signs herself "your littlest Benjamin". Postscript: she forgot to thank her aunt for the crown she is giving for her clothing.
- When / where: 28 December 1888, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Mme Guérin; Isidore Guérin; her cousins.
- Source: LT 71 (to Mme Guérin, 28 Dec 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-71-a-mme-guerin-28-decembre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « hier soir, ne sachant pas que mes sœurs allaient vous écrire, j'ai dormi comme une paresseuse... Ce matin je n'ai que bien peu de temps, et encore faut-il le prendre sur l'office. »
- Translation: "Last night, not knowing my sisters were going to write to you, I slept like a lazybones... This morning I have very little time, and even that must be taken from the Office."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: She says she is "too silly to speak to two people at once" (so wrote only to her aunt).

### F-OW-397 — Gifts for the clothing: a lily crown, a giant sugar stick, gingerbread for her 16th birthday
- Claim: Her cousins gave the lilies for her clothing crown; they look "just picked". She was happy to have "the enormous sugar stick" (sucre de pomme) to offer to "our Mother". The big packet of gingerbread arrived; "I was very proud in the refectory when our mother told the Community that in honour of my 16 years you were giving us this treat." The Mother and the community find the crown very beautiful. She prays to be adorned inside as well as outside.
- When / where: 2 January 1889 (her 16th birthday), Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Mme Guérin; Isidore Guérin; Jeanne and Marie Guérin; Mère Marie de Gonzague ("notre Mère"); the community.
- Source: LT 73 (to Mme Guérin, 2 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-73-a-mme-guerin-2-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Si vous saviez comme j'ai été heureuse d'avoir l'énorme sucre de pomme à offrir à notre Mère [...] j'ai été toute fière au réfectoire quand notre mère a dit à la Communauté qu'en l'honneur de mes 16 ans vous nous donniez ce régal. »
- Translation: "If you knew how happy I was to have the enormous apple-sugar stick to offer to our Mother [...] I was quite proud in the refectory when our mother told the Community that you were giving us this treat in honour of my 16 years."
- New to English readers: yes — "sucre de pomme" and "pain d'épices" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: "Sucre de pomme" is a Rouen sweet (sugar stick); the letter does not say more.

### F-OW-398 — Clothing retreat: "the creatures... there is something that repels me"
- Claim: In her clothing retreat, Thérèse tells Pauline that Jesus "riddles me with pin-pricks"; the little ball has little holes everywhere, which hurt more than one big one. With Jesus: "nothing, dryness! sleep!" But: "the creatures, oh! the creatures!... The ones around me are very good, but there is something that repels me." She is happy that Jesus sleeps: "he does not go to any trouble to make conversation with me". On her betrothal day she wants to "convert all the sinners on earth and save all the souls in purgatory"; "I know it is madness."
- When / where: 6 January 1889, Lisieux Carmel (retreat before the clothing of 10 January).
- People: Sr Agnès de Jésus; the community ("les créatures").
- Source: LT 74 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 6 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-74-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-6-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Mais les créatures !... Celles qui m'entourent sont bien bonnes, mais il y a je ne sais quoi qui me repousse !... [...] je vous assure qu'il ne fait pas de frais pour me tenir conversation !... [...] je voudrais convertir tous les pécheurs de la terre et sauver toutes les âmes du purgatoire !... »
- Translation: "But the creatures! The ones around me are very good, but there is something I don't know what that repels me!... [...] I assure you he goes to no trouble to keep up a conversation with me!... [...] I would like to convert all the sinners on earth and save all the souls in purgatory!..."
- Other sources: 1911 prints an edited merge of LT 74 and LT 76 (next card).
- New to English readers: partial — edited text in Taylor; the "repels me" line is new.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Honest admission of difficulty with the community in her first year.

### F-OW-399 — Edit: the clothing-retreat letters in 1911 — the community complaint removed
- Claim: 1911 "Lettre IIe (Janvier 1889)" to Mère Agnès merges LT 74 and LT 76. It removes "Celles qui m'entourent sont bien bonnes, mais il y a je ne sais quoi qui me repousse" and replaces it with a neutral line: "Quelle différence avec celle des créatures!" (What a difference from the hand of creatures!). It also removes: her pain at Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul's (LT 76), the postponed date of 9 January, the remark on "la lettre du Père", and her plea "Je vous EN PRIE, déchirez ces papiers après les avoir lus!" (Please tear up these papers after reading them).

- Source type: own-writing vs edited composite
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: LT 76: « Je vous EN PRIE, déchirez ces papiers après les avoir lus !... »
- Translation: "I BEG you, tear up these papers after reading them!..."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Pauline kept and later published papers Thérèse asked her to destroy.

### F-OW-400 — Pain at Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul's; the clothing date moved from the 9th
- Claim: "This morning I had some pain at Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul's; I left with a heavy heart." She notes Jesus changed the date: "He no doubt found that the date of the 9th was too delightful; he wants nothing delightful for her." (Her entry had been on 9 April; the clothing was set for 10 January.) She is "deprived of all consolation" but has great peace. "The letter from the Father" (Fr Pichon) is heavenly, "but happiness? ... happiness is only in suffering, and in suffering without any consolation".
- When / where: 7 January 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Agnès; Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul (Carmelite, not described further); Fr Pichon.
- Source: LT 76 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 7 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-76-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-7-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Ce matin j'ai eu de la peine chez ma Sœur St Vincent de Paul, je me suis en allée le cœur bien gros... [...] Il a sans doute trouvé que la date du 9 était trop ravissante »
- Translation: "This morning I was hurt at Sr St Vincent de Paul's; I went away with a very heavy heart... [...] He no doubt found the date of the 9th too delightful."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for words; the letter does not say what happened at Sr St Vincent de Paul's (she may have been in charge of a work Thérèse helped with — not stated).
- Notes: A concrete, unexplained hurt from a named sister during the retreat.

### F-OW-401 — "At our Mother's I am constantly interrupted"
- Claim: During the retreat Thérèse tells Pauline that at the Prioress's (for spiritual direction), "I am constantly interrupted, and when I have a moment, I cannot tell her what is happening in my soul; I leave without joy, having come in without joy". She is "plunged in darkness" — perhaps "luminous darkness, but darkness all the same". Postscript requests: Pauline's Indian ink and some gold; whether St Agnes's responses would suit her little clothing holy-cards; and to open "our door" at 6 a.m. if she is there, "otherwise I will wake up by myself".
- When / where: 8 January 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Agnès; Mère Marie de Gonzague ("Notre Mère").
- Source: LT 78 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 8 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-78-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-8-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Chez Notre Mère, je suis continuellement dérangée, et puis quand j'ai un instant, je ne puis lui dire ce qui se passe dans mon âme, je m'en vais sans joie, après être entrée sans joie !... » ; « Voulez-vous s'il vous plaît me donner : 1° votre encre de Chine et de l'or ; 2° me dire si pour mes petites images de prise d'habit les répons de Ste Agnès conviennent ; 3° entr'ouvrir notre porte à 6h »
- Translation: "At our Mother's I am constantly interrupted, and when I do have a moment I can't tell her what is going on in my soul; I leave without joy, having gone in without joy!..." / "Would you please give me: 1. your Indian ink and some gold; 2. tell me whether St Agnes's responses are right for my little clothing holy cards; 3. half-open our door at 6 o'clock."
- Other sources: Ms A (1911) speaks of the prioress's severity and of her difficulty opening her soul.
- New to English readers: yes — "entrée sans joie" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: She made her own clothing holy cards (with ink and gold). Contrast with LT 57's praise of "notre Mère".

### F-OW-402 — A melon, champagne and fireworks for the clothing
- Claim: Her father sent a melon and champagne; "it would make me want to cry if I didn't hold back". For the "wedding" of the Queen of France and Navarre there will be fireworks, paid by the King. "The little blond cockchafer has only to say thank you." Writing is not allowed in retreat, but "our Mother" allowed this note; "can one trouble one's peace by writing to a Saint?" She signs "La Reine de France et de toutes les Navarre".
- When / where: 8 January 1889, Lisieux Carmel; clothing on Thursday 10 January 1889.
- People: Louis Martin; Mère Marie de Gonzague.
- Source: LT 77 (to Louis Martin, 8 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-77-a-m-martin-8-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Un melon !... du champagne, oh ! cela me donnerait bien envie de pleurer si je ne me retenais pas [...] c'est sans doute pour cela que la Reine de France et de Navarre aura un feu d'artifice... C'est le Roi qui fait les frais pour La Reine [...] le petit hanneton blond n'a qu'à remercier !... »
- Translation: "A melon!... champagne! Oh, it would make me want to cry if I didn't hold back [...] no doubt that is why the Queen of France and Navarre will have fireworks... It's the King who pays for the Queen [...] the little blond cockchafer has only to say thank you!..."
- New to English readers: yes — "melon", "hanneton" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high for words; that the fireworks actually happened is not confirmed in these letters.
- Notes: Her father was well enough to attend the clothing (LT 70); the letters do not describe the day itself.

### F-OW-403 — First signature "of the Holy Face", to a fellow novice
- Claim: On her clothing day she gave a keepsake to Sr Marthe de Jésus, her "dear little companion", whose clothing was coming: "Soon the divine Spouse of Thérèse of the Child Jesus will also be that of Sr Marthe of Jesus! Ask Jesus that I become a great saint; I will ask the same grace for my dear little companion." She signs "Sœur Thérèse de l'Enfant Jésus de la Sainte Face. nov.carm.ind."
- When / where: 10 January 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Marthe de Jésus (fellow novice, lay sister; the letter says only "compagne").

- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Demandez à Jésus que je devienne une grande sainte, je demanderai la même grâce pour ma chère petite compagne ! »
- Translation: "Ask Jesus that I may become a great saint; I will ask the same grace for my dear little companion!"
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: In these letters the full name "de la Sainte Face" first appears on 10 Jan 1889 (LT 80), then regularly from March 1889. Her clothing (10 Jan 1889) is baseline.

### F-OW-404 — To Céline during the father's crisis: "You are not a Soldier but a General"
- Claim: With their father's illness weighing on the family, Thérèse tells Céline that Jesus fell three times on the way to Calvary, so Céline may fall "100 times". She is "almost jealous" of Céline's trial. Céline feels no love, but "the smoke is only for you". "What do the things of this earth matter to us... what does it matter that paltry men cut the moulds growing on this mud?" "To suffer and be despised!... That is the motto of the Lily-Immortelle." "You are not a Soldier but a General."
- When / where: 23–25 January 1889 (date uncertain), Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline (caring for their father).
- Source: LT 81 (to Céline, 23–25 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-81-a-celine-23-25-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Sais-tu bien que j'en suis presque jalouse ? [...] Souffrir et être méprisé ! quelle amertume mais quelle gloire ! Voilà la devise du Lys-immortelle !... [...] Tu n'es pas Soldat mais Général... »
- Translation: "Do you know, I am almost jealous of it? [...] To suffer and be despised! What bitterness, but what glory! That is the motto of the Lily-Immortelle!... [...] You are not a Soldier but a General..."
- Other sources: 1911 "Lettre IIIe (Janvier 1889)" uses the start of LT 81 only.
- New to English readers: partial — first lines in Taylor; "Général" and the "moisissures" image are new.
- Confidence: medium on context — the letter implies people were talking against the family ("chétifs hommes", "méprisé") but gives no facts.
- Notes: Reactions: suggests public contempt around the father's illness; details not given in this letter.

### F-OW-405 — "Is it possible that I am writing to you in Caen?" — the father at Caen
- Claim: Thérèse writes to Céline at Caen, where their father has been taken: "I wonder if I am dreaming or awake... But no, it is a reality!" She says she does not pity Céline; Céline's lot is enviable; Jesus himself is giving her "first novitiate", a novitiate "of sorrow". "Jesus is a spouse of blood." "Don't you find that the misfortune striking him is quite the completion of his beautiful life?" "Life is only a dream." The trial is "a gold mine to exploit". "It is Martyrdom beginning."
- When / where: 28 February 1889, Lisieux Carmel; Céline (and Léonie) in Caen near their father.
- People: Céline; Louis Martin.
- Source: LT 82 (to Céline, 28 Feb 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-82-a-celine-28-fevrier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Est-il possible que ce soit à Caen que je t'écrive ?... Je me demande si je rêve ou si je suis éveillée... [...] mais ne trouves-tu pas que le malheur qui le frappe est tout à fait le complément de sa belle vie ?... »
- Translation: "Is it possible that I am writing to you in Caen?... I wonder whether I am dreaming or awake... [...] but don't you find that the misfortune striking him is quite the crowning of his beautiful life?..."
- Other sources: baseline does not name the Bon-Sauveur; the letter does not name it either — only "Caen".
- New to English readers: partial — edited version in Taylor; the Caen opening and the "complement" sentence are cut there.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: The letter does not name the asylum (Bon-Sauveur) nor describe his illness. See Gaps.

### F-OW-407 — A lily by their father; "Poor Léonie... Jesus gave her less"
- Claim: Thérèse thanks Jesus for placing "a lily near our dear father", a lily "that would rather die than abandon the glorious field" — Céline staying at Caen. "Now we have nothing more to hope for on earth, nothing but suffering." The real purpose of the letter comes at the end: Céline should write to "Mlle Pauline" about "the misfortune that has struck us in Papa's illness". "Poor Léonie, I love her too; she is unhappier than we are; Jesus gave her less. But of those to whom much is given, much will be asked."

- People: Céline; Léonie; Louis Martin; "Mlle Pauline" (family acquaintance).

- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Pauvre Léonie, je l'aime bien aussi, elle est plus malheureuse que nous, Jésus lui a moins donné. »
- Translation: "Poor Léonie, I love her very much too; she is unhappier than we are; Jesus has given her less."
- Other sources: 1911 merges part of LT 83 into "Lettre IIIe (Janvier 1889)" and changes "Les chérubins" to "Les Séraphins"; the Léonie sentence is ABSENT from 1911.
- New to English readers: yes — the Léonie line is not in 1911 or baseline.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: A frank, somewhat condescending judgement about Léonie. Also a sign of self-mockery: "Laugh in your turn at your poor Thérèse, who reaches her subject at the end of her letter."

### F-OW-408 — Money from her aunt: "instead of earning money I only know how to lose it"
- Claim: Her aunt forbade her to say thank you; Thérèse cannot obey. She was astonished to find herself suddenly "so rich" without having earned anything. "It is true, alas, that I am incapable of doing anything well; instead of earning money I only know how to lose it." She smiles that thanks to her relatives "it is I who will supply the fish for the whole Community". She sends a kiss to Jeanne and to "ma petite maîtresse de maison" (little housekeeper).
- When / where: 12 March 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Mme Guérin; Isidore Guérin; Jeanne Guérin; "ma petite maîtresse de maison" (probably Marie Guérin; not stated).
