- Source: LT 84 (to Mme Guérin, 12 Mar 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-84-a-mme-guerin-12-mars-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « au lieu de gagner de l'argent je ne sais que le perdre [...] Je ne puis m'empêcher de sourire en pensant que grâce à mes bons parents, c'est moi qui vais fournir le poisson de la Communauté tout entière... »
- Translation: "Instead of earning money I only know how to lose it [...] I can't help smiling at the thought that, thanks to my good relatives, I am the one who will supply fish for the whole Community..."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for words; the amount and purpose are not stated (the letter implies the gift paid for fish for the community, perhaps in Lent).
- Notes: Money detail; the Guérins supported the Martin daughters during the father's illness ("la consolation de voir notre douleur partagée").

### F-OW-409 — "Jesus has done mad things for Céline; let Céline do mad things for Jesus"
- Claim: In the evening Thérèse writes to Céline: "here below everything tires me"; her only joy is to suffer for Jesus, "a joy not felt". "Jesus has done mad things for Céline... Let Céline do mad things for Jesus... Love is repaid only by love." Jesus makes the salvation of souls depend "on a sigh of our heart". The bell rings before she can write to Léonie.
- When / where: 12 March 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Léonie.
- Source: LT 85 (to Céline, 12 Mar 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-85-a-celine-12-mars-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Jésus a fait des folies pour Céline... Que Céline fasse des folies pour Jésus... L'amour ne se paie que par l'amour et les plaies de l'amour ne se guérissent que par l'amour. »
- Translation: "Jesus has done foolish things for Céline... Let Céline do foolish things for Jesus... Love is repaid only by love, and the wounds of love are healed only by love."
- Other sources: 1911 "Lettre Ve (12 mars 1889)" prints the first part and cuts this passage.
- New to English readers: partial — part in Taylor; "folies pour Jésus" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: "L'amour ne se paie que par l'amour" is a phrase of St John of the Cross in spirit; the letter gives no source.

### F-OW-410 — Céline as "the little shadow of Jesus"; "before dying by the sword, let us die by pin-pricks"
- Claim: Céline had written that she was Thérèse's "shadow". Thérèse answers that the shadow of a grain of sand would be sad; Céline must be "the little shadow of Jesus". Céline's "dream" (unexplained) may one day come true, "but meanwhile let us begin our martyrdom... Before dying by the sword, let us die by pin-pricks."
- When / where: 15 March 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline.
- Source: LT 86 (to Céline, 15 Mar 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-86-a-celine-15-mars-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Avant de mourir par le glaive, mourons à coup d'épingles... Céline comprend-elle ?... »
- Translation: "Before dying by the sword, let us die by pin-pricks... Does Céline understand?..."
- New to English readers: yes — ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high for words; "le rêve de ma Céline" is not explained (possibly martyrdom, given the context).
- Notes: None.

### F-OW-411 — "Your letter put great sadness in my soul... Poor little Father"; the Holy Face
- Claim: News from Céline about their father saddened Thérèse. "Let us see life in its true light... It is a moment between two eternities." She found "the secret of suffering in peace": peace is not felt joy; it is enough to will all that Jesus wills. She copies Psalm 136 (the harps on the willows) and ends: "Look at his adorable Face!... Look at those dimmed and lowered eyes!... look at those wounds... Look at Jesus in his Face... There you will see how he loves us."
- When / where: 4 April 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Louis Martin.
- Source: LT 87 (to Céline, 4 Apr 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-87-a-celine-4-avril-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Ta lettre a mis une grande tristesse dans mon âme !... Pauvre petit Père !... [...] Regarde Jésus dans sa Face... Là tu verras comme il nous aime. »
- Translation: "Your letter has put great sadness in my soul!... Poor little Father!... [...] Look at Jesus in his Face... There you will see how he loves us."
- Other sources: 1911 moves the "peace" passage into "Lettre Ve (12 mars 1889)"; the opening about the father is ABSENT from 1911.
- New to English readers: partial.
- Confidence: high for words; the letter does not say what news Céline sent.
- Notes: The Holy Face devotion grows in these letters exactly during the father's confinement (from Jan 1889).

### F-OW-412 — The Buissonnets "now deserted": asking for her First Communion books
- Claim: Thérèse asks Marie Guérin to fetch from the Buissonnets, "which now, alas, are deserted", a book Mme Tifenne gave her at First Communion, "Le bouquet de la jeune fille", which should be in one of the drawers "of poor little Father's chest of drawers", and a smaller brown book with a gold border (meditations on the Eucharist) given by the Misses Primois, on a shelf of the cupboard in Céline's room, by the door. Marie might explain to "la bonne" (the maid) instead of going herself. She thanks the family for Easter chocolate and fish: "my uncle had something so PATERNAL that day... I will never forget that parlour."
- When / where: Wednesday 24 April 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Marie Guérin; Mme Tifenne; the Misses Primois; Isidore Guérin; the maid of the Buissonnets; Céline and Léonie ("nos pauvres petites sœurs de là-bas", at Caen).
- Source: LT 88 (to Marie Guérin, 24 Apr 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-88-a-marie-guerin-24-avril-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « les Buissonnets qui maintenant, hélas ! sont déserts [...] Ce livre doit se trouver dans un des tiroirs de la commode à ce pauvre petit Père [...] mon oncle avait quelque chose de si PATERNEL ce jour-là, quelque chose de pas ordinaire !... jamais je n'oublierai ce parloir. »
- Translation: "The Buissonnets, which now, alas, are deserted [...] That book must be in one of the drawers of poor little Father's chest of drawers [...] my uncle had something so FATHERLY about him that day, something out of the ordinary!... I will never forget that parlour."
- New to English readers: yes — "bouquet de la jeune fille", "Tifenne" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Precise memory of her home's furniture. The empty house dates the break-up of the family home to spring 1889.

### F-OW-413 — Céline's 20th birthday: "his reason is that he has no reason"
- Claim: For Céline's 20th birthday Thérèse writes: Jesus has fruits in his garden that ripen "almost in the blink of an eye"; why are we among them? "His reason is that he has no reason!" She quotes St Francis de Sales: "When the fire of love is in a heart, all the furniture flies out of the windows." "Let us suffer bitterly, without courage!" She writes a prayer: "What does it matter, my Jesus, if I fall at every moment; I see my weakness by it... you will be more tempted to carry me in your arms." "If you knew my misery!... Holiness does not consist in saying fine things... it consists in suffering, and suffering from everything."
- When / where: 26 April 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline.
- Source: LT 89 (to Céline, 26 Apr 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-89-a-celine-26-avril-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Quelle raison Jésus peut-il nous donner ? Hélas ! sa raison est qu'il n'a pas de raison !... [...] «Quand le feu de l'amour est dans un cœur tous les meubles volent par les fenêtres.» [...] Si tu connaissais ma misère !... oh ! si tu savais... La Sainteté ne consiste pas à dire de belles choses »
- Translation: "What reason can Jesus give us? Alas, his reason is that he has no reason!... 'When the fire of love is in a heart, all the furniture flies out of the windows.' [...] If you knew my misery!... oh, if you knew... Holiness does not consist in saying fine things."
- Other sources: 1911 moves the "falling" passage into "Lettre Ve (12 mars 1889)"; the "no reason" and furniture lines are ABSENT from 1911.
- New to English readers: partial.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: "Nous voudrions ne jamais tomber? Qu'importe..." is an early form (April 1889) of her acceptance of weakness.

### F-OW-414 — She decorates the Child Jesus of the Carmel; memory for dates
- Claim: Thérèse sends Céline a bouquet "from the little Jesus": "the little Jesus of Carmel is poor"; the bouquet spent hours before him "in a vase even poorer than he". She says, "since I have been in Carmel I have a very good memory for dates" (Léonie's 25th birthday). In October 1889 Céline sent "little pots" (vases) that made the little Jesus "look radiant to be so well adorned", and relatives sent two plants "for the altar of the Child Jesus... the Jesus of Thérèse".
- When / where: 27 April and 15 October 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Léonie; Mme Guérin; an unknown donor of two plants.
- Source: LT 90 (to Céline, 27 Apr 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-90-a-celine-27-avril-1889/ ; LT 96 (to Céline, 15 Oct 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-96-a-celine-15-octobre-1889/ ; LT 97 (to Mme Guérin, 15 Oct 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-97-a-mme-guerin-15-octobre-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « le petit Jésus du Carmel est pauvre [...] (Le bouquet de Jésus a passé plusieurs heures devant lui dans un vase encore plus pauvre que lui !...) [...] depuis que je suis au Carmel j'ai beaucoup de mémoire pour les dates » ; LT 97: « deux magnifiques plantes pour le petit Jésus [...] au Jésus de Thérèse »
- Translation: "The little Jesus of Carmel is poor [...] (Jesus's bouquet spent several hours in front of him in a vase even poorer than he!...) [...] since I have been in Carmel I have a great memory for dates." / "two magnificent plants for the little Jesus [...] for Thérèse's Jesus."
- New to English readers: yes — "petits pots" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high that she decorated a Child Jesus statue/altar; the letters do not call it an official office.
- Notes: Compare LT 98 (22 Oct 1889): she forgot Céline's feast because in Carmel she "never knows what date it is" — a direct contradiction six months later (see next card).

### F-OW-415 — Marie's lessons on the big chair, and Papa's offering: "something better to offer to God"
- Claim: Thérèse reminds Marie of the time when, "seated on the big chair, holding me on your knees, you spoke to me of heaven": "Look at the merchants, how they take trouble to earn money; and we can gather treasures for heaven at every moment... we have only to pick up diamonds with a RAKE." "Perhaps, without you, I would not be in Carmel." Before her entry, "our incomparable Father said, giving me to God: 'I would really like to have something better to offer to God.' Jesus heard his prayer... that something better was himself!" "Papa is God's little child." It will not matter in Heaven that "all the relics of the Buissonnets" have been scattered.
- When / where: end of May 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (Marie, "Lion"); Louis Martin.
- Source: LT 91 (to Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur, end May 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-91-a-soeur-marie-du-sacre-coeur-fin-mai-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « «Regarde les marchands comme ils se donnent du mal pour gagner de l'argent, et nous, nous pouvons amasser des trésors pour le ciel à chaque instant sans nous donner tant de mal, nous n'avons qu'à ramasser des diamants avec un RATEAU.» [...] notre incomparable Père disait en me donnant au bon Dieu : «Je voudrais bien avoir quelque chose de mieux à offrir au bon Dieu.» Jésus a écouté sa prière... ce quelque chose de mieux, c'était lui-même !... »
- Translation: "'Look at the merchants, how hard they work to earn money; and we can gather treasures for heaven at every moment without so much trouble; we only have to pick up diamonds with a RAKE.' [...] Our incomparable Father said, as he gave me to God: 'I would like to have something better to offer to God.' Jesus heard his prayer... that something better was himself!..."
- Other sources: 1911 prints an edited version (next card). Ms A (Taylor) tells of Marie's instruction but not the father's words.
- New to English readers: yes for the father's words (ABSENT from 1911); partial for the rake.
- Confidence: high — though the father's words are reported second-hand by Thérèse.
- Notes: Thérèse reads her father's illness as God accepting his offering of himself.

### F-OW-416 — Edit: Marie's "rake" saying rewritten in 1911, and a moral added
- Claim: 1911 prints LT 91 as "Lettre IIIe" to Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur, dated "14 août 1889". It cuts "assise sur la grande chaise"; changes "Regarde les marchands" to "Regarde ceux qui veulent s'enrichir"; changes "avec un RATEAU" to "comme avec un râteau"; and adds a sentence not in the letter: "Pour cela, il suffit de faire toutes nos actions par amour pour le bon Dieu." It cuts the father's words ("quelque chose de mieux à offrir"), "Papa, c'est le petit enfant du bon Dieu", and the Buissonnets relics. It adds a Revelation quote about tears from LT 68.
- When / where: letter end May 1889; 1911 print.
- People: Marie; Louis Martin.
- Source: LT 91, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-91-a-soeur-marie-du-sacre-coeur-fin-mai-1889/ ; Histoire d'une âme 1911, "Lettres à Sœur Marie du Sacré-Cœur", Lettre IIIe.
- Source type: own-writing vs edited text
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: 1911 (added): « Pour cela, il suffit de faire toutes nos actions par amour pour le bon Dieu. »
- Translation: "For that, it is enough to do all our actions out of love for the good God."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: The added moral makes Marie's childhood lesson sound like Thérèse's later "little way".

### F-OW-417 — Scruples: advising Marie Guérin to go to Communion — "she too passed through the martyrdom of scruple"
- Claim: Marie Guérin, tormented by scruples (temptations she feared were sins), had stopped going to Communion on Ascension day and on the last day of the month of Mary. Thérèse assures her: "You have not done the shadow of wrong." The devil wants to keep Jesus from a loved tabernacle. "Your poor little Thérèse ... too passed through the martyrdom of scruple, but Jesus gave her the grace to receive Communion all the same, even when she thought she had committed great sins." "What offends Jesus, what wounds him to the heart, is lack of trust." "Receive Communion often, very often... that is the only remedy." She dreamed that night of trying to console Marie, in vain. She suggests a candle at Notre-Dame-des-Victoires. She thinks Jesus would be pleased if Marie "made up" her two missed Communions.
- When / where: Thursday 30 May 1889, Lisieux Carmel; Marie Guérin apparently in Paris (candle at Notre-Dame-des-Victoires).
- People: Marie Guérin (cousin, aged 18–19).
- Source: LT 92 (to Marie Guérin, 30 May 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-92-a-marie-guerin-30-mai-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « C'est que ma petite Marie a laissé ses communions... le jour de l'Ascension et le dernier jour du mois de Marie !... [...] Elle aussi a passé par le martyre du scrupule mais Jésus lui a fait la grâce de communier quand même, alors même qu'elle croyait avoir fait de grands péchés [...] Ce qui offense Jésus, ce qui le blesse au cœur c'est le manque de confiance !... »
- Translation: "It is that my little Marie has given up her Communions... on Ascension day and on the last day of the month of Mary!... [...] She too went through the martyrdom of scruple, but Jesus gave her the grace to receive Communion all the same, even when she thought she had committed great sins [...] What offends Jesus, what wounds his heart, is lack of trust!..."
- Other sources: 1911 prints this letter edited (next card). Ms A describes her own scruples of 1885–86.
- New to English readers: partial — edited in Taylor; the missed feasts, the dream, the candle and "make up the Communions" are cut.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: In these letters this is the first strong statement of "confiance" as the centre. Daily Communion was not then allowed to lay people without the confessor's leave; the letter does not discuss this.

### F-OW-418 — Edit: the scruples letter in 1911 — redated to 1888, merged, details cut
- Claim: 1911 prints LT 92 as "A sa cousine Marie Guérin, Lettre Ire, 1888", with an added heading "(à propos des scrupules)". It opens with a phrase from LT 93 (14 July 1889: "Puisque tu as l'humilité de demander des conseils à ta petite Thérèse"). It cuts the named feasts (Ascension, last day of Mary's month), the dream, the Notre-Dame-des-Victoires candle, "cela m'amuse" in the imagined objection, and the suggestion to make up the missed Communions. It changes "communier quand même" to "communier toujours". The second 1911 letter to Marie Guérin ("1894") is in fact LT 109 of July 1890.
- When / where: letters 30 May 1889 and 27–29 Jul 1890; 1911 print.
- People: Marie Guérin.
- Source: LT 92, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-92-a-marie-guerin-30-mai-1889/ ; LT 109, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-109-a-marie-guerin-27-29-juillet-1890/ ; Histoire d'une âme 1911, "A sa cousine Marie Guérin".
- Source type: own-writing vs edited text
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: LT 92: « Jésus lui a fait la grâce de communier quand même » ; 1911: « Jésus lui a fait la grâce de communier toujours »
- Translation: LT 92: "Jesus gave her the grace to receive Communion all the same." / 1911: "Jesus gave her the grace to receive Communion always."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Wrong dates in 1911 (1888, 1894) would mislead a biographer about when she gave this advice (as a novice of 16–17).

### F-OW-419 — The grain of sand wants to be forgotten; "Petit Belloni", "little Veronica"
- Claim: Pauline's words on Sunday lit her up: "Let us hold back a word that could raise us up." Thérèse wants the grain of sand "always at its place, that is under everyone's feet"; it "does not want to be humiliated; that is still too glorious", it wants only "to be FORGOTTEN, counted for nothing" — but seen by Jesus. "Let Jesus hide the poor grain of sand in his adorable Face; there it will be sure not to sin any more." "I am suffering!... but the hope of the Homeland gives me courage." She calls Pauline "Petit Belloni de Jésus" and "petite Véronique".
- When / where: July–August 1889 (date uncertain), Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Agnès de Jésus.
- Source: LT 95 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, Jul–Aug 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-95-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-juillet-aout-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « le grain de sable ne désire pas d'être humilié, c'est encore trop glorieux puisqu'on serait obligé de s'occuper de lui, il ne désire qu'une chose, être OUBLIE, compté pour rien !... [...] Je souffre !... mais l'espoir de la Patrie me donne du courage »
- Translation: "The grain of sand does not want to be humiliated; that is still too glorious, since people would have to pay attention to it; it wants only one thing: to be FORGOTTEN, counted as nothing!... [...] I am suffering!... but the hope of the Homeland gives me courage."
- Other sources: 1911 prints a short piece of it as "Lettre VIe (1891)" to Mère Agnès.
- New to English readers: partial — a fragment in Taylor under a wrong year; "Belloni" ABSENT.
- Confidence: high for words; "Belloni" is not explained.
- Notes: 1911 dates this "1891" — two years late.

### F-OW-420 — The prioress's verdict on Marie Guérin's scruples: "let yourself be led blindly"
- Claim: Marie Guérin had confided more to Céline. Thérèse asked "our good Mother" that morning what to reply. Mère Marie de Gonzague's message: "You did very well to tell Céline everything; however, it is better not to talk about these things... our Mother is sure you are doing nothing wrong." Thérèse praises the prioress's "deep knowledge of souls" and says in Marie's place she would have "let myself be led blindly". Comfort in Communion is not needed: lack of consolation is a trial. She has "5 letters to write today" and began with Marie: "I love her so much, and so little naturally!"
- When / where: Sunday 14 July 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Marie Guérin; Mère Marie de Gonzague; Céline.
- Source: LT 93 (to Marie Guérin, 14 Jul 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-93-a-marie-guerin-14-juillet-1889/
- Source type: own-writing (quoting the prioress)
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « «Tu as très bien fait de tout dire à Céline, cependant il vaut mieux ne pas s'entretenir de ces choses-là, il vaut mieux n'y faire aucune attention, car notre Mère est sûre que tu ne fais pas de mal.» [...] je me serais laissée conduire en aveugle [...] je l'aime tant, et si peu naturellement !... »
- Translation: "'You did very well to tell Céline everything; however it is better not to talk about those things; it is better to pay them no attention, for our Mother is sure you are doing no wrong.' [...] I would have let myself be led blindly [...] I love her so much, and so little naturally!..."
- New to English readers: yes — the prioress's verdict and "si peu naturellement" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Shows Mère Marie de Gonzague giving spiritual direction to a lay relative through a novice; Thérèse signs "pauvre petite novice sans expérience".

### F-OW-421 — "Let us save the souls of priests... how many bad priests"
- Claim: Thérèse tells Céline that souls "are lost like snowflakes" and Jesus weeps. "Let us live for souls... let us be apostles... let us save above all the souls of Priests; these souls should be more transparent than crystal... Alas! how many bad priests, priests who are not holy enough... Let us pray, let us suffer for them." Postscript: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur cannot write because "the letter would weigh too much" (postage).
- When / where: 14 July 1889, Lisieux Carmel; Céline in Caen area.
- People: Céline; Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur.
- Source: LT 94 (to Céline, 14 Jul 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-94-a-celine-14-juillet-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « sauvons les âmes les âmes, elles se perdent comme des flocons de neige [...] sauvons surtout les âmes des Prêtres, ces âmes devraient être plus transparentes que du cristal... Hélas ! combien de mauvais prêtres, de prêtres qui ne sont pas assez saints... »
- Translation: "Let us save souls, souls; they are being lost like snowflakes [...] let us save above all the souls of Priests; these souls should be more transparent than crystal... Alas, how many bad priests, priests who are not holy enough..."
- Other sources: 1911 "Lettre VIe (14 juillet 1889)" keeps "des âmes de prêtres, surtout" but cuts "combien de mauvais prêtres". Ms A says she learned in Italy that priests are weak men.
- New to English readers: yes — "mauvais prêtres" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: An edit that softened criticism of clergy. 1911 also attaches to this date a passage from LT 102 (April 1890) about the family as lilies.

### F-OW-422 — She forgot Céline's feast: "life in Carmel is so hermit-like"
- Claim: Thérèse let Céline's feast (21 October) pass without wishing it. "Life in Carmel is so hermit-like that the poor little solitary never knows what date it is." She turns it round: this year their feasts fall on the same day, the octave of St Teresa. She sends a Holy Face picture the prioress gave her, suited to "Marie de la Ste Face" (the religious name Céline hopes for), to be "another Veronica". Postscript: "Don't forget to pick a little flower, Céline; it is my heart that will offer it to you."
- When / where: 22 October 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Mère Marie de Gonzague (gave the picture).
- Source: LT 98 (to Céline, 22 Oct 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-98-a-celine-22-octobre-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « la vie au Carmel est si érémitique que la pauvre petite solitaire ne sait jamais à quelle date elle se trouve... »
- Translation: "Life in Carmel is so like a hermit's that the poor little solitary never knows what date it is..."
- Other sources: 1911 "Lettre VIIe (22 octobre 1889)" keeps only the Veronica passage; the apology is ABSENT.
- New to English readers: yes (the apology).
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Contradicts her boast six months earlier (LT 90): "since I have been in Carmel I have a very good memory for dates." A human, humorous inconsistency.

### F-OW-423 — "The littlest of your seven little daughters"
- Claim: For her aunt's feast (19 November) Thérèse recalls writing from Rome two years earlier. She counts herself "the littlest of your 7 little daughters" (the five Martin girls and two Guérin girls). She is "so imperfect" that her prayers have little value, but like beggars who get what they want by pestering, she will not let God send her away empty-handed. "Four o'clock is striking; I must leave you."
- When / where: 18 November 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Mme Guérin; Mme Fournet (Mme Guérin's mother, feast the same day); Isidore Guérin; cousins.
- Source: LT 99 (to Mme Guérin, 18 Nov 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-99-a-mme-guerin-18-novembre-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « il est des mendiants qui a force d'importuner obtiennent ce qu'ils désirent ; je ferai comme eux et le bon Dieu ne pourra me renvoyer les mains vides... [...] la plus petite de vos 7 petites filles »
- Translation: "There are beggars who get what they want by pestering; I will do as they do, and the good God will not be able to send me away empty-handed... [...] the littlest of your 7 little daughters."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high for words; "7" as five Martins plus two Guérins is our reading (not stated).
- Notes: Shows the Guérins' role as substitute parents during the father's confinement.

### F-OW-424 — New Year 1890: "I still see Papa covering us with caresses"
- Claim: To her uncle and aunt: "The first day of the year is a world of memories for me... I still see Papa covering us with his caresses... He was so good!... But why recall these memories? This dear Father has received the reward of his virtues; God has sent him a trial worthy of him." She hopes Marie Guérin recovers quickly: "I will be cross with her if the INFLUENZA stops her coming to see us!" Nine o'clock strikes.
- When / where: 30 December 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Isidore and Mme Guérin; Louis Martin; Marie Guérin (ill with influenza).
- Source: LT 100 (to M. and Mme Guérin, 30 Dec 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-100-a-m-et-mme-guerin-le-30-decembre-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Le premier jour de l'an est pour moi un monde de souvenirs... Je vois encore Papa nous comblant de ses caresses... Il était si bon !... Mais pourquoi rappeler ces souvenirs ? »
- Translation: "New Year's Day is a world of memories for me... I can still see Papa showering us with caresses... He was so good!... But why bring back these memories?"
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: She speaks of her father in the past tense while he is alive in Caen. The influenza epidemic of winter 1889–90 is noted.

### F-OW-425 — New Year's Eve 1889: "now we are orphans"
- Claim: Thérèse writes to Céline, who is in bed ("dans son dodo") after a hard year. "Our New Year's Day is very sad this year... it is with a heart full of memories that I will stay awake waiting for midnight... now we are orphans, but we can say with love 'Our Father who art in Heaven'." "Perhaps the year about to begin will be the last!!!" "This year we must make many priests who know how to love Jesus." Postscript: New Year wishes to "Lolo" (not identified), thanks to uncle, aunt, Jeanne and Marie for gifts.
- When / where: 31 December 1889, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; "Lolo"; the Guérins.
- Source: LT 101 (to Céline, 31 Dec 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-101-a-celine-31-decembre-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « c'est le cœur rempli de souvenirs que je vais veiller en attendant minuit... Je me rappelle tout... maintenant nous sommes orphelines, mais nous pouvons dire avec amour «Notre Père qui êtes aux Cieux». [...] Peut-être l'année qui va commencer sera-t-telle la dernière !!! »
- Translation: "With a heart full of memories I am going to stay up waiting for midnight... I remember everything... now we are orphans, but we can say with love 'Our Father who art in Heaven.' [...] Perhaps the year about to begin will be the last!!!"
- New to English readers: yes — "nous sommes orphelines" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high for words; "the last" is not explained (her own death? the world's? the father's?).
- Notes: She stays up until midnight — a detail of Carmel life (or an exception); the letter does not say which.

### F-OW-426 — Tears at the Holy Face: "our family is a branch of lilies"
- Claim: For Céline's 21st birthday Thérèse writes: "Seeing the picture of the Holy Face, tears came to my eyes; is it not the image of our family? Yes, our family is a branch of lilies, and the Lily without a name lives in the middle... his divine blood waters our corollas, and his thorns, tearing us, release the perfume of our love." "Jesus cannot live in half a heart." "It seems to me I have loved you for centuries."
- When / where: 27 April 1890, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline.
- Source: LT 102 (to Céline, 27 Apr 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-102-a-celine-27-avril-1890/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « En voyant l'image de la Ste Face, les larmes me sont venues dans les yeux, n'est-ce pas l'image de notre famille ? »
- Translation: "When I saw the picture of the Holy Face, tears came to my eyes; is it not the image of our family?"
- Other sources: 1911 moves the "branch of lilies" sentence into "Lettre VIe (14 juillet 1889)" and drops the tears and the family link.
- New to English readers: partial.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Links the humiliated Face directly to the family's humiliation through the father's illness.

### F-OW-427 — "I desire to be forgotten, not only by creatures but by myself"
- Claim: While Pauline is on retreat, Thérèse writes: "a grain of sand is not heavy". It wants nothing but "OBLIVION... not contempt, not insults; that would be too glorious... If people despised it, they would have to see it. But OBLIVION!... Yes, I desire to be forgotten, not only by creatures but also by myself." "He will tire of making me wait sooner than I will tire of waiting for him." She calls Pauline a "light lark" with a thread on its leg.
- When / where: about 4 May 1890, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Agnès de Jésus.
- Source: LT 103 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 4(?) May 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-103-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-4-mai-1890/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Oui je désire d'être oubliée, et non seulement des créatures mais aussi de moi-même [...] Il se lassera plus vite de me faire attendre que moi de l'attendre !... »
- Translation: "Yes, I desire to be forgotten, and not only by creatures but also by myself [...] He will get tired of making me wait sooner than I will get tired of waiting for him!..."
- Other sources: 1911 "Lettre VIIe (1892)" to Mère Agnès combines LT 54 (July 1888), LT 106 and LT 103 and adds a sentence (next card).
- New to English readers: partial.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: None.

### F-OW-428 — Edit: 1911 "Lettre VIIe (1892)" — three letters of 1888–1890 and an added confession
- Claim: 1911 prints as one letter to Mère Agnès dated "1892": the "Jésus seul" lines of LT 54 (July 1888), the "hidden... unknown even to those who live with you" lines of LT 106 (10 May 1890), and the "oblivion" lines of LT 103 (May 1890). It adds a first-person sentence not in these letters: "Je n'ai jamais désiré la gloire humaine, le mépris avait eu de l'attrait pour mon cœur; mais, ayant reconnu que c'était encore trop glorieux pour moi, je me suis passionnée pour l'oubli." It also drops LT 106's line about not wanting to go to Lourdes for ecstasies.
- When / where: letters July 1888 and May 1890; 1911 print.
- People: Sr Agnès.
- Source: LT 54, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-54-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-4-juillet-1888/ ; LT 103, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-103-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-4-mai-1890/ ; LT 106, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-106-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-10-mai-1890/ ; Histoire d'une âme 1911, "Lettres à la Rde Mère Agnès", Lettre VIIe.
- Source type: own-writing vs edited composite
- Rights: public-domain
- Original words: 1911 (added): « Je n'ai jamais désiré la gloire humaine, le mépris avait eu de l'attrait pour mon cœur [...] je me suis passionnée pour l'oubli. »
- Translation: "I never desired human glory; contempt had attracted my heart; but having recognized that this was still too glorious for me, I became passionate for oblivion."
- New to English readers: yes.
- Confidence: high that the sentence is not in LT 54, 103 or 106; it may paraphrase LT 95/103, but it is not her wording there.
- Notes: Also: 1911 "Lettre IVe (Sept 1890)" ends with "la palme d'Agnès; si ce n'est par le sang, il faut que ce soit par l'AMOUR", which is from LT 54 (July 1888).

### F-OW-429 — Léonie's swelling and the Lourdes pilgrimage; "we have found our cross again"
- Claim: In fragments to Pauline, Thérèse reports a parlour visit: Céline spoke of "poor little Father" and remarked that on Saturday, feast of the Finding of the Holy Cross (3 May), "we too have found our cross again". Céline asked prayers for Léonie, "for she suffers from her ailment, and I think my uncle finds it dangerous; it is swollen all round". Léonie hopes to be cured at the Holy Face (Tours) or at Lourdes: "she will go down into the pool". Léonie offered to give up her parlour visits to please Céline. "It is you who taught me to love Jesus." "Is not our family a virginal family, a family of lilies?"
- When / where: 5–6 May 1890, Lisieux Carmel (parlour); pilgrimage to Tours and Lourdes following.
- People: Sr Agnès; Céline; Léonie; Isidore Guérin (pharmacist); Jeanne Guérin (mentioned); Louis Martin.
- Source: LT 104 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, fragments, 5–6 May 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-104-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-fragments-5-6-mai-1890/
- Source type: own-writing (fragments)
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « elle a remarqué que c'est samedi, jour de l'Invention de la Sainte Croix que nous aussi nous avons retrouvé notre croix ! Léonie était là. Elle espère être guérie à la Ste Face ou à Lourdes. Elle descendra dans la piscine. [...] je crois que mon oncle trouve cela dangereux, c'est enflé tout autour. »
- Translation: "She noted that it was on Saturday, feast of the Finding of the Holy Cross, that we too found our cross again! Léonie was there. She hopes to be cured at the Holy Face or at Lourdes. She will go down into the pool. [...] I think my uncle finds it dangerous; it is swollen all around."
- New to English readers: yes — "piscine", "Invention de la Sainte Croix" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: medium — the letter is fragmentary; "retrouvé notre croix" probably refers to a new turn in the father's case but the text does not say what.
- Notes: Léonie's illness is not named. Outcome of the Lourdes hope is not in these letters.

### F-OW-430 — "I have no wish to go to Lourdes to have ecstasies; I prefer 'the monotony of sacrifice'"
- Claim: As Céline and Léonie travel to Lourdes, Thérèse writes to Pauline: "How happy I am to be a prisoner in Carmel for ever; I have no wish to go to Lourdes to have ecstasies; I prefer 'the monotony of sacrifice'! What happiness to be so well hidden that no one thinks of you!... to be unknown even to the people who live with you." To Céline the same day she asks her to bring back, if she has bought nothing for "Notre Mère", an unpainted statue of Our Lady of Lourdes "for 4 or 5 francs". "We have received nothing from Canada" (Fr Pichon).
- When / where: 10 May 1890, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Sr Agnès; Céline; Léonie; Mère Marie de Gonzague; Fr Pichon (in Canada).
- Source: LT 106 (to Sr Agnès de Jésus, 10 May 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-106-a-soeur-agnes-de-jesus-10-mai-1890/ ; LT 105 (to Céline, 10 May 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-105-a-celine-10-mai-1890/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Que je suis heureuse d'être pour toujours prisonnière au Carmel, je n'ai pas envie d'aller à Lourdes pour avoir des extases, je préfère «la monotonie du sacrifice» ! » ; LT 105: « tu pourrais rapporter une notre-dame de Lourdes sans être peinte dans les 4 ou 5 f. »
- Translation: "How happy I am to be a prisoner in Carmel for ever! I have no wish to go to Lourdes to have ecstasies; I prefer 'the monotony of sacrifice'!" / "You could bring back an unpainted Our Lady of Lourdes for about 4 or 5 francs."
- New to English readers: yes — "monotonie du sacrifice" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: "La monotonie du sacrifice" is in quotation marks in her letter: a borrowed phrase (source not given, perhaps Pauline or Fr Pichon). What sets her apart: preference for hidden routine over extraordinary graces, stated at 17.

### F-OW-431 — Céline misjudged: "Blessed are you when they say all kinds of evil against you falsely"
- Claim: Thérèse was charged to tell Céline not to come with news of Papa during the Pentecost retreat, but to write, and come on Monday. She tells Céline God treats her as privileged, quoting "You will be happy when they persecute you and falsely say all kinds of evil against you." "What a privilege to be misunderstood on earth!" "I will not tell you to aim at [St Teresa's] seraphic holiness, but to be perfect as your heavenly Father is perfect." Jesus makes them drink his cup "by leaving our dear little Father over there".
- When / where: 19–20 May 1890, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Louis Martin ("là-bas", at Caen); Marie Guérin (sent a bouquet).
- Source: LT 107 (to Céline, 19–20 May 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-107-a-celine-19-20-mai-1890/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Céline, quel privilège d'être méconnue sur la terre !... [...] Jésus veut nous faire boire son calice jusqu'à la lie en laissant notre cher petit Père là-bas »
- Translation: "Céline, what a privilege to be misunderstood on earth!... [...] Jesus wants us to drink his cup to the dregs by leaving our dear little Father over there."
- Other sources: 1911 moves the "seraphic holiness" passage into "Lettre IIe (20 octobre 1888)" and adds an invented frame: "Tu voudrais devenir une sainte, et tu me demandes si ce n'est pas trop oser."
- New to English readers: partial.
- Confidence: medium on the persecution — the letter implies people said false things about Céline but gives no facts.
- Notes: Edit: the 1911 frame question ("you ask me if it is too bold") is not in LT 65 or LT 107.

### F-OW-432 — Isaiah 53: the texts of the Holy Face copied for Céline
- Claim: Thérèse sends Céline a sheet on which she copied Isaiah 53 ("no beauty or comeliness... his face was as it were hidden... we thought him a leper, a man struck by God"), Isaiah 63 ("I have trodden the winepress alone"), Revelation 7, lines from the Song of Songs, and a fragment of St John of the Cross ("Le visage penché sur mon bien Aimé..."). "Céline, everything I have to tell you you know, since you are me." "Time is only a mirage, a dream." "Jesus sent us the best-chosen cross that he could invent in his immense love." "Papa!... ah, Céline, I cannot tell you all I think." "Let us pray for priests... Jesus makes me feel every day that he wants this from us both." She also sends a message to Marie Guérin as "Marie du St Sacrement".
- When / where: 18 July 1890, Lisieux Carmel.
- People: Céline; Léonie ("ma chère petite Visitandine"); Marie Guérin.
- Source: LT 108 (to Céline, 18 Jul 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-108-a-celine-18-juillet-1890/
- Source type: own-writing (with her copied texts)
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Céline, tout ce que j'ai à te dire tu le sais, puisque toi c'est moi... [...] Jésus nous a envoyé la croix la mieux choisie qu'il a pu inventer dans son amour immense »
- Translation: "Céline, all I have to tell you, you know, since you are me... [...] Jesus has sent us the best-chosen cross he could invent in his immense love."
- Other sources: 1911 "Lettre VIIIe (18 juillet 1890)" prints an edited version (see Notes).
- New to English readers: partial — edited in Taylor; "toi c'est moi" and "croix la mieux choisie" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Edit: for "Papa!... ah! Céline je ne puis te dire tout ce que je pense", 1911 prints "Et notre père chéri! Ah! mon cœur est déchiré" ("my heart is torn" is not in LT 108). 1911 also replaces "il ne marchande pas avec nous" (he does not bargain with us) with "il nous laisse souffrir".

### F-OW-433 — To Marie Guérin: the village girl, "Aimer", and "Jesus is sick"
- Claim: Thérèse tells her cousin she is like a village girl who dares not accept a king's proposal because she is poor. "If you are nothing, do not forget that Jesus is everything." "You are mistaken if you think your little Thérèse always walks with ardour on the road of virtue; she is weak, very weak." "I know no other means to reach perfection than 'Love'... Aimer." "Jesus is sick, and one must note that the sickness of love is cured only by love." Jesus, to have her heart, "consents to lodge in a dirty, dark little hole". Greetings to "la chère Colonie" (the family on holiday).
- When / where: 27–29 July 1890, Lisieux Carmel (six weeks before her profession).
- People: Marie Guérin ("Marie du St Sacrement").
- Source: LT 109 (to Marie Guérin, 27–29 Jul 1890), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-109-a-marie-guerin-27-29-juillet-1890/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Tu te trompes, ma chérie, si tu crois que ta petite Thérèse marche toujours avec ardeur dans le chemin de la vertu, elle est faible et bien faible [...] pour moi je ne connais pas d'autre moyen pour arriver à la perfection que «L'Amour»... [...] Jésus est malade et il faut remarquer que la maladie de l'amour ne se guérit que par l'amour !... [...] il consent à loger sous un réduit sale et obscur !... »
- Translation: "You are wrong, my darling, if you think your little Thérèse always walks eagerly on the road of virtue; she is weak, very weak [...] as for me, I know no other way to reach perfection than 'Love'... [...] Jesus is sick, and note that the sickness of love is cured only by love!... [...] he agrees to lodge in a dirty, dark little hovel!..."
- Other sources: 1911 prints it as "Lettre IIe, 1894" to Marie Guérin; see Notes.
- New to English readers: partial — edited in Taylor under a wrong year; "Jésus est malade" and "réduit sale" ABSENT from 1911.
- Confidence: high.
- Notes: Edits in 1911: date 1894 (four years late); "le chemin de la vertu" becomes "le chemin du sacrifice"; the whole "Marie du St Sacrement... Jésus est malade... réduit sale et obscur" paragraph is cut. The biographer should date "je ne connais pas d'autre moyen ... que l'Amour" to July 1890.

## Appended block B — small gaps in LT 1–109 (LT 64, 67, 72, 79), continued numbering

### F-OW-434 — To her father: a letter for Léonie sent "through the King himself"
- Claim: During a retreat, a postulant of 15 (in her ninth month in Carmel) writes a short note to her father. She needs to send a letter to "the King's daughter, Princess Léonie" (Léonie Martin). She sends it inside her father's note and addresses him as "His Greatness the King of France and Navarre". She adds that his dignity is hidden from men, but in Heaven God will show it, and the least of the elect will be "like the head of a great people".
- When / where: 8–15 Oct 1888 (the source marks the date "(?)"), Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Louis Martin (father, recipient); Léonie Martin (sister; the letter gives only 'princesse Léonie'); Thérèse
- Source: LT 64 (to Louis Martin, 8–15 (?) Oct 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-64-a-m-martin-8-15-octobre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Si sa dignité ne paraît pas aux yeux des hommes je sais bien, moi, que dans le Ciel elle se montrera aux yeux de Dieu »
- Translation: "If his dignity does not show in the eyes of men, I know well that in Heaven it will show in the eyes of God."
- New to English readers: yes (not found in the 1911 edition by chk.py: "Roi de France et de Navarre" ABSENT)
- Confidence: high (exact text). The letter is a short cover note.
- Notes: The tone is the "King and Queen" game she kept with her father. Written a few weeks after his first collapse and the autumn 1888 illness (see other cards). She does not name the illness. The letter does not say what Léonie's letter said.

### F-OW-435 — To her aunt on her feast: "the cross, the cross alone" and Jesus's answer
- Claim: On Mme Guérin's feast day (St Elisabeth's, 18–19 Nov), the postulant writes that at Communion that morning she asked Jesus to fill her aunt with joys. She says Jesus sends "the cross, the cross alone" to the family in that period. She asked to take all her aunt's sorrows on herself. She says she felt in reply that suffering was the best gift he could give, and that he gave it "only to his chosen friends". This reply told her she was not heard: Jesus loved her aunt too much to take away her cross. A post-script says Marie (Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur) had finished her letter when the cake from the Guérins arrived, and Marie thanks her too. Thérèse's own gift is "a poor little picture".
- When / where: 18 Nov 1888, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Céline Fournet Guérin (aunt, recipient); Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (sister)
- Source: LT 67 (to Mme Guérin, 18 Nov 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-67-a-mme-guerin-18-novembre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « c'est la croix, la croix seule qu'il nous donne pour nous reposer... » ; « il ne la donnait qu'à ses amis de choix »
- Translation: "It is the cross, the cross alone, that he gives us to rest on..." ; "he gave it only to his chosen friends."
- New to English readers: yes (both phrases ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: It is the same month as her father's recovery (LT 66, 15 Nov) and just before the next crisis. The letter says "our trial" and does not name it. Compare the earlier card on her father's autumn 1888 illness. The letter also shows the aunt sent a cake to the Carmel for her own feast, and the nuns received it.

### F-OW-436 — "If she is not a saint, it will be her own fault": New Year letter to her father
- Claim: In her last New Year letter of 1888 to her father, she says she is glad to send wishes "from the Kingdom of Carmel". She says earthly kings are happy when they have made noble marriages for their daughters. Her father, "as a Father and truly as a King", gave her only to the King of Heaven. From "Orphan of the Berezina" she has become a Carmelite. She imagines the Rome guide saying "Gentlemen priests, I will show you a Father like you have never seen". She tells him he could do no more for his Queen, and "if she is not a saint it will be her own fault, for with a Father like you she has the means". She thanks him for treats "this week" and "the whole year". She ends by looking to the day "without shadows" when they will speak with no end. She wishes him "the hundredfold in this life and his BEAUTIFUL Heaven in the other".
- When / where: 30 Dec 1888, Carmel of Lisieux (nine days before her clothing)
- People: Louis Martin (father, recipient); the Roman guide of the 1887 pilgrimage (as remembered in her earlier letters)
- Source: LT 72 (to Louis Martin, 30 Dec 1888), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-72-a-m-martin-30-decembre-1888/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Si elle n'est pas une Sainte ce sera bien de sa faute, car avec un Père comme toi elle en a bien les moyens !... »
- Translation: "If she is not a saint it will be her own fault, for with a Father like you she has all the means!"
- Other sources: The "Orphan of the Berezina" and the guide's joke are the same private jokes as in the letter of about 29 April 1888 (see the card "Three weeks in Carmel"). In Ms A she wrote about her father's gift of her to God.
- New to English readers: yes ("Orpheline de la Bérésina", "Si elle n'est pas une Sainte" and "Roi du Ciel" passage ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The letter is written in the weeks when the family feared for his mind and health (he collapsed early in 1889; see the Caen cards). She does not mention his illness here. It is a joyful letter written to a sick man, which makes the "day without shadows" wording sharper.

### F-OW-437 — The night before her clothing: "one more day and I shall be the Fiancée of Jesus"
- Claim: On 8 Jan 1889 she answers a note from Marie, whom she calls "Lion". She writes that Jesus's plaything (the little ball) is weakness itself and stays where it is unless Jesus throws it. "One more day and I shall be the Fiancée of Jesus." She asks what she can do to thank him. She writes that she thirsts for Heaven, "where Jesus will be loved without reserve", but that one must suffer and weep to get there. She says she wants to suffer whatever Jesus wishes and to let him do what he likes "with his little ball".
- When / where: 8 Jan 1889, Carmel of Lisieux (on the clothing retreat; the clothing was moved from 9 to 10 Jan, see the card "Pain at Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul's")
- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (recipient; the nickname "Lion" is used in LT 75, 79 and 91)
- Source: LT 79 (to Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur, 8 Jan 1889), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-79-a-soeur-marie-du-sacre-coeur-8-janvier-1889/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Plus qu'un jour et je serai la Fiancée de Jésus, quelle grâce !... » ; « Que j'ai soif du Ciel, là où l'on aimera Jésus sans réserve !... »
- Translation: "Only one more day, and I shall be the Fiancée of Jesus. What a grace!" ; "How I thirst for Heaven, where one will love Jesus without reserve!"
- New to English readers: yes ("Plus qu'un jour" and "Lion chéri" ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: Her letters of 6–10 Jan 1889 use the "little ball" (balle) image before Ms A. The clothing took place on 10 Jan, not 9 Jan. The letter says 'one more day' on the 8th, so it seems to count 9 Jan as the day before. The source does not settle this.

## Appended block C — letters LT 182–219 (Feb 1896 – Feb 1897), continued numbering

### F-OW-438 — Céline's profession day imagined in Heaven: Thérèse's long "wedding" letter
- Claim: Céline (Sr Geneviève) asked Thérèse to tell her "how things will happen in Heaven on the day of your wedding" (her profession, 24 Feb 1896). Thérèse writes a very long letter as a court scene. At midnight St Peter opens Heaven's doors. Mary, before coming down, opens the abyss of purgatory and frees its souls to join the procession, because the bride asked that on her wedding day "all suffering be banished from my Spouse's kingdom". Jesus leads Céline to "the poor little cell of the St Elijah dormitory" to rest, and tells the angels: "Do not wake my Beloved". Angels weave crowns and paint the coats of arms of Jesus and Céline on the cloister walls, refectory and choir. Doctors of the Church read the Book of Life and write Céline's hidden graces on banners. Martyrs lay palms and burning arrows on the path, to honour "the martyrdom of love" that will soon consume her life. Thérèse sees Cecilia, Genevieve, Agnes and "Joan the shepherdess in her warrior's costume", St Celine (Céline's patron), and the Carmel led by St Teresa, St John of the Cross and Mère Geneviève. Saints and angels each draw up a family tree to prove they are Céline's relatives. She ends: "I have no wedding gift for my Céline", but she will offer the little cherubs themselves, and she will be a bridesmaid herself. A post-script says at waking Céline will find Jesus, Mary, St Joseph, and her parents beside her, and that Jesus rejoices to hear Céline say the words of the Holy Office for the first time as her own office.
- When / where: 23 Feb 1896, Carmel of Lisieux (for the profession on 24 Feb 1896)
- People: Céline Martin, Sr Geneviève de Sainte-Thérèse (recipient, profession the next day); Louis and Zélie Martin (parents, in the vision); St Peter; the Virgin Mary; St Cecilia, St Genevieve, St Agnes, Joan of Arc; St Celine; St Teresa of Avila; St John of the Cross; Mère Geneviève (foundress of the Lisieux Carmel); St Sebastian
- Source: LT 182 (to Sr Geneviève, 23 Feb 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-182-a-soeur-genevieve-23-fevrier-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Le 24 Février à minuit, St Pierre ouvrira les portes du Ciel » ; « Ne réveillez pas ma Bien-Aimée, laissez-moi seul avec elle » ; « Je vois déjà Cécile, Geneviève, Agnès avec leur compagne Jeanne la bergère, revêtue de son costume guerrier »
- Translation: "On 24 February at midnight St Peter will open the gates of Heaven." ; "Do not wake my Beloved; leave me alone with her." ; "I already see Cecilia, Genevieve, Agnes, with their companion Joan the shepherdess, dressed in her warrior's costume."
- Other sources: The same profession is covered in the Carnet jaune and in LT 183–185 (next cards).
- New to English readers: yes (the passages quoted are ABSENT from the 1911 edition by chk.py; no English text found in local files)
- Confidence: high (exact text); the "wedding" is a ritual profession, and the letter is imagined, not a report.
- Notes: The letter has a clear list of Thérèse's saintly "family": the Carmelite line (Teresa, John of the Cross, Mère Geneviève), virgin martyrs, and Joan of Arc in armour, which links to her plays (RP 1, RP 3). The line "the warriors, St Sebastian at their head" shows her humour. The letter is a "commissioned" piece: Céline asked for it.

### F-OW-439 — The four dead siblings in the Heaven letter: "the little thief who took my name"
- Claim: In LT 182, Thérèse gives the dead children of the Martin family a scene. Four "cherubs" (the letter says "quatre petits chérubins"; Hélène and "les deux petits Joseph" are named) are dressed by "a Mother of inexpressible beauty", while "a venerable Old Man with silver hair" caresses them (the letter does not name them; they are her parents, as the children say). One child, "la petite Thérèse", explains to a curious little one that they are the sisters and brothers of the bride. She says she has come "three times already" to feasts like this, "for Marie, Pauline and Thérèse (the little thief who stole my name)", and that this one is the greatest "because Céline is the last". She claps her hands and jumps into her Papa's and Maman's arms. The "Papa" answers the children who want Céline to see them: he hides today so that his "courageous one" gains merit from staying "without consolation" in exile. He says: "Before, I suffered a lot, and Céline was my only support; now I want to be hers." Maman adds that Céline told her, when very small, that Jesus would be Master and she the Mistress.
- When / where: 23 Feb 1896, Carmel of Lisieux (imagined scene)
- People: the four Martin children who died in infancy or childhood (identification is not in the letter; Hélène named); Louis Martin (father, died 29 Jul 1894); Zélie Martin (mother, died 1877); Céline Martin
- Source: LT 182 (to Sr Geneviève, 23 Feb 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-182-a-soeur-genevieve-23-fevrier-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Thérèse (la petite voleuse qui m'a ravi mon nom) » ; « Autrefois j'ai beaucoup souffert, alors Céline était mon seul appui, maintenant je veux être le sien »
- Translation: "Thérèse (the little thief who took my name from me)" ; "Once I suffered a great deal, and Céline was my only support; now I want to be hers."
- New to English readers: yes (ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high for the text. The identity of the four children is our reading: the letter names "Hélène" and "les deux petits Joseph", which fit the Martin children who died young (Hélène 1870, Joseph-Louis 1866, Joseph-Jean-Baptiste 1867, Marie-Mélanie-Thérèse 1870). Check against the family list in the family-records part of the dossier.
- Notes: The "little thief" line is the child Thérèse (b. 1870), who died before the saint's birth; the saint was named after her, so she "took" the name. This is Thérèse's own spoken picture of her dead siblings and her parents after her father's illness, 19 months after his death.

### F-OW-440 — "Contract of alliance" between Jesus and Céline (24 Feb 1896)
- Claim: On the profession day, Thérèse writes a contract in Jesus's voice. Jesus "marries today Céline, an exiled princess, poor and without titles" and gives himself to her under the name "Knight of Love, Suffering and Contempt". He will not yet return her homeland or wealth. He gives her the "Helmet of salvation and grace, so that her face is hidden like mine". Her name becomes "Geneviève de Sainte Thérèse"; her "most glorious title, Marie of the Holy Face, will stay hidden on earth". She will be "shepherdess of the only Lamb". He says: "I want to Live in my Beloved" and gives his own Name as pledge. It closes: "Given on the mountain of Carmel ... on the feast of my agony, the twenty-fourth day of February, in the year 1896", signed "th. de l'enfant Jésus, editor of the divine knight".
- When / where: 24 Feb 1896, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Céline (Sr Geneviève de Sainte-Thérèse); Thérèse (writer, calls herself "editor")
- Source: LT 183 (to Sr Geneviève, 24 Feb 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-183-a-soeur-genevieve-24-fevrier-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Moi Jésus, le Verbe Eternel, ... j'épouse aujourd'hui Céline, princesse exilée, pauvre et sans titres. Je me donne à elle sous le nom de : Chevalier de l'Amour, de la Souffrance et du Mépris. »
- Translation: "I, Jesus, the Eternal Word ... marry Céline today, an exiled princess, poor and without titles. I give myself to her under the name: Knight of Love, Suffering and Contempt."
- New to English readers: yes (ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The name Sr Geneviève de Sainte-Thérèse is the name Céline took as a nun. The letter gives the hidden name only as "Marie de la Sainte Face"; it does not explain it. The letter shows Thérèse's habit of writing pieces on demand for family occasions, in the legal style of a feudal document.

### F-OW-441 — A gift "in Mère Geneviève's name" and a holy card with three days of thorns
- Claim: LT 184 is a note signed "Mère Geneviève", written by Thérèse for Céline's profession. It gives Céline "the last tear I shed on this land of exile" as a wedding gift, and tells her to keep it on her heart, and that "Sr Geneviève de Ste Thérèse" can reach holiness only by suffering. LT 185 is a text on the back of a holy card of St Agnes (front in Gothic script: "Posuit signum in faciem meam"). It says Céline "will press on her heart the thorns of Suffering and Contempt", knowing that Mary can turn into milk the blood of wounds made by Love. In her left hand she presses the thorns, with her right she embraces Jesus, "the divine bouquet of myrrh". The four little cherubs "whose wings have hardly touched the earth" watch her. It is dated 24 Feb – 17 Mar 1896.
- When / where: 24 Feb 1896 (LT 184); 24 Feb – 17 Mar 1896 (LT 185), Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Céline (recipient); Mère Geneviève of St Bernard (foundress of the Lisieux Carmel, died 1891; Thérèse writes in her name); St Agnes (image)
- Source: LT 184 and LT 185 (to Sr Geneviève, 24 Feb 1896 and 24 Feb – 17 Mar 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-184-a-soeur-genevieve-24-fevrier-1896/ ; https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-185-a-soeur-genevieve-24-fevrier-17-mars-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « je donne pour cadeau de noces la dernière larme que j'ai versée sur cette terre d'exil » ; « Marie peut changer en Lait, le sang des blessures faites par l'Amour »
- Translation: "I give as a wedding gift the last tear I shed on this land of exile." ; "Mary can change into milk the blood of the wounds made by Love."
- New to English readers: yes ("Marie peut changer en Lait" and "Posuit Signum" ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high for the text. That LT 184's "last tear" is Mère Geneviève's has to be read from the signature. The letter does not say the tear is real; the wording suggests a keepsake, and the letter does not say more.
- Notes: The date on LT 185 is a span because it was given on the day and finished later. Thérèse uses the name of a dead foundress as a way to speak to Céline.

### F-OW-442 — To Léonie on her feast day: "there are five of us who can call Jesus 'our Beloved'"
- Claim: On Léonie's feast day (11 Apr 1896), Thérèse says she has nothing to give, "not even a holy card", but that "tomorrow" (the next day, 12 Apr) she will offer her Jesus in the Host, "your Spouse and mine". She writes that "all five of us" can call Jesus "our Beloved". She says: "I love you a thousand times more tenderly than ordinary sisters love each other, because I can love you with the Heart of our heavenly Spouse." She signs "your very little sister".
- When / where: 11 Apr 1896, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Léonie Martin (recipient); the "five" (the letter does not list them)
- Source: LT 186 (to Léonie, 11 Apr 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-186-a-leonie-11-avril-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Je t'aime mille fois plus tendrement que ne s'aiment des soeurs ordinaires, puisque je puis t'aimer avec le Coeur de notre Céleste Epoux. »
- Translation: "I love you a thousand times more tenderly than ordinary sisters love each other, because I can love you with the Heart of our heavenly Spouse."
- New to English readers: yes (ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The letter does not mention the haemoptysis of Good Friday (3 Apr 1896) that came eight days before; it also does not mention the trial of faith that began at Easter. Both are known from her later writings. The silence is a fact to record: nothing in LT 186–191 tells Léonie about the illness (LT 191 has only "I no longer cough at all").

### F-OW-443 — To a novice: forget-me-nots, and St John of the Cross on being judged on love
- Claim: On 30 Apr 1896 Thérèse gives Sr Marie de la Trinité forget-me-nots and writes that she wants "immortal flowers", which exist only in Heaven. She recalls "the day when Jesus gave you the Kiss of union" (LT 187). On 7 May 1896 she gives her a holy card. On the front she wrote "By Love, to suffer and be despised". On the back she copied three sayings of "our Father St John of the Cross", including "In the evening of this life, you will be examined on love. Learn then to love God as he wants to be loved, and leave yourself" (LT 188).
- When / where: 30 Apr 1896 and 7 May 1896, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Sr Marie de la Trinité (Marie-Louise Castel, novice, recipient); St John of the Cross
- Source: LT 187 (to Sr Marie de la Trinité, 30 Apr 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-187-a-soeur-marie-de-la-trinite-30-avril-1896/ ; LT 188 (7 May 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-188-a-soeur-marie-de-la-trinite-7-mai-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words and the quoted saint); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Au soir de cette vie, on vous examinera sur l'amour. Apprenez donc à aimer Dieu comme il veut être aimé et laissez-vous vous-même. »
- Translation: "At the evening of this life you will be examined on love. Learn then to love God as he wishes to be loved, and leave yourself behind."
- Other sources: The phrase "au soir de cette vie" is also the opening of a line in her Act of Oblation (June 1895).
- New to English readers: partial (the John of the Cross saying is well known; this dated gift to a novice is not in the 1911 edition, "on vous examinera sur l'amour" ABSENT)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The letter does not say the day is the novice's profession, but "the Kiss of union" is a phrase used for it. The date 30 Apr 1896 as her profession day is not proved by this file. Do not say so without the community's records.

### F-OW-444 — First letter to Père Roulland: linens she painted, and "missionary by love and penance"
- Claim: Thérèse's first letter to the missionary priest Adolphe Roulland (23 Jun 1896). For the feast of the prioress (21 Jun) she offered Mère Marie de Gonzague a corporal, a purificator and a pall, so that "our Good Mother" could send them to Roulland for 29 June. She says she owes to that Mother "the intimate happiness of being united to you by the apostolic bonds of prayer and mortification". She writes that obedience "entrusts me with this sweet task", and that she became a Carmelite for this end. "Unable to be a missionary of action, I wished to be one by love and penance, like St Teresa." She asks that he name her at the altar at his first Mass. She says she long wished to know an apostle who would say her name at the altar on his first Mass, and that she had wished to prepare "the sacred linens and the white host". She says she painted the pall "with so much happiness". She asks that their "apostolic union be known to Jesus alone". She signs "your unworthy little Sister in Jesus-Host".
- When / where: 23 Jun 1896, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Père Adolphe Roulland (Missions Étrangères de Paris, going to China); Mère Marie de Gonzague (prioress, the "Bonne Mère"; her feast is 21 June, St Aloysius Gonzaga's day); St Teresa of Avila
- Source: LT 189 (to Père Adolphe Roulland, 23 Jun 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-189-au-pere-adolphe-roulland-23-juin-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « ne pouvant être missionnaire d'action, j'ai voulu l'être par l'amour et la pénitence comme Sainte Thérèse ma séraphique Mère » ; « la pale que j'ai peinte avec tant de bonheur »
- Translation: "Since I cannot be a missionary of action, I wanted to be one by love and penance, like St Teresa, my seraphic Mother." ; "the pall that I painted with so much happiness."
- Other sources: Baseline has Roulland by name only. Compare LT 198 (Bellière, next batch) and LT 201.
- New to English readers: yes ("missionnaire d'action", "pale que j'ai peinte", "Divin Prisonnier d'Amour" ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high for the text. The letter does not state that the priest's first Mass was on 29 June; it says the linens were to reach him "for the 29th".
- Notes: Roulland was assigned to her by the prioress, and this is her own account of the assignment as obedience. The letter shows she worked with her hands (painting a pall) as a sign of her care. It also shows a request for secrecy about the union ("known to Jesus alone").

### F-OW-445 — "Legend of a little Lamb" for Mère Marie de Gonzague (29 Jun 1896): the election that took seven ballots
- Claim: Thérèse gives her prioress a story in which a "Shepherdess" is hurt because her flock did not choose her at once. In the story a little lamb falls asleep at the Shepherdess's feet and finds herself before the Good Shepherd. Jesus takes her on his lap. She says the flock chose its shepherdess by vote, and that "only after 7 deliberations was the crook placed in her hands". Jesus says he did not just permit but "willed" the trial, because he loves the Shepherdess and had prepared it from all eternity. The lamb argues: could he not have given it to another, as the Shepherdess's "dear Mother" wished, or at least after the first ballot? Jesus says he shows his wounds, "received in the house of those who loved me", and asks "What had I done to my people?" He says he removes human supports "to fill her loving heart alone". He also says the creatures are to be used as "steps", not fled from. He promises to take "the Shepherdess and her lamb" soon. The lamb asks Jesus not to leave them long in exile; he answers "soon, yes soon".
- When / where: 29 Jun 1896, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Mère Marie de Gonzague (prioress, recipient; the letter does not name her in the story); Thérèse (the "lamb" who is the Shepherdess's favourite); the community (the flock)
- Source: LT 190 (to Mère Marie de Gonzague, 29 Jun 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-190-a-mere-marie-de-gonzague-29-juin-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « ce ne fut qu'après avoir délibéré 7 fois que la houlette fut placée dans ses mains » ; « Regarde ces plaies, ce sont celles que j'ai reçues dans la maison de ceux qui m'aimaient!... »
- Translation: "Only after deliberating 7 times was the crook placed in her hands." ; "Look at these wounds. I received them in the house of those who loved me!"
- New to English readers: yes ("Légende d'un tout petit agneau" and "délibéré 7 fois" ABSENT from the 1911 edition; the sentence "these wounds I received in the house of those who loved me" appears in the 1911 edition in another letter, so that wording is known)
- Confidence: high for the text. The identification (the Shepherdess as Mère Marie de Gonzague, the vote as the March 1896 prioral election, the lamb as Thérèse) is our reading from the addressee, the date and "7 deliberations", and the letter does not name anyone.
- Notes: This is the fullest record in her hand of the community's opposition to Mère Marie de Gonzague in 1896: the flock chose her only after seven ballots, "the spirit of the flock is going", and "sheep who reason at ground level" hurt her. Thérèse is on the prioress's side. The letter reports what the nuns thought only through the lamb's words. Check the election date and the ballot count in the community records before printing it as fact. She also says the story brings peace: "your words put peace and joy in my little heart".

### F-OW-446 — "Take Jesus by the heart": the child, the mother and the single hair (to Léonie)
- Claim: Léonie wrote that the thought of death is sweet to her. Thérèse answers that "God is much better than you think; he is content with a look, a sigh of love". She says perfection is easy, "because I have understood that one has only to take Jesus by the Heart". She compares a child who has angered its mother: if it hides in a corner and cries for fear of punishment, the mother will not forgive; if it holds out its arms, smiling, and says "Kiss me, I will not do it again", the mother will hold it, even though she knows it will do it again. Jesus is held "by a hair that flies on our neck". "The smallest actions done for love are those that charm his heart." She says joys, too, are hairs, if offered to Jesus and not kept in selfish happiness.
- When / where: 12 Jul 1896, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Léonie Martin (recipient); Isaiah (quoted: "Can a mother forget her child?"); Jeanne and Francis La Néele (mentioned); Mme and M. Guérin (aunt and uncle)
- Source: LT 191 (to Léonie, 12 Jul 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-191-a-leonie-12-juillet-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « il n'y a qu'à prendre Jésus par le Coeur » ; « comment craindre celui qui se laisse enchaîner par un cheveu qui vole sur notre cou !... »
- Translation: "One has only to take Jesus by the Heart." ; "How can we fear him who lets himself be bound by one hair that flies about on our neck?"
- Other sources: The 1911 edition prints most of this letter; chk.py finds "prendre Jésus par le coeur", the child-and-mother passage and the hair passage there. The hair image comes from the Song of Songs (outside knowledge; the letter does not cite it).
- New to English readers: no (the passages quoted are in the 1911 edition, so in Taylor 1912)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This is one of the clearest statements of her "little way" by the mother-child picture, written about a month after Ms B. It is in the letter to Léonie, her sister who was still not settled in religious life.

### F-OW-447 — "I no longer cough at all" and no preparations for dying (12 Jul 1896)
- Claim: Léonie asked about her health. Thérèse answers: "I do not cough at all any more. Are you happy?" She says this will not stop God from taking her whenever he wishes. "Since I make every effort to be a very little child, I have no preparations to make. Jesus will have to pay all the costs of the journey and the price of entry into Heaven himself." She also explains that letters reach her last: "we are five, and you know I am the smallest", so she sees letters "well after the others or not at all".
- When / where: 12 Jul 1896, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Léonie (recipient)
- Source: LT 191 (to Léonie, 12 Jul 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-191-a-leonie-12-juillet-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « je ne tousse plus du tout. Es-tu contente ?... » ; « Jésus devra Lui-même payer tous les frais du voyage et le prix d'entrée au Ciel »
- Translation: "I do not cough at all any more. Are you glad?" ; "Jesus will have to pay all the costs of the journey and the price of entry into Heaven himself."
- New to English readers: partial ("je ne tousse plus du tout" and "prix d'entrée au Ciel" are in the 1911 edition, so in Taylor; the letters-arrive-last remark is not)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: Three months after the first haemoptysis (Good Friday 1896) she tells Léonie she has stopped coughing. The letter does not mention the spitting of blood. Compare her later letters, where the illness reappears (1897). The "five" are not named.

### F-OW-448 — Her health in July 1896: Dr de Cornière says "she looks well", and the sermon on Ahab
- Claim: Writing to her aunt as to "a mamma", Thérèse says she is well but will let "the famous Dr de Cornière" speak. She was presented to him in the parlour the day before, and he "honoured me with a look" and declared she had "a good complexion". She adds that she still thinks she will soon "go to Heaven with the little angels", not because of her health but because on that day Abbé Lechêne preached in the Carmel chapel. He traced the origins of the order, compared the nuns to Elijah against the priests of Baal, and said "times like those of the persecution of Ahab were going to begin again". "It seemed to us we were already flying to martyrdom." She hopes the whole family will enter Heaven on the same day.
- When / where: 16 Jul 1896 (the letter says the doctor was met "yesterday", the sermon "today"), Carmel of Lisieux (the date is 16 July; the letter does not name the feast)
- People: Mme Guérin (aunt, recipient); Dr de Cornière (a doctor; the letter gives only this name); Abbé Lechêne (preacher); Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie (Marie Guérin)
- Source: LT 192 (to Mme Guérin, 16 Jul 1896), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-192-a-mme-guerin-16-juillet-1896/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Cet illustre personnage, après m'avoir honorée d'un regard, a déclaré que : «J'avais bonne mine!...» » ; « des temps semblables à ceux de la persécution d'Achab allaient recommencer »
- Translation: "This illustrious person, after honouring me with a look, declared that 'I had a good complexion!'" ; "times like those of the persecution of Ahab were about to begin again."
- New to English readers: yes ("de Cornière" and "Lechêne" ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: She writes with irony about the doctor. The letter says nothing about a diagnosis. The sermon is a small trace of the political climate of 1896 (fears of a new persecution of religious orders in France) as it reached the cloister. The letter does not state the cause of the fears.

### F-OW-449 — Working at the "emploi" with her cousin Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie
- Claim: In LT 192 Thérèse says Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie (her cousin Marie Guérin) "will make up for my poverty" in the letter. They are "always together at the emploi (work office)" and "agree very well".
