### F-OW-516 — 10 Aug 1897 to Bellière: "my passport for Heaven", 29 July, and extreme unction on 30 July
- Claim: On 10 Aug 1897 Thérèse writes: "I am now quite ready to leave; I have received my passport for Heaven, and it is my dear father who obtained this grace for me: on the 29th he gave me the guarantee that I would soon join him." (29 July is the date of her father's death in 1894; the letter does not say so.) "The next day, the doctor, astonished at the progress the illness had made in two days, told our good Mother that it was time to fulfil my desires by having me receive Extreme Unction. I had this joy on the 30th, and also the joy of seeing Jesus-Host leave the tabernacle for me, whom I received as Viaticum of my long journey!... This Bread of Heaven strengthened me; see, my pilgrimage seems unable to end. Far from complaining, I am glad the good God lets me suffer still for love of him; how sweet it is to abandon oneself in his arms, without fears or desires."
- When / where: 29–30 Jul 1897; told 10 Aug 1897, Carmel of Lisieux (infirmary)
- People: Abbé Maurice Bellière (recipient); Louis Martin (father, "mon père chéri"); the doctor (unnamed here); Mère Marie de Gonzague (prioress)
- Source: LT 263 (to l'abbé Bellière, 10 Aug 1897), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-263-a-labbe-belliere-10-aout-1897/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « j'ai reçu mon passeport pour le Ciel et c'est mon père chéri qui m'a obtenu cette grâce, le 29 il m'a donné la garantie que j'irai bientôt le rejoindre » ; « le médecin étonné des progrès que la maladie avait faits en deux jours, dit à notre bonne Mère qu'il était temps de combler mes désirs en me faisant recevoir l'Extrême-Onction »
- Translation: "I have received my passport for Heaven, and my dear father obtained this grace for me: on the 29th he gave me the guarantee that I will soon join him." ; "The doctor, astonished at the progress the illness had made in two days, told our good Mother that it was time to fulfil my desires by having me receive Extreme Unction."
- Other sources: The Carnet jaune of 30 Jul 1897 records the extreme unction by Canon Maupas (see the Carnet jaune cards to come).
- New to English readers: yes ("passeport pour le Ciel" ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The 29 July of the letter is the third anniversary of her father's death (29 Jul 1894). The doctor's remark is quoted through the prioress. The letter does not say what the "guarantee" was (a dream, a feeling, or a sign).

### F-OW-517 — Bellière's "inheritance": a reliquary, a worn crucifix, and the last picture she painted (10 Aug 1897)
- Claim: In LT 263 she lists what the prioress will give Bellière after her death: "1° The reliquary I received the day of my clothing, which has never left me since. 2) A little Crucifix that is incomparably dearer to me than the big one, for it is no longer the first that was given to me that I have now. In Carmel one sometimes changes objects of piety, a good way to keep one from getting attached. I come back to the little Crucifix. It is not beautiful, the figure of Christ has almost disappeared; you will not be surprised when you know that since I was 13 this souvenir from one of my sisters has followed me everywhere. It was above all during my journey in Italy that this Crucifix became precious to me; I had it touch all the important relics I had the happiness to venerate; to say the number would be impossible; moreover it was blessed by the Holy Father. Since I have been ill I hold our dear little Crucifix almost always in my hands; looking at it I think with joy that after receiving my kisses it will go to claim those of my little brother." She adds that the prioress will also give him "the last picture I painted". She thanks him for his photograph.
- When / where: 10 Aug 1897, Carmel of Lisieux (infirmary)
- People: Abbé Maurice Bellière (recipient); Mère Marie de Gonzague (prioress, who will give); one of her sisters (donor of the crucifix, at age 13)
- Source: LT 263 (to l'abbé Bellière, 10 Aug 1897), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-263-a-labbe-belliere-10-aout-1897/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Il n'est pas beau, la figure du Christ a presque disparu ... depuis l'âge de 13 ans ce souvenir d'une de mes sœurs m'a suivie partout » ; « Depuis que je suis malade je tiens presque toujours dans mes mains notre cher petit Crucifix »
- Translation: "It is not beautiful; the figure of Christ has almost disappeared... since I was 13 this keepsake from one of my sisters has followed me everywhere." ; "Since I have been ill I hold our dear little Crucifix almost always in my hands."
- New to English readers: yes ("petit Crucifix", "l'héritage que vous recueillerez" and "dernière image que j'ai peinte" ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: This is a rare inventory of her physical objects. The crucifix touched the relics on the Rome trip and was blessed by Leo XIII, per this letter. The letter does not say whether Bellière received them; check the Carmel's records.

### F-OW-518 — The Blessed "are more merciful, not more strict": her view of Heaven, and Bellière's new "dignity"
- Claim: In LT 263 she disagrees with Bellière's idea of Heaven: he thinks that sharing in God's justice and holiness she will not be able to excuse his faults as on earth. She answers: "Have you forgotten that I will also share in the infinite mercy of the Lord? I believe the Blessed have great compassion for our miseries, they remember that, being like us fragile and mortal, they committed the same faults and fought the same battles, and their brotherly tenderness becomes even greater than it was on earth; that is why they never cease to protect us and pray for us." A postscript: "I congratulate you on your new dignity; on the 25th, the day I keep the feast of my dear little father, I will have the happiness to keep the feast of my brother Louis de France too." She also writes: "(It is by choice that I became your sister.)"
- When / where: 10 Aug 1897, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Abbé Maurice Bellière (recipient)
- Source: LT 263 (to l'abbé Bellière, 10 Aug 1897), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-263-a-labbe-belliere-10-aout-1897/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Oubliez-vous donc que je participerai aussi à la miséricorde infinie du Seigneur ? » ; « (C'est par choix que je suis devenue votre sœur.) »
- Translation: "Have you forgotten that I will also share in the infinite mercy of the Lord?" ; "(It was by choice that I became your sister.)"
- New to English readers: yes ("Louis de France" ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high for the words; low for "Louis de France" and "nouvelle dignité": the letter does not say what they are (her father was called Louis; the letter suggests Louis de France is a name or title of Bellière).
- Notes: The post-script "C'est par choix que je suis devenue votre sœur" corrects the idea that she was only assigned. It shows she saw the tie as chosen.

- Notes: These are among her last dated dedications. She still remembered the sisters' birthdays in her last month.

### F-OW-520 — Her last letter: "I cannot fear a God who made himself so small for me" (25 Aug 1897)
- Claim: Her last letter in the corpus is a holy card sent to Bellière on 25 Aug 1897. The front (recto) reads: "I cannot fear a God who made himself so small for me... I love him!... for he is only love and mercy!" The back (verso): "Last souvenir of a soul sister of yours, Th. de E. J."
- When / where: 25 Aug 1897, Carmel of Lisieux (infirmary), five weeks before her death
- People: Abbé Maurice Bellière (recipient)
- Source: LT 266 (to l'abbé Bellière, 25 Aug 1897), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/correspondance/lt-266-a-labbe-belliere-25-aout-1897/
- Source type: own-writing
- Rights: public-domain (her words); Carmel transcription/notes: unknown
- Original words: « Je ne puis craindre un Dieu qui s'est fait pour moi si petit... je l'aime !... car Il n'est qu'amour et miséricorde ! » ; « Dernier souvenir d'une âme soeur de la vôtre »
- Translation: "I cannot fear a God who made himself so small for me... I love him!... for he is only love and mercy!" ; "Last souvenir of a soul sister of yours."
- New to English readers: yes ("Je ne puis craindre un Dieu" ABSENT from the 1911 edition)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The Carmel site lists LT 266 (25 Aug 1897) as the last letter in its series. Other last writings (for example the prayer of 8 Sep 1897) are still to be checked. The text does not say whether the words were written on a printed card.

## Appended block G — Carnet jaune, 16–22 July 1897 (CJ 16.7–22.7), continued numbering

### F-OW-521 — 16 July 1897: "Do not worry in advance": persecutions, massacres and "eyes torn out"
- Claim: Mère Agnès recorded that she said to Thérèse, "I fear that in dying you will suffer a great deal". Thérèse answered: "Why are you afraid in advance? Wait at least until it happens to be sad. Do you see me torment myself thinking that if persecutions and massacres come, as is foretold, they may tear out your eyes!"
- When / where: 16 Jul 1897, infirmary, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder and speaker of the first line); Thérèse
- Source: CJ 16.7.1, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain (1897 text); Carmel transcription: unknown
- Original words: « Voyez-vous que je me mette à me tourmenter en pensant que s'il survient des persécutions et des massacres, comme c'est prédit, on vous arrachera peut-être les yeux ! »
- Translation: "Do you see me tormenting myself with the thought that if persecutions and massacres come, as is foretold, they may tear your eyes out!"
- Other sources: Compare her letter of 16 Jul 1896 (LT 192) about Abbé Lechêne's sermon on a new persecution "like the time of Ahab".
- New to English readers: yes (keyword check of Novissima Verba: no "massacre")
- Confidence: high (Mère Agnès's contemporary notebook)
- Notes: A joke that shows how the community's fear of persecution (the anticlerical politics of the 1890s) had entered the infirmary. Thérèse's point is trust, not politics.

### F-OW-522 — 16 July 1897: Céline's entry as a proof that "all my great desires will be granted"
- Claim: She said she had completely given up Céline (Sr Geneviève) but could not say she no longer wanted her. "Often in summer, in the hour of silence before Matins, sitting on the terrace, I said to myself: Ah! if my Céline were here beside me! But no! that would be too great a happiness for earth!" It seemed an impossible dream. She desired it "not by nature" but for Céline's soul, "that she walk by our way". When she saw Céline enter, "and not only enter but given to me completely to teach her everything", "I understood what immensity of love God has for me". She concludes: "if a desire barely expressed is thus fulfilled, then it is impossible that all my great desires, of which I speak so often to the good God, will not be completely fulfilled."
- When / where: 16 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Céline (Sr Geneviève); Thérèse
- Source: CJ 16.7.2, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « si un désir à peine exprimé est ainsi comblé, il est donc impossible que tous mes grands désirs dont je parle si souvent au bon Dieu ne soient pas complètement exaucés. »
- Translation: "If a desire barely spoken is fulfilled like that, then it is impossible that all my great desires, of which I speak so often to God, should not be fully granted."
- Other sources: Novissima Verba has the terrace passage in some form (keyword check "terrace").
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: It shows that Céline's entry (14 Sep 1894, other cards) was given to her as a formation charge: "given to me completely to instruct her". Céline was her novice-sister.

### F-OW-523 — 16 July 1897: "The saints of the last times will surpass the first as cedars surpass other trees"
- Claim: She repeated "with a convinced air" a sentence she had read in the book "Les Petites Fleurs" by Abbé Bourbon: "The saints of the last times will surpass those of the first as much as the cedars surpass the other trees."
- When / where: 16 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); the Abbé Bourb[on] (book author, as written in the notebook)
- Source: CJ 16.7.3, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Les saints des derniers temps surpasseront autant ceux des premiers que les cèdres surpassent les autres arbres. »
- Translation: "The saints of the last times will surpass those of the first as much as the cedars surpass other trees."
- New to English readers: yes (no "cedar" in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high for the saying; she quotes a book, and the notebook gives the author only as "l'Abbé Bourb."
- Notes: A trace of her reading (Abbé Bourbon's "Petites fleurs"?). The book title as given is "Petites Fleurs"; we did not identify the work.

### F-OW-524 — To die now with great glory or at 80 with less: "I work only for his pleasure"
- Claim: A saying that Mère Agnès forgot and added further on in the notebook (marked "Oublié par Mère Agnès et rajouté plus loin"): "If the good God said to me: if you die now you will have great glory; if you die at 80 the glory will be much less, but it will please me much more. Oh! then I would not hesitate to answer: 'My God, I want to die at 80, for I do not seek my glory but only your pleasure.'" "The great saints worked for the glory of the good God, but I, who am only a very small soul, I work for his single pleasure, and I would be glad to bear the greatest sufferings, even if only to make him smile once."
- When / where: 16 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 16.7.6, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « je ne cherche pas ma gloire, mais seulement votre plaisir. » ; « je serais heureuse de supporter les plus grandes souffrances, quand ce ne serait que pour le faire sourire même une seule fois. »
- Translation: "I do not seek my glory, only your pleasure." ; "I would be happy to bear the greatest sufferings, even if only to make him smile once."
- Other sources: Novissima Verba has a saying on dying "at eighty" in another context (keyword check).
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The Carmel editors note that Mère Agnès forgot this and added it later. It shows how the notebook was assembled.

### F-OW-525 — 17 July 1897: "I want to spend my heaven doing good on earth" (in context)
- Claim: On Saturday 17 July 1897, after she had spat blood at 2 a.m., Mère Agnès recorded her saying: "I feel that I am going to enter into rest... But above all I feel that my mission is about to begin, my mission to make the good God loved as I love him, to give my little way to souls. If the good God grants my desires, my Heaven will pass on earth until the end of the world. Yes, I want to spend my Heaven doing good on earth. That is not impossible, since even in the very bosom of the beatific vision the Angels watch over us."
- When / where: Saturday 17 Jul 1897 (a note says "à 2 heures du matin elle avait craché le sang"), infirmary of the Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Thérèse
- Source: CJ 17.7, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Je sens que je vais entrer dans le repos... Mais je sens surtout que ma mission va commencer, ma mission de faire aimer le bon Dieu comme je l'aime, de donner ma petite voie aux âmes. Si le bon Dieu exauce mes désirs, mon Ciel se passera sur la terre jusqu'à la fin du monde. Oui, je veux passer mon Ciel à faire du bien sur la terre. Ce n'est pas impossible, puisqu'au sein même de la vision béatifique, les Anges veillent sur nous. »
- Translation: "I feel I am going to enter into rest... But above all I feel that my mission is going to begin, my mission of making the good God loved as I love him, of giving my little way to souls. If the good God grants my desires, my Heaven will be spent on earth until the end of the world. Yes, I want to spend my Heaven doing good on earth. It is not impossible, since even in the bosom of the beatific vision the Angels watch over us."
- Other sources: Baseline has the shorter form. Her letters of the same week say the idea: LT 254 (14 Jul, "I count on not staying idle in Heaven"), LT 253 (13 Jul), LT 257 (17 Jul). Novissima Verba has this saying. Langlois's essay (Carmel site) discusses the wording in the process versions.
- New to English readers: no (the saying is in the baseline; the full form is in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high for the notebook text (Mère Agnès's own record, dated on the same day); the wording differs slightly in later published versions.
- Notes: The text begins with "mission... to give my little way to souls", which goes beyond the short form. Compare LT 253: "I will do more than write to my dear little brother". The claim "until the end of the world" is in the notebook, not only in later editions.

### F-OW-526 — 17 July 1897: "I cannot rest as long as there are souls to be saved"
- Claim: The same day she said: "I cannot make a feast of enjoying, I cannot rest as long as there are souls to be saved... But when the Angel says 'Time is no more!' then I will rest, I will be able to enjoy, because the number of the elect will be complete and all will have entered into joy and rest. My heart leaps at this thought..."
- When / where: 17 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 17.7 (second paragraph), https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Je ne puis pas me faire une fête de jouir, je ne peux pas me reposer tant qu'il y aura des âmes à sauver... Mais lorsque l'Ange aura dit : «Le temps n'est plus!» alors je me reposerai »
- Translation: "I cannot make a feast of enjoying myself; I cannot rest while there are souls to be saved... But when the Angel has said 'Time is no more!' then I will rest."
- New to English readers: partial (the sentence "I cannot rest as long as there are souls to be saved" was not compared line by line with Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The phrase "Le temps n'est plus" is from the Apocalypse (Rev 10:6, not quoted by her). The passage gives the reason for the "heaven on earth" promise: she will not rest before the last soul is saved.

### F-OW-527 — 18 July 1897: "God would not give me this desire unless he meant to fulfil it" and "only inconveniences"
- Claim: On 18 July Mère Agnès recorded: "The good God would not give me this desire to do good on earth after my death if he did not want to fulfil it; he would rather give me the desire to rest in him." And: "I have only inconveniences to bear, not sufferings."
- When / where: 18 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 18.7.1–2, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Le bon Dieu ne me donnerait pas ce désir de faire du bien sur la terre après ma mort, s'il ne voulait pas le réaliser » ; « Je n'ai que des incommodités à supporter, pas des souffrances. »
- Translation: "The good God would not give me this desire to do good on earth after my death if he did not want to fulfil it." ; "I have only inconveniences to bear, not sufferings."
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The two sayings give a dated "before the big sufferings" mark: on 18 July she says she has not yet real suffering; the notebook and editors date the "great sufferings" from 28 July.

### F-OW-528 — 19 July 1897: "I am a little seed; no one knows yet what will come out of it", and her refusal to ask what the doctor said
- Claim: At the start of recreation Thérèse said "I am going to water this evening", and Mère Agnès said "but you should water me too!". Asked "What are you?", she answered "I am a little seed; one does not yet know what will come out of it." On the same day, she told Mère Agnès she had wanted to ask her sister Marie, back from the parlour where she had seen "Mr Youf", what he had said of her condition. She thought it might comfort her. But she said to herself it was curiosity and she decided against it: "since the good God does not allow her to tell me of her own accord, it is a sign he does not want me to know". She avoided bringing up the subject, "for fear that Sr Marie would tell me as if forced". She also confessed that she had sought herself by wiping her face once more than necessary, "so that Sr Marie would see that I was sweating a great deal".
- When / where: 19 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (sister); Abbé Youf (chaplain, "Mr Youf")
- Source: CJ 19.7.1–3, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Je suis une petite graine, on ne sait pas encore ce qui en sortira... » ; « puisque le bon Dieu ne permet pas qu'elle me le dise d'elle-même, c'est signe qu'il ne veut pas que je le sache »
- Translation: "I am a little seed; one does not yet know what will come out of it." ; "Since the good God does not allow her to tell me herself, it is a sign he does not want me to know."
- New to English readers: yes (no "seed" in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The passage says "Mr Youf" was seen by Marie; the notebook does not say what he said about her state. It shows she was not told the doctor's reports and chose not to ask.

### F-OW-529 — 20 July 1897: what she would have done if a sister were ill: "straight to recreation, quite simply, so that no one notices my sacrifice"
- Claim: On 20 July (at 3 a.m. she had spat blood) Mère Agnès asked: "What would you have done if one of us had been ill in your place? Would you have come to the infirmary during recreations?" She answered: "I would have gone straight to recreation without asking any news, but I would have done it quite simply so that nobody would notice my sacrifice. If I had come to the infirmary, I would have done it to please, never to satisfy myself." She added that she would have drawn from such sacrifices great strength; if she had sometimes done the opposite "through weakness", she would not have been discouraged but "would have tried to make up for my failings by depriving myself still more, without it showing".
- When / where: 20 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder, who asked); Thérèse
- Source: CJ 20.7.1, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « J'aurais été tout droit à la récréation sans demander aucune nouvelle, mais j'aurais fait cela bien simplement pour que personne ne s'aperçoive de mon sacrifice. »
- Translation: "I would have gone straight to recreation without asking for any news, but I would have done it very simply so that nobody would notice my sacrifice."
- New to English readers: partial (keyword check finds recreation passages in Novissima Verba; the exact saying was not compared line by line)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: A concrete "little way" rule: hidden self-denial toward the sick sister. It also shows Mère Agnès's worry that others neglected the sick Thérèse.

### F-OW-530 — 20 July 1897: "I am happy to die in the arms of our Mother, because she represents God"; "mortal sin would not take away my trust"
- Claim: Mère Agnès recorded: "The good God has himself represented by whom he wishes, but that does not matter... With you there would have been a human side; I prefer that there be only the divine. Yes, I say it from the bottom of my heart, I am happy to die in the arms of our Mother [the prioress], because she represents God." Then: "... Mortal sin would not take away my trust... Do not forget to tell the story of the sinner above all! It is that which will prove I am not mistaken." Mère Agnès told her she feared for her "the anguish of death". She answered that in those who had died before her eyes she had never seen the terrible sufferings that show as frightening signs; "Mère Geneviève had them for the soul, but not for the body."
- When / where: 20 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Mère Marie de Gonzague (prioress, "Notre Mère"); Mère Geneviève (dead foundress)
- Source: CJ 20.7.2–4, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Le péché mortel ne m'enlèverait pas la confiance. » ; « je suis heureuse de mourir entre les bras de Notre Mère, parce qu'elle représente le bon Dieu. »
- Translation: "Mortal sin would not take away my confidence." ; "I am happy to die in the arms of our Mother, because she represents God."
- Other sources: The "story of the sinner" is the parable of the sinner who is forgiven (the notebook does not say which story).
- New to English readers: partial (Novissima Verba has "mortal sin"; the prioress remark not checked)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: "Notre Mère" may be Mère Marie de Gonzague; the notebook does not say. Mère Agnès here is set aside by Thérèse as too "human" a presence for her death. The sisters' rivalry over the death-bed is visible.

### F-OW-531 — 20 July 1897: questioned "like Joan of Arc before her tribunal"
- Claim: She said: "I am harassed with questions; it makes me think of Joan of Arc before her tribunal! It seems to me I answer with the same sincerity."
- When / where: 20 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 20.7.6, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « On me harcèle de questions, cela me fait penser à Jeanne d'Arc devant son tribunal ! Il me semble que je réponds avec la même sincérité. »
- Translation: "I am harassed with questions; it makes me think of Joan of Arc before her tribunal! It seems to me that I answer with the same sincerity."
- Other sources: The same comparison appears in Novissima Verba (keyword "tribunal").
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: A link to Joan of Arc from her last weeks: she, who had played Joan in her plays, likens the sisters' questions to Joan's trial. The context (who questioned her, and about what) is not given.

### F-OW-532 — 21 July 1897: "If God scolds me a little I will not weep from tenderness; if he does not scold at all, I will"
- Claim: Recorded on 21 July: "If the good God scolds me, even a very little, I will not weep from tenderness... but if he does not scold at all, if he receives me with a smile, I will weep..." Also: "Oh! I would like to know in Heaven the history of all the saints; but no one must tell it to me, because it would take too long. On meeting a saint I will have to know his name and his whole life at one glance." Also: "I never acted like Pilate, who refused to hear the truth. I always said to God: O my God, I really want to hear you; I beg you, answer me when I humbly say: What is truth? Let me see things as they are; let nothing throw dust in my eyes." And when the sisters said she was fortunate to be chosen by God to show souls the way of trust: "What does it matter to me whether it is I or another who gives this way to souls; so long as it is shown, what does the instrument matter!"
- When / where: 21 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 21.7.2–5, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Si le bon Dieu me gronde, même un tout petit peu, je ne pleurerai pas d'attendrissement... mais s'il ne me gronde pas du tout, s'il m'accueille avec un sourire, je pleurerai... » ; « Qu'est-ce que cela me fait que ce soit moi ou une autre qui donne cette voie aux âmes ; pourvu qu'elle soit montrée, qu'importe l'instrument ! »
- Translation: "If the good God scolds me, even a very little, I will not weep with tenderness... but if he does not scold me at all, if he welcomes me with a smile, I will weep." ; "What does it matter to me whether it is I or another who gives this way to souls, as long as it is shown; what does the instrument matter!"
- Other sources: Novissima Verba has the Pilate and "instrument" sayings (keyword checks).
- New to English readers: partial for the whole; yes for "if he does not scold me at all" (no "scold" in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The "way of trust" is called a voie she gives to souls; she says it makes no difference who gives it. This is a dated statement that she did not see herself as owner of the "little way".

### F-OW-533 — 22 July 1897: her nursing as "a flash in my darkness", and "if the angels swept Heaven, the dust would be diamonds"
- Claim: When Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur said "Go on! you are cared for with much love...", Thérèse answered: "Yes, I see it well... It is an image of the love that the good God has for me. I only ever gave him love, so he gives me back love, and it is not finished, he will give me more soon. I am much touched, it is like a ray or rather a flash in the middle of my darkness... but only like a flash!" Marie also recorded the words of M. Youf after her confession: "If the angels swept Heaven, the dust would be made of diamonds."
- When / where: 22 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur (speaker of the first line); Mère Agnès (recorder); Abbé Youf (source of the diamonds saying)
- Source: CJ 22.7.1–2, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « c'est comme un rayon ou plutôt un éclair au milieu de mes ténèbres... mais seulement comme un éclair ! » ; « Si les anges balayaient le Ciel, la poussière serait faite de diamants. »
- Translation: "It is like a ray, or rather a flash, in the middle of my darkness... but only like a flash!" ; "If the angels swept Heaven, the dust would be made of diamonds."
- New to English readers: yes ("diamonds" and "flash" not found in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: She uses "darkness" for the trial of faith while accepting a moment of comfort. The "diamonds" saying is attributed to Fr Youf, not to Thérèse.

## Appended block H — Carnet jaune, 23–27 July 1897 (CJ 23.7–27.7), continued numbering

### F-OW-534 — 23 July 1897: "So near Heaven, all this seems sad", and "It is as if you sang!"
- Claim: On 23 July she was told of associations (pious societies): "I am so near Heaven that all this seems sad to me." When a sister who thought she had consoled her in her "great trial" by reading to her asked "Isn't it true your trial has stopped for a moment?", Thérèse answered: "No! It is as if you were singing!" Later, when Mère Agnès told her again of her fear that she would suffer still more, she said: "We who run in the way of Love, I find we must not think of what painful things may happen to us in the future, for that is a lack of trust and it is like meddling in creation."
- When / where: 23 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); an unnamed sister (who read to her)
- Source: CJ 23.7.1–3, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Non ! C'est comme si vous chantiez ! » ; « Nous qui courons dans la voie de l'Amour, je trouve que nous ne devons pas penser à ce qui peut nous arriver de douloureux dans l'avenir, car alors c'est manquer de confiance et c'est comme se mêler de créer. »
- Translation: "No! It is as if you were singing!" ; "We who run in the way of Love, I think we must not think of what painful things may happen to us in the future, for that is to lack trust, and it is like meddling in creation."
- New to English readers: partial for the last sentence; yes for "It is as if you were singing" (keyword check: no match in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: A dated record that on 23 July the "trial" (of faith) had not lifted even when a sister read to her. It also shows her rule not to worry about the future.

### F-OW-535 — 23 July 1897: her father's illness and her "violent desire for suffering"; Sr Marie des Anges remembers her on the straw mattress
- Claim: She said: "At the time of Papa's trials I had a violent desire for suffering... One evening when I knew he was worse, Sr Marie des Anges, seeing me very sad, consoled me as best she could; but I said to her: 'O my Sr Marie des Anges, I feel I can still suffer more!' She looked at me astonished and often reminded me of it later." Mère Agnès adds Sr Marie des Anges's own account: the little saint, still a postulant, was about to go to bed, sitting on her straw mattress in her nightdress with her beautiful hair on her shoulders; "her look and her whole person had something so noble, so beautiful, that I thought I saw a virgin from Heaven."
- When / where: told 23 Jul 1897; the event is 1888–89 (postulancy, father's illness), Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Sr Marie des Anges (speaker of the second account); Mère Agnès (recorder); Louis Martin (father)
- Source: CJ 23.7.4, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès), with a second testimony (Sr Marie des Anges)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « O ma Sr M. des Anges, je sens que je puis encore souffrir davantage! » ; « Son regard, dit-elle, et toute sa personne avaient quelque chose de si noble, de si beau, que je crus voir une vierge du Ciel. »
- Translation: "O my Sr Marie des Anges, I feel I can still suffer more!" ; "Her look and her whole person had something so noble, so beautiful, that I thought I saw a virgin of Heaven."
- New to English readers: yes (keyword check of Novissima Verba: no match)
- Confidence: high for the sayings; the second paragraph is Sr Marie des Anges's later account, not written in 1897.
- Notes: The detail is physical: nightdress, paillasse (straw mattress), long hair down. It is a rare picture of the postulant. The date is when she was a postulant (1888–89).

### F-OW-536 — 23 July 1897: Mme Swetchine on "union and unity", and the request to pray for her father's cure on the day of her profession
- Claim: She recalled a day at the height of the family's troubles when she met Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur after sweeping the dormitory stairs. She told her she felt strong, and that she was "so possessed" by a saying of Mme Swetchine that she was "as if on fire": "Resignation is still distinct from the will of God; there is the same difference as between union and unity. In union one is still two; in unity one is more than one." She adds "I do not know if this is quite textual". Then: "They obliged me to ask for papa's cure on the day of my profession; but it was impossible for me to say anything but: My God, I beg you, may it be your will that papa be cured!" And: "'In te Domine speravi!' At the time of our great pains, how happy I was to say this verse in the choir!"
- When / where: told 23 Jul 1897; the events are 1889–90 (Sep 1890 for the profession), Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Mère Agnès (recorder); Louis Martin (father); Mme Swetchine (author quoted)
- Source: CJ 23.7.5–7, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « La résignation est encore distincte de la volonté du bon Dieu; il y a la même différence qu'entre l'union et l'unité. Dans l'union, on est encore deux, dans l'unité, on est plus qu'un. » ; « On m'avait obligée de demander la guérison de papa le jour de ma profession ; mais il me fut impossible de dire autre chose que cela : Mon Dieu, je vous en supplie, que ce soit votre volonté que papa guérisse ! »
- Translation: "Resignation is still distinct from the will of God; there is the same difference as between union and unity. In union one is still two; in unity one is more than one." ; "I was obliged to ask for papa's cure on the day of my profession; but it was impossible to say anything but this: My God, I beg you, let it be your will that papa be cured."
- Other sources: The 1911 edition prints the Swetchine sentence in one of her letters and notes it as Mme Swetchine's (chk.py finds it). The profession-day prayer was not checked.
- New to English readers: partial (Swetchine sentence is in the 1911 edition; the setting told here is not)
- Confidence: high; she says the quotation may not be word-for-word.
- Notes: This shows her reading (Anne-Sophie Swetchine, a Russian-born French writer, is named only as "Mme Swetchine" in the notebook). It also shows that at her profession (8 Sep 1890) the family wanted her to ask for her father's cure and that she could only pray for his will.

### F-OW-537 — 24 July 1897: the Holy Family gets the peaches and plums; the omelette "like a slipper"
- Claim: On 24 July she was sent fine fruit but could not eat it. She took the pieces "one after the other as if to offer them to someone" and said: "The Holy Family has been well served. St Joseph and the Child Jesus each had a peach and two plums." Half-voiced, she asked whether it was wrong that she touched them "with satisfaction": "It gives me much pleasure to touch the fruit, above all the peaches, and to see them close." "The Blessed Virgin had her share too. When they give me milk with rum, I offer it to St Joseph; I tell myself: oh, how much good this will do the poor St Joseph!" In the refectory she always saw to whom she should give: the sweet was for the Child Jesus, the strong for St Joseph; when she lacked something, for example when they forgot to pass her the sauce or salad, she was happier because she was giving "for real". She added that Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur once placed her plate of salad so close to Sr Marie de l'Incarnation that she could no longer regard it as hers and did not touch it. "And what a 'slipper' of an omelette they served me in my life! They thought I liked it all dried up. After my death, take care not to give this filth to the poor sisters."
- When / where: 24 Jul 1897, infirmary and refectory memories
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Sr Marie du Sacré-Cœur; Sr Marie de l'Incarnation; the Holy Family (Jesus, Mary, Joseph)
- Source: CJ 24.7.1–2, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « La Sainte Famille a été bien servie. St Joseph et le petit Jésus ont eu chacun une pêche et deux prunes. » ; « quelle «savate» d'omelette on m'a servie dans ma vie ! On croyait que je l'aimais ainsi toute desséchée. »
- Translation: "The Holy Family has been well served. St Joseph and the little Jesus each had a peach and two plums." ; "What a 'slipper' of an omelette they served me in my life! They thought I liked it all dried up."
- Other sources: Novissima Verba has the peaches-and-plums passage; the omelette remark was not found there (keyword check).
- New to English readers: partial (peaches and plums are in Novissima Verba); yes for the omelette "savate"
- Confidence: high
- Notes: A very concrete detail of daily food in Carmel: a dried omelette, salad, sauce. The omelette remark gives a sisterly instruction: do not serve it to the poor sisters.

### F-OW-538 — 25 July 1897: "It is the face I kiss", "The Thief is in my heart", and the Virgin holding the dead Jesus
- Claim: On 25 July Mère Agnès held the crucifix out to be kissed as usual. Thérèse said: "But I kiss the face!" Looking at the picture of the Child Jesus that Sr Marie de la Trinité brought from the Carmel of Messina, she said: "This little Jesus seems to say to me: 'You will come to Heaven, I tell you so!'" When Mère Agnès asked "Where is the Thief now? no one speaks of him", she put her hand on her heart: "He is there! He is in my heart." When Mère Agnès said death was sad to look at and that she would have great pain seeing her dead, she answered in a moved voice: "The Blessed Virgin held her dead Jesus on her knees, disfigured, bloody! It was something else than what you will see! Ah! I do not know how she did it!... Suppose they bring me back to you in this state, what would become of you? Responde mihi?..."
- When / where: 25 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Sr Marie de la Trinité (brought the image from Messina); the Virgin Mary (in her saying)
- Source: CJ 25.7.4–6, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Ah ! mais, moi, c'est la figure que j'embrasse ! » ; « Il est là ! Il est dans mon coeur. » ; « La Sainte Vierge a bien tenu son Jésus mort sur ses genoux, défiguré, sanglant ! C'était autre chose que ce que vous verrez ! »
- Translation: "Ah! but I, it is the face that I kiss!" ; "He is here! He is in my heart." ; "The Blessed Virgin held her dead Jesus on her knees, disfigured, bloody! It was something else than what you will see!"
- New to English readers: yes (no match for these in Novissima Verba by keyword)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: "The Thief" is her image for death and for Jesus who comes "like a thief" (see the 9 June card). "Responde mihi" is Latin for "answer me"; she leaves the question open.

### F-OW-539 — 25 July 1897: grapes from her uncle, tears kept as a relic, and "no boys' games"
- Claim: On 25 July her uncle (M. Guérin) sent her grapes. She ate a little and said: "How good this grape is! But I do not like what comes to me from my family... In the past, when they brought me bouquets for my little Jesus from them, I would never take them without being sure that Our Mother had said so." She also confessed some small things that she blamed herself for, and asked if she had offended God. Mère Agnès answered these were not sins. Thérèse said: "When I heard you, I remembered Father Alexis: your words penetrated as deep into my heart," and she wept; Mère Agnès wiped her tears with a fine cloth ("Sr Geneviève keeps this relic"). She said: "Never throw faded little flowers, only fresh-opened ones". She refused noisy distraction: "No boys' games! No little girls' games either; only games of little angels." She said she looked at a grape: "It is pretty, it looks good; then I eat one; that one I do not give to the little Jesus, he gives it to me."
- When / where: 25 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Isidore Guérin (uncle, sender of the grapes); Père Alexis (a priest she remembered: the notebook gives only the first name); Sr Geneviève (keeps the cloth)
- Source: CJ 25.7.3, 7, 8, 9, 10, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Mais je n'aime pas ce qui me vient de ma famille... » ; « Pas de jeux de garçons !.. Pas de jeux de petites filles non plus ; seulement des jeux de petits anges. » ; « Jamais lancer de petites fleurs fanées... rien que des petites fleurs «fraîches écloses». »
- Translation: "But I do not like what comes to me from my family..." ; "No boys' games! No little girls' games either; only games of little angels." ; "Never throw little faded flowers... only little 'freshly opened' flowers."
- New to English readers: yes (keyword check: no match for "boys" or "games" in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The "faded flowers" saying is about rose-petal throwing at the crucifix and pictures. The tears kept as a relic are Mère Agnès's note: the recorder is already making relics. The notebook says only "le Père Alexis"; we do not identify him.

### F-OW-540 — 25 July 1897: "Fat face" and "wasting away": two opinions on the same novice (told by Thérèse)
- Claim: In a story she told on 25 July Thérèse said: "One day, after my clothing, Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul saw me at Our Mother's and cried: 'Oh, what a face of prosperity! How strong she is, this big girl! how fat she is!' I was going off quite humbled by the compliment when Sr Madeleine stopped me in front of the kitchen and said: 'What is becoming of you, poor little Sr Thérèse of the Child Jesus! You are getting thin before our eyes! If you go on at this rate, with that face that makes one tremble, you will not follow the rule for long!' I could not get over hearing one after the other two such opposite opinions. From that moment I never gave importance to the opinion of creatures, and this has so grown that now blame and compliments glide off me without leaving the slightest trace."
- When / where: told 25 Jul 1897; the event is after Jan 1889 (after her clothing), Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Sr Saint-Vincent-de-Paul; Sr Madeleine (the notebook says "Sr Madeleine"); Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 25.7.15, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Oh! quelle figure de prospérité! Est-elle forte cette grande fille! est-elle grosse! » ; « Vous maigrissez à vue d'oeil! Si vous y allez de ce train là, avec cette mine qui fait trembler, vous ne suivrez pas longtemps la règle! »
- Translation: "Oh, what a face of prosperity! How strong she is, this big girl! How fat she is!" ; "You are getting thin before our eyes! If you go on at this rate, with that look that makes one tremble, you will not follow the rule for long!"
- Other sources: See the card on LT 69 (fur boots) about the same first winter. chk.py finds neither remark in the 1911 edition.
- New to English readers: yes (keyword check: none)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: It says at 16 she looked "strong" and "fat" to one sister and "thin" to another. It is one of the few outside remarks about her looks in the novitiate.

### F-OW-541 — 25 July 1897: on spiritual direction: "the guards took my cloak" (Song of Songs)
- Claim: About spiritual directors she said: "I think we must take care not to seek ourselves, for then the heart is wounded afterwards and one could say with truth: 'The guards took my cloak, they wounded me... it was only after passing them a little that I found my Beloved.'" She thought that if the soul had humbly asked the guards where her Beloved was, "they would have told her where he was, but wanting to be admired she fell into trouble; she lost simplicity of heart."
- When / where: 25 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 25.7.13, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Les gardes m'ont enlevé mon manteau, ils m'ont blessée... ce n'est qu'après les avoir dépassés un peu que j'ai trouvé mon Bien-Aimé »
- Translation: "The guards took my cloak, they wounded me... it was only after passing them a little that I found my Beloved."
- New to English readers: yes (no "guards/watchmen" in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The text is from the Song of Songs (5:7, which she does not cite in the notebook). It is her only recorded remark in these pages on directors and the risk of seeking oneself in direction.

### F-OW-542 — 26 July 1897: a dream of a bazaar with her father; and her job timeline in the novitiate
- Claim: On 26 July she said: "I dreamed last night that I was in a bazaar with Papa; I saw pretty little white pincushions that tempted me to put my pins in; but in the end I told myself that they make the same at the Carmel, and I asked for a little music." Later she told Mère Agnès the timeline of her charge of the novices: "Around 8 Dec 1892 she had taken care of Sr Marthe; in 1893 she had helped Mère Marie de Gonzague at the novitiate, and at the last election in 1896 she found herself, so to speak, entirely charged with the novices." And: "Virtue shines naturally; as soon as it is not there, I see it."
- When / where: 26 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Louis Martin (father, in the dream); Sr Marthe (novice); Mère Marie de Gonzague (novice mistress and prioress)
- Source: CJ 26.7.1–3, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « vers le 8 Déc.1892 elle s'était occupée de Sr Marthe ; qu'en 1893 elle avait aidé Mère Marie de Gonzague au noviciat et qu'à la dernière élection en 1896 elle s'était vue pour ainsi dire chargée complètement des novices. »
- Translation: "Around 8 Dec 1892 she had taken care of Sr Marthe; in 1893 she had helped Mère Marie de Gonzague at the novitiate, and at the last election in 1896 she found herself, so to speak, entirely in charge of the novices."
- New to English readers: yes (bazaar dream and dated timeline absent from Novissima Verba by keyword)
- Confidence: high; the timeline is Mère Agnès's report in indirect speech.
- Notes: This is the only dated list in the notebook of when she took on the novices: Dec 1892 (Sr Marthe), 1893 (with the prioress), 1896 (after the election). Compare Ms C on her role and Sr Marthe's entry.

### F-OW-543 — 27 July 1897: "Break the web of this sweet encounter" and "the smallest movement of pure love"
- Claim: On the evening of 27 July, Thérèse reminded Mère Agnès of a saying of St John of the Cross: "Break the web of this sweet meeting." She said: "I have always applied this saying to the death of love that I desire. Love will not wear out the web of my life, it will break it suddenly." She added other words of St John of the Cross that she had repeated "from the beginning of my religious life": "It is most important that the soul practise Love greatly so that, being consumed quickly, it stays hardly at all here below but arrives quickly to see its God face to face." Repeating these last words "she raised her finger and took a heavenly air".
- When / where: 27 Jul 1897 (evening), infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); St John of the Cross
- Source: CJ 27.7.5, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Rompez la toile de cette douce rencontre. » ; « L'amour n'usera pas la toile de ma vie, il la rompra tout à coup. »
- Translation: "Break the web of this sweet meeting." ; "Love will not wear out the web of my life; it will break it suddenly."
- Other sources: Novissima Verba has the "web" saying (keyword). The second John of the Cross saying is on her June 1897 farewell card (LT 245).
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The phrase "the death of love" recurs (see her Act of Oblation and Ms B). The gesture (raised finger) is Mère Agnès's note.

### F-OW-544 — 27 July 1897: "as Joan of Arc said: God's will shall be done despite men's jealousy" — on the publication of her life
- Claim: Mère Agnès mentioned difficulties she foresaw for the publication of Thérèse's life. Thérèse answered: "Well, I say like Joan of Arc: '...And God's will shall be accomplished in spite of men's jealousy.'"
- When / where: 27 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Joan of Arc (quoted)
- Source: CJ 27.7.6, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/

- Translation: "Well, I say as Joan of Arc did: '...And the will of God will be accomplished in spite of the jealousy of men.'"
- Other sources: Novissima Verba has a saying "As for my mission, like that of Joan of Arc, the will of God will be [accomplished, despite the jealousy of men]" (keyword hit "Joan of Arc" and "jealousy").

### F-OW-545 — 27 July 1897: doctor's drops, a dream of being carried between torches, and the laundry prayer
- Claim: On 27 July, Thérèse told Mère Agnès not to forget the drops of a medicine: "Oh! you must build yourself up; 30 drops this evening, do not forget!" When asked "Do we tire you?" she answered "No, because you are very nice people of the world." She told with a laugh that she had dreamed she was carried to the warming room between two torches for Our Father's feast. And on the day the community was at the laundry: "Towards one o'clock I said to myself: they are very tired at the wash! and I prayed to God that he relieve you all, that the work be done in peace and charity. When I saw myself so ill, I felt joy at having to suffer like you."
- When / where: 27 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); the community
- Source: CJ 27.7.1–4, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Quand je me suis vue si malade, j'ai éprouvé de la joie d'avoir à souffrir comme vous. » ; « Non, parce que vous êtes du monde très gentil. »
- Translation: "When I saw myself so ill, I felt joy at having to suffer like you." ; "No, because you are very nice people."
- New to English readers: partial (Novissima Verba has "drops" and "laundry" passages; "very nice people of the world" not checked)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The "chauffoir" (warming room) is the Carmel's heated room. "Notre Père" is not identified in the notebook.

### F-OW-546 — 27 July 1897: nicknames and baby talk with her sisters ("telephone", "E' voit bien...")
- Claim: On 27 July she said "I shall soon no longer see your dear little face! I shall see only your little soul." – "It is much more beautiful!" And to those who said "when one thinks we are going to lose you!", she answered "But you will not lose me... point fine!..." To Sr Geneviève who was crying she said in a mock-village voice: "E' voit bien qu'c'est ça qui lui pend au bout du nez (la mort) la v'la saisie d'peur maintenant!" Of Mère Agnès: "Little Mother is my telephone; I have only to stretch my little ear when she comes and I know everything!" And after offering a bunch of grapes to the Child Jesus: "I offered him this bunch to make him want to take me, because I believe I am of that kind..." Tasting a grape: "Yes, I am of that kind."
- When / where: 27 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès; Sr Geneviève (Céline); Thérèse
- Source: CJ 27.7.7–11, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « La petite Mère c'est mon téléphone ; je n'ai qu'à tendre ma petite oreille quand é vient, et j'sais tout ! » ; « Je lui ai offert cette grappe là pour lui donner envie de me prendre, parce que je crois que je suis de cette espèce là... »
- Translation: "Little Mother is my telephone; I have only to stretch my little ear when she comes and I know everything!" ; "I offered him this bunch of grapes to make him want to take me, because I believe I am of that kind..."
- New to English readers: yes ("telephone" and the grapes sayings: keyword check found no match in Novissima Verba)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The notebook keeps her baby talk and village accent ("é vient", "j'sais"). It shows humour at the bedside three days before extreme unction. A telephone in 1897 was a rare object; the saying uses it as a picture of Mère Agnès telling her everything.

### F-OW-547 — 27 July 1897: "I am not selfish"; never asked to die young; "God will give me strength"; the iron cross; "curried like a horse"; "from 9 June I was sure I would die soon"
- Claim: A group of sayings of 27 July: "I am not selfish, it is God I love, not myself." "For nature I would rather die, but I rejoice at death only because it is God's will for me." "I never asked God to die young, so I am sure that at this moment he is only accomplishing his will." When she was suffocating and Mère Agnès showed compassion: "Do not be sad, go on! If I suffocate, God will give me strength. I love him! He will never abandon me." She told how she wore "her little iron cross" for a long time and was made ill by it: "it was not God's will for me or for us to throw ourselves into great mortifications; this proved it to me." About the frictions ordered by the doctor: "Ah! to be 'curried' as I have been is much worse than anything!" And: "From 9 June I was sure I would die soon."
- When / where: 27 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); the doctor (not named in these lines)
- Source: CJ 27.7.12–18, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/

- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Elle me raconta comment elle avait porté longtemps sa petite croix de fer et qu'elle en avait été malade. » (Mère Agnès's words) ; « Ah ! d'être «étrillée» comme je l'ai été, c'est bien pire que n'importe quoi ! » ; « A partir du 9 juin, j'ai été sûre de mourir bientôt. »
- Translation: "She told me how she wore her little iron cross for a long time and was made ill by it." ; "Ah! to be 'curried' as I have been is worse than anything!" ; "From 9 June I was sure I would die soon."
- New to English readers: partial (the 1911 edition tells of the iron cross that made her ill, in its narrative; "from 9 June I was sure" is not in Novissima Verba by keyword); partial for the rest
- Confidence: high (Mère Agnès's words for the iron cross; her own words for the rest)
- Notes: "From 9 June" links to her letter to Bellière of 9 June (LT 244) and the "shower of roses" saying. The iron cross is a penitential object (a small cross with points) she wore; the notebook does not date it.

## Appended block I — Carnet jaune, 28–31 July 1897, and three Paroles retrouvées (May–June 1897), continued numbering

### F-OW-548 — Paroles retrouvées, 21–26 May 1897 (no. 11): the Gospel line "the one God has sent says the same things as God"
- Claim: A saying left out of later copies: one evening she was sad and wondered whether God was pleased with her, thinking what each sister would say of her: one "a good little soul, she can become a saint", another "very sweet, very pious, but this... but that...", and several would find her "very imperfect, which is true". About Mère Agnès: "she loves me so much it blinds her, so I cannot believe her." Then Mère Agnès's note reached her, saying she pleased her in everything and was cherished by God, who had not made her climb "the rough stairs of perfection like the others but put her in an elevator so that she would be more quickly brought to him". Still not fully comforted, she opened her little Gospel asking God to answer, and fell on a passage she had never noticed: "He whom God has sent says the same things as God, because he has not given him his Spirit by measure." She wept with joy and woke "still perfumed". She said to Mère Agnès: "It is you whom God sent for me; you brought me up, you made me enter Carmel; all the great graces of my life I have received through you; so you say the same things as God, and now I believe the good God is very pleased with me, since you tell me so."
- When / where: 21–26 May 1897, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Mère Agnès (recipient of the saying; the note-writer); Thérèse
- Source: CJ Paroles retrouvées, "Du 21 au 26 mai", no. 11 https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/paroles-retrouvees/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès; the sayings she left out of later copies)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Celui que Dieu a envoyé dit les mêmes choses que Dieu, parce qu'il ne lui a pas communiqué son Esprit avec mesure. » ; « il ne m'avait pas fait monter comme les autres le rude escalier de la perfection, mais qu'il m'avait mise dans un ascenseur »
- Translation: "The one whom God has sent says the same things as God, because he has not given him his Spirit by measure." ; "He had not made me climb the rough stairs of perfection like the others, but had put me in an elevator."
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high
- Notes: Mère Agnès's note (about the elevator) is her own; The Ms C elevator image is written in the same weeks (Ms C, June–July 1897). The saying shows how Thérèse tested Pauline's praise against the Gospel, and gave Pauline a divine authority: one theme in the later question of Mère Agnès's control of the record ("Remarque importante").

### F-OW-549 — Paroles retrouvées: "even to be 'taken in dislike'" (8 June) and "Superior General of my heart" (19 June)
- Claim: On 8 June, after a talk about long illnesses that tire the nurses, which is a great suffering for patients who notice it, she said: "I am willing to stay like this until the end of a very long life; if it pleases the good God I am even willing to be 'taken in dislike' (prise en grippe)." On 19 June Mère Agnès wrote: their cousin Mère Marguerite (Superior General in Paris of the Auxiliatrices sisters, nurses) had sent Thérèse a pretty basket of artificial lilies for 21 June, the feast of Mère Marie de Gonzague. Mère Agnès brought it, saying joyfully: "It is the Superior General of the Auxiliatrices who sends you this!" Thérèse answered "suddenly with élan and affection": "Well, it is you who are the Superior General of my heart."
- When / where: 8 Jun and 19 Jun 1897, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Mère Marguerite (cousin; Superior General in Paris of the Auxiliatrices sisters, nurses; the notebook says "Notre cousine, la Mère Marguerite"); Mère Marie de Gonzague (whose feast is 21 June)
- Source: CJ Paroles retrouvées, "Le 8 juin" and "Le 19 juin" https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/paroles-retrouvees/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Je veux bien rester comme cela jusqu'à la fin d'une très longue vie ; si cela plaît au bon Dieu, je veux même bien être «prise en grippe.» » ; « Eh ! bien, c'est vous qui êtes la Supérieure Générale de mon coeur. »
- Translation: "I am quite willing to stay like this until the end of a very long life; if it pleases the good God I am even willing to be 'taken in dislike'." ; "Well, it is you who are the Superior General of my heart."
- New to English readers: yes
- Confidence: high

### F-OW-550 — Editors' summary of July 1897: daily blood-spitting from 6 July, the move on 8 July, and the "great sufferings" from 28 July
- Claim: The Carmel editors' heading for the July notebook (their words, not Thérèse's) says: from 6 July the haemoptysis returns almost every day (until 5 August); on 7 July she is suffocating, very weak, with high fever; she is treated with "gentle remedies": ice to stop the bleeding, pancreatin to digest milk, mustard poultices, cupping. Dr de Cornière diagnoses a wound in the right lung and forbids her to move. On 8 July she is taken down to the infirmary. She loses weight daily, with stomach, head and heart pains and diarrhoea, and can no longer eat fruit; the doctor diagnoses congestion in the upper right lung to the shoulder. On 25 July she still gets up for two hours a day, but from 28 July the "great sufferings" begin (continual haemoptysis, suffocation, too oppressed to speak). On 30 July she receives extreme unction and communion as Viaticum from Canon Maupas.
- When / where: 6 Jul – 30 Jul 1897, Carmel of Lisieux
- People: Dr de Cornière; Canon Maupas (priest who gave the sacraments); Thérèse
- Source: Carmel editors' introduction to cj-juillet-1897, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/ (in _work/cj1.txt, lines 374–376)
- Source type: scholarship (Carmel editors; facts only, our words)
- Rights: unknown (modern editorial text; facts only)
- Original words: « Le 30 juillet, elle reçoit l'extrême onction et la communion en viatique du chanoine Maupas. »
- Translation: "On 30 July she receives extreme unction and communion as Viaticum from Canon Maupas."
- Other sources: The earlier F-OW card on the July note ("Editors' July note") gives the first part of this summary. Her letters LT 253–263 and the Carnet jaune give the same dates.
- New to English readers: yes for the treatments (mustard poultices, cupping, pancreatin) and Maupas's name
- Confidence: medium (modern summary of the notebook; the name and treatments are the editors', not in the entries)
- Notes: The name "Canon Maupas" is only in the editors' summary; the notebook entries of 30 July say "Notre Père" (see next cards). Compare her own words in LT 263 (10 Aug) on the same day.

### F-OW-551 — 29 July 1897: "Up there, in the blue sky"; a sister says she is no saint because she did not suffer humiliations
- Claim: On 29 July she said: "I would like to go away!" – "Where?" – "Up there, in the blue Heaven!" A sister had reported to her a remark made at recreation: "Why do they talk of Sr Thérèse of the Child Jesus as a saint?! She practised virtue, it is true, but it was not a virtue acquired by humiliations, and above all by sufferings." Thérèse said: "And I who suffered so much from my earliest childhood! Ah! how good it does me to see the opinion of creatures at the moment of death!"
- When / where: 29 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); an unnamed sister (who reported the remark); an unnamed sister (who made it)
- Source: CJ 29.7.1–2, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Et moi qui ai tant souffert dès ma plus tendre enfance ! Ah ! que cela me fait de bien de voir l'opinion des créatures au moment de la mort ! » ; « Pourquoi donc parle-t-on de ma Sr Thérèse de l'Enfant Jésus comme d'une sainte ?! »
- Translation: "And I, who suffered so much from my very tender childhood! Ah! how good it does me to see the opinion of creatures at the moment of death!" ; "Why do they speak of my Sr Thérèse of the Child Jesus as a saint?!"
- New to English readers: partial (Novissima Verba has a passage on "acquired virtue/humiliations" by keyword; not compared)
- Confidence: high

### F-OW-552 — 29 July 1897: she loses her temper at a gift, asks pardon with tears, and is "happy to be imperfect"
- Claim: A sister thought to please her by bringing her an object; "the opposite happened". Thérèse showed displeasure, guessing that somebody had been deprived of the thing, but soon repented and asked pardon with tears: "Oh! I beg your pardon, I acted by nature; pray for me!" A little later: "Oh! how happy I am to see myself imperfect and to have so much need of the good God's mercy at the moment of death!"
- When / where: 29 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); an unnamed sister
- Source: CJ 29.7.3, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Oh ! je vous demande bien pardon, j'ai agi par nature, priez pour moi ! » ; « Oh ! que je suis heureuse de me voir imparfaite et d'avoir tant besoin de la miséricorde du bon Dieu au moment de la mort ! »
- Translation: "Oh! I ask your pardon, I acted from nature; pray for me!" ; "Oh! how happy I am to see myself imperfect and to have such need of the good God's mercy at the moment of death!"
- New to English readers: partial
- Confidence: high
- Notes: Like 28 May (LT 230), a scene where she loses patience and turns it to trust. The object and the giver are not named.

### F-OW-553 — 29 July 1897: blood at 3 p.m.; "not die at night"; the novices "exhaust me like preachers"; "to be my historian, take care of yourself"
- Claim: On 29 July she had spat blood in the morning and at 3 p.m. She was told they feared she would die at night: "I will not die at night, believe me; I wished not to die at night." She said "Two days after Sr Marie de la Trinité's entry they treated my throat... The good God allowed the novices to exhaust me. Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie told me it happened to me as to preachers." To Mère Agnès: "To be my historian, you must spare yourself." And: "Well! 'baby' is going to die then! For 3 days, it is true, I have suffered a lot; tonight I am as if in purgatory." "Very often, when I can, I repeat my offering to Love." To a sister who had sat her up: "I lean my head on my little Mother's heart."
- When / where: 29 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder); Sr Marie de la Trinité; Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie
- Source: CJ 29.7.4–11, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Le bon Dieu a permis quel les novices m'épuisent. Sr M. de l'Eucharistie m'a dit que cela m'arrivait comme aux prédicateurs. » ; « Pour être mon historien, il faut vous ménager. » ; « ce soir je suis comme en purgatoire. »
- Translation: "The good God allowed the novices to exhaust me. Sr Marie de l'Eucharistie told me it happened to me as to preachers." ; "To be my historian, you must spare yourself." ; "Tonight I am as if in purgatory."
- Other sources: Novissima Verba has "exhaust" and the offering sayings (keyword).
- New to English readers: partial

### F-OW-554 — 29 July 1897: "I am weary of the earth", "no capacity to enjoy, a very great capacity to suffer"
- Claim: On 29 July: "You planted the seed of trust in my little soul; do you not remember?" (to Mère Agnès). Mère Agnès notes that she had not asked for relief, and the sisters thought it was virtue, but she had not thought of mortifying herself in that. As they admired her act: "I am weary of the earth! They pay compliments when one does not deserve them and reproaches when one does not deserve them either. All this!... all this!..." "What causes our humiliation at the moment causes our glory afterwards, even in this life." "I have no capacity for enjoyment, I have always been like that; but I have a very great one for suffering. In the past, when I had great pain, I felt appetite in the refectory, but when I had joy it was the opposite: impossible to eat."
- When / where: 29 Jul 1897, infirmary
- People: Mère Agnès (recorder)
- Source: CJ 29.7.10–14, https://archives.carmeldelisieux.fr/archive/cj-juillet-1897/
- Source type: eyewitness record of her words (Mère Agnès)
- Rights: public-domain; transcription unknown
- Original words: « Je n'ai point de capacité pour jouir, j'ai toujours été comme cela ; mais j'en ai une très grande pour souffrir. » ; « Je suis lasse de la terre ! On fait des compliments quand on n'en mérite pas et des reproches quand on n'en mérite pas non plus. »
- Translation: "I have no capacity for enjoying, I have always been like that; but I have a very great one for suffering." ; "I am tired of the earth! They pay compliments when one does not deserve them, and reproaches when one does not deserve them either."
- New to English readers: partial ("capacity" appears in Novissima Verba by keyword; the appetite remark is not compared)
- Confidence: high
- Notes: The appetite remark is a body fact: pain made her hungry; joy took away her appetite. It links to the earlier card on LT 69 ("I always have to eat").

### F-OW-556 — 30 July 1897: the photographs of Bellière and Roulland, "I am nicer than they are", and "not Chinese: negroes!"
- Claim: On 30 July, looking at the photograph of "the Fathers Bellière and Roulland", she said: "I am nicer than they are!" When they promised to buy her "little Chinese", she said: "It is not Chinese I want, it is negroes!" (the notebook's word). Novissima Verba, on another day, has her asking that the money spent on a gift be used "to ransom some poor little Negroes from slavery".
